Model parameters¶

A scenario selects a model in a slot with { id, params }; the parameters a model accepts are listed on its card. This page is generated from the model registry.

Slots¶

attacker¶

attacker/jammer/constant¶

A constant wideband jammer: continuous OFDM-like noise in the channel, entering every receiver's noise term for as long as it is on.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
tx_power_dbm dBm 16.75 paper: Puñal, Aguiar and Gross, 'In VANEX There is No Such Thing as Jamming' (2012). The study's own receiver noise floor is −86 dBm; this crate keeps its own −98 dBm kTB-plus-noise-figure floor and does not mix the two.
channel - 180 standard: : single-channel operation on the ITS-G5A control channel is the default

Equations.

  • noise rise: N' = N + Σ_j P_j, with P_j the jammer's received power at this receiver in mW The same sum the aggregate-interference term goes through, ordered by node id.

Assumptions.

  • The jammer is on for the whole window it is asked about: it has no protocol and nothing to wait for.
  • Its received power at a victim is computed by the engine with the same link budget a frame gets, so obstacles and shadowing bound its reach the way says they do.

Limitations.

  • The measured anchors are blind areas, not powers: about 250 m in open space and 167 m at a dense urban crossroad, reproduced only with the study's own 17.58 dBm victim transmitter.

Ignores.

  • Subcarrier selectivity: a jammer here is wideband over the 10 MHz channel, so attacker/jammer/constant-pilot is not shipped (see the module docs).
  • The jammer's own antenna pattern and its own duty-cycle-dependent power amplifier behaviour: the declared power is constant while it is on.
  • GNSS jamming and spoofing, which are 's models and not this family's.

Sources.

  • paper: Puñal, Aguiar and Gross, 'In VANEX There is No Such Thing as Jamming' (2012). The study's own receiver noise floor is −86 dBm; this crate keeps its own −98 dBm kTB-plus-noise-figure floor and does not mix the two.
  • standard: (PHY jamming and flooding: constant, reactive, random duty cycle, modelled as an interferer in the SINR sums) and

attacker/jammer/pulsed¶

A duty-cycle jammer: wideband noise for a fraction of a fixed period, with a per-jammer phase drawn from the Attack stream.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium, high; hosting in-process; draws random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
tx_power_dbm dBm 16.75 paper: Puñal, Aguiar and Gross, 'In VANEX There is No Such Thing as Jamming' (2012). The study's own receiver noise floor is −86 dBm; this crate keeps its own −98 dBm kTB-plus-noise-figure floor and does not mix the two.
period_ns ns 100000000 todo-calibrate: names a 'random duty cycle' jammer but neither it nor prints a period; the default is one T_CBR so that the duty cycle is visible in the CBR a receiver reports (calibration pending)
duty - 0.5 [0.0, 1.0] todo-calibrate: no cited duty cycle exists for a V2X jammer; 0.5 is a design choice at the midpoint of the sweep (calibration pending)

Equations.

  • emission windows: on over [phase + k·T, phase + k·T + d·T) for every integer k, phase ~ U{0, …, T−1} ns The phase is drawn once per jammer from (Attack, Node) and cached, so the window list is a pure function of (seed, node, T, d).

Assumptions.

  • The period is a constant, not a random variable: the randomness is in the phase, which is what makes two jammers in one run independent without making one jammer's schedule depend on how often it was polled.

Limitations.

  • Neither the period nor the duty is cited (both todo-calibrate), so a number produced with this model rests on a design choice and the registry reports it.

Ignores.

  • Subcarrier selectivity: a jammer here is wideband over the 10 MHz channel, so attacker/jammer/constant-pilot is not shipped (see the module docs).
  • The jammer's own antenna pattern and its own duty-cycle-dependent power amplifier behaviour: the declared power is constant while it is on.
  • GNSS jamming and spoofing, which are 's models and not this family's.

Sources.

  • paper: Puñal, Aguiar and Gross, 'In VANEX There is No Such Thing as Jamming' (2012). The study's own receiver noise floor is −86 dBm; this crate keeps its own −98 dBm kTB-plus-noise-figure floor and does not mix the two.
  • standard: (PHY jamming and flooding: constant, reactive, random duty cycle, modelled as an interferer in the SINR sums) and

attacker/jammer/reactive¶

A reactive jammer: wideband noise only while energy above a trigger threshold is sensed, which is the profile whose losses are uncorrelated with SINR dips.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
tx_power_dbm dBm 16.75 paper: Puñal, Aguiar and Gross, 'In VANEX There is No Such Thing as Jamming' (2012). The study's own receiver noise floor is −86 dBm; this crate keeps its own −98 dBm kTB-plus-noise-figure floor and does not mix the two.
trigger_dbm dBm -75.0 paper: Puñal, Aguiar and Gross, 'In VANEX There is No Such Thing as Jamming' (2012). The study's own receiver noise floor is −86 dBm; this crate keeps its own −98 dBm kTB-plus-noise-figure floor and does not mix the two.
reaction_delay_ns ns 40000 todo-calibrate: the study prints the trigger threshold but no reaction latency; the default is the standard's own preamble plus SIGNAL field (32 µs + 8 µs), which is a lower bound on when detection can have happened, not a measurement (calibration pending)
tail_ns ns 0 paper: Puñal, Aguiar and Gross, 'In VANEX There is No Such Thing as Jamming' (2012). The study's own receiver noise floor is −86 dBm; this crate keeps its own −98 dBm kTB-plus-noise-figure floor and does not mix the two.

Equations.

  • emission windows: on over [s.from + δ, s.to + τ) for every sensed interval s with s.energy ≥ trigger; overlapping windows merge δ is the reaction delay and τ the hold-over tail; τ = 0 by default because the study describes emission only while energy is sensed.

Assumptions.

  • The engine supplies what the jammer heard (SensedInterval); this model does not compute the jammer's own link budget, for the same reason the PHY does not compute a hidden terminal's.
  • A sensed interval below the trigger produces no emission at all, so a reactive jammer in a quiet region is silent and costs nothing.

Limitations.

  • The cited anchors are a 170 m open-space blind area and delivery down to 0.6 in dense urban with the transmitter near the jammer; the study also reports low impact with reduced line of sight, which this model reproduces only through the engine's own obstacle stack.

Ignores.

  • Subcarrier selectivity: a jammer here is wideband over the 10 MHz channel, so attacker/jammer/constant-pilot is not shipped (see the module docs).
  • The jammer's own antenna pattern and its own duty-cycle-dependent power amplifier behaviour: the declared power is constant while it is on.
  • GNSS jamming and spoofing, which are 's models and not this family's.

Sources.

  • paper: Puñal, Aguiar and Gross, 'In VANEX There is No Such Thing as Jamming' (2012). The study's own receiver noise floor is −86 dBm; this crate keeps its own −98 dBm kTB-plus-noise-figure floor and does not mix the two.
  • standard: (PHY jamming and flooding: constant, reactive, random duty cycle, modelled as an interferer in the SINR sums) and

backhaul¶

backhaul/dsl¶

A VDSL2 telephone line: asymmetric rate and an interleaving delay of milliseconds.

version 1.0.0; tiers high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
rate_mbps Mbit/s 20.0 [0.1, 100000.0] datasheet: Openreach FTTC 80/20 tier: 20 Mbit/s upstream
down_mbps Mbit/s 80.0 [0.1, 100000.0] datasheet: Openreach FTTC 80/20 tier: 80 Mbit/s downstream
distance_km km 5.0 [0.0, 5000.0] todo-calibrate: a metro-scale route from the roadside to the backend (calibration pending)
hops - 7.0 [0.0, 64.0] todo-calibrate: router hops of a metro access network (calibration pending)
loss - 0.0 [0.0, 0.5] todo-calibrate: per-packet loss of the medium (calibration pending)
latency_ms ms 8.3245 [0.0, 2000.0] standard: derived: distance × propagation (ITU-T G.652 fibre 4.9 µs/km; c in air) + hops × per-hop delay

Equations.

  • FIFO per direction: arrival = max(offer, line free) + bytes·8 / rate(dir) + distance·us_per_km + hops·per_hop

Assumptions.

  • One FIFO per direction per roadside unit: the messages of every vehicle it relays queue behind each other on the line.
  • latency_ms, when given, replaces the distance and hop delay as the fixed one-way part.

backhaul/fixed¶

Fixed-latency roadside backhaul (abstract tier), configured as fibre: one-way latency plus an optional serialisation term.

version 0.1.0; tiers abstract; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
latency_ns ns 402000 paper: Coll-Perales 2022 Table VI, transport network with the MEC at the gNB: 0.402 ms mean, 0.422 ms at the 99.99th percentile takes fibre and microwave from these figures (0.4-2.4 ms mean)
bandwidth_bps bit/s null todo-calibrate: no published figure gives the capacity of a roadside unit's fibre or Ethernet drop (calibration pending)
degraded_multiplier - 10 todo-calibrate: specifies the degraded state as a 'backhaul latency multiplier' and gives no multiplier (calibration pending)

Equations.

  • one-way delay: d = latencymultiplier + bytes8/bandwidth, the serialisation term omitted when no capacity is modelled; the multiplier is 1 except in the degraded state

Assumptions.

  • One latency for the link, in both directions, with no queueing: the abstract tier. The measured transport-network chain (backhaul/measured-tn, the Coll-Perales M/M/1 model) is the medium and high tier and is not this model.

Limitations.

  • No queueing, no loss, no jitter and no availability model, so the 99.99th percentile a study of an undersized link needs is not reachable here — that is exactly what says backhaul/measured-tn is for.

Sources.

  • paper: Coll-Perales et al., 'End-to-end V2X latency modeling and analysis in 5G networks', IEEE TVT 2022, arXiv:2201.06082
  • paper: Narayanan et al., 'A first look at commercial 5G performance on smartphones', WWW'20

The floor is an operator setting, so no single value is the standard's (calibration pending)
mean_packet_bytes B 300.0 standard: TR 37.885 §6.1.5 Traffic Model 1: sizes {300, 190, 190, 190, 190} B

Equations.

  • M/M/1 sojourn time: W ~ Exp(µ − λ), µ = C / (8·mean_packet_bytes), λ from the node's sends in the last second; λ >= µ drops the packet An unstable queue has an infinite expected delay, which no finite run can schedule, so the packet is dropped and counted instead.
  • fixed latency chain: L_fixed = radio_UL+DL / 2 + transport_network + server The measured radio figure is uplink plus downlink, so one direction takes half of it.
  • Uu path loss: PL = 128.1 + 37.6·log10(R_km) TR 36.885 Table A.1.4-2.

Assumptions.

  • The cell layout is a square lattice at the inter-site distance, which is the only number the sources fix; a hexagonal layout would change which cell serves a point near a boundary but not the distance distribution's scale.
  • λ is the sending node's own offered rate, so the queue is per node as specifies; Jackson's theorem for multi-node transit is not composed here because the chain's fixed latency already carries the measured per-hop figures.

Ignores.

  • Handover interruption, radio-link failure, and per-packet loss correlation ( "Ignores", medium row).

Sources.

  • paper: Coll-Perales et al. 2022 Tables III, IV, VI-IX: radio UL+DL 2.00 ms (low LoA, low load) to 2.60-4.55 ms; transport network mean / 99.99th 0.402/0.422, 0.835/0.875, 2.355/2.396 ms by MEC placement; application server 0.0027-0.0031 ms at MEC, 0.035-3.295 ms centralized; requirement anchors 25 ms at 90 % (low LoA) and 10 ms at 99.99 % (high LoA)
  • paper: Narayanan et al., WWW'20 Table 2: LTE first-hop RTT 29.2 ± 4.8 ms, 5G mmWave 27.4 ± 6.4 ms, total RTT to an east/west-coast server 5G 54.0 ± 4.5 / 81.9 ± 5.5 ms and 4G 58.0 ± 4.3 / 88.9 ± 5.5 ms; packet-loss percentiles 0.01 / 0.1 / 1.2 %

  • Capacity, coverage geometry and handover ( "Ignores", abstract row).

cellular/uu/handover-outage¶

The capacity model plus handover interruption, per-packet loss at the measured percentiles, and outage beyond coverage.

version 1.0.0; tiers high; hosting in-process; draws random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
t_iu_ms ms 5.0 [0.0, 50.0] standard: TS 36.133 §5.1.2.1.2: T_IU is the uncertainty in acquiring the first available PRACH occasion in the new cell, up to 30 ms; 5 ms is its mean when the target cell offers one PRACH occasion per 10 ms radio frame (TS 36.211 §5.7.1, Table 5.7.1-2) and the handover command lands uniformly in the frame. Assumption: one PRACH occasion per frame, a common sparse configuration; a cell with two per frame halves it. T_sync of the E-UTRA-to-UTRA formula (§5.3.1.1.2) is folded into the same term.
t_search_ms ms 80.0 standard: TS 36.133 §5.1.2.1.2: unknown target adds T_search 80 ms; RRC procedure delay +50 ms
loss_probability - 0.0001 paper: Narayanan et al., WWW'20 Table 2: LTE first-hop RTT 29.2 ± 4.8 ms, 5G mmWave 27.4 ± 6.4 ms, total RTT to an east/west-coast server 5G 54.0 ± 4.5 / 81.9 ± 5.5 ms and 4G 58.0 ± 4.3 / 88.9 ± 5.5 ms; packet-loss percentiles 0.01 / 0.1 / 1.2 %

Equations.

  • handover interruption: known target: T_IU + 20 ms (+ 50 ms RRC procedure); unknown target: + T_search 80 ms; E-UTRA to UTRA: T_IU + T_sync + 50 + 10·F_max (known) or + 150 ms (unknown) TS 36.133 §5.1.2.1.2 and §5.3.1.1.2. A handover is seamless, not lossless (the ns-3 5G-LENA note the same section records), so a packet offered inside the interruption is lost.

Assumptions.

  • Qos::Reliable buys exactly one retry and the retry is not drawn again, so its effective loss is the single-shot probability squared.
  • A handover is detected from the serving cell changing under observe_position, so a scenario that never calls it sees no handovers.

Ignores.

  • Scheduler detail, HARQ on Uu, beam management; there is no LENA-level PHY here and ns-3 5G-LENA remains the external cross-check ( "Ignores", high row; ADR 0006).
  • Handover-failure recovery and gNB-side radio-link failure, which 5G-LENA does not implement either.

Sources.

  • standard: TS 36.133 §5.1.2.1.2, §5.3.1.1.2
  • paper: Narayanan et al., WWW'20 Table 2: LTE first-hop RTT 29.2 ± 4.8 ms, 5G mmWave 27.4 ± 6.4 ms, total RTT to an east/west-coast server 5G 54.0 ± 4.5 / 81.9 ± 5.5 ms and 4G 58.0 ± 4.3 / 88.9 ± 5.5 ms; packet-loss percentiles 0.01 / 0.1 / 1.2 %

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
lte_prbs PRB 100 standard: TS 36.101 Table 5.6-1: 100 PRB at 20 MHz
nr_prbs PRB 273 standard: TS 38.101-1 Table 5.3.2-1: 273 PRB at 100 MHz, 30 kHz SCS
overhead_dl - 0.14 standard: TS 38.306 §4.1.2: OH 0.14 DL, 0.08 UL in FR1
bler_first - 0.1 standard: TS 36.213 §7.2.3: CQI chosen for a transport-block error probability of 0.1
lte_harq_rtt_ms ms 8.0 standard: TS 36.213 §8, §10.1: FDD HARQ timing n+4 / n+8

bler_retx - 0.05 todo-calibrate: assumption: half the first transmission's 0.1, because chase combining adds the retransmission's energy to the first (about 3 dB at equal power, Chase, IEEE Trans. Commun. 1985), which on the steep turbo BLER curve near 10 % leaves well under half the errors; 0.05 is the conservative end (calibration pending)
rlc_retx_ms ms 20.0 code: ns-3 LTE module, LteRlcAm PollRetransmitTimer default 20 ms (t-PollRetransmit, TS 36.322 §7.3); 5G-LENA reuses the same RLC AM entity for NR
lte_ul_access_ms ms [10.0, 8.0] standard: TR 36.881 Table 5.1-1 (SR-based uplink access): SR periodicity 10 ms (mean wait 5 ms); SR transmission 1 ms + eNB SR decoding and grant generation 3 ms + grant transmission 1 ms + UE grant decoding and PUSCH encoding 3 ms (the n+4 timing of TS 36.213 §8.0) = 8 ms SR to data
lte_ho_interruption_ms ms 49.5 standard: TR 36.881 §5.2 LTE intra-frequency handover interruption analysis, 49.5 ms; inside the TS 36.133 §5.1.2.1 requirement T_interrupt = T_search + T_IU + 20 ms with T_IU up to 30 ms (≤ 50 ms to a known target cell)
nr_ho_interruption_ms ms 30.0 todo-calibrate: assumption: between the 49.5 ms LTE figure (TR 36.881 §5.2) and the 0 ms TR 38.913 §7.11 target that Rel-16 DAPS meets (TS 38.300 §9.2.3.2), because a Rel-15 break-before-make NR handover keeps LTE's steps with a shorter RACH (calibration pending)
shannon_fit - [0.75, 1.25] paper: Mogensen et al., "LTE Capacity Compared to the Shannon Bound", VTC 2007-Spring: link-level SISO AWGN fit BW_eff = 0.75, SNR_eff = 1.25 (the system overheads are applied separately, as here)
background_load - 0.5 todo-calibrate: assumption: a mid-loaded macro cell, because operator PRB utilisation is not published per city; TR 36.814 Annex A's full-buffer system evaluations bound it at 1 (calibration pending)

Equations.

  • proportional fair: serve UEs in order of r_i(t) / R_i(t); R_i ← (1 − 1/T)·R_i + (1/T)·served_i, T = 100 TTI
  • spectral efficiency: SE = clamp(0.75·log2(1 + SINR/1.25), CQI1, CQI_max); bits per PRB = SE·168·(1 − OH)
  • HARQ and RLC AM: first transmission fails with BLER 0.1, a retransmission with 0.05, every HARQ RTT, up to max_harq_tx; then RLC AM retransmits after rlc_retx_ms, up to max_rlc_retx; then the SDU is lost
  • one-way latency: UL: SR wait U[0, T_SR) + grant chain + scheduling + HARQ + core; DL: core + BS processing + scheduling + HARQ

Assumptions.

  • A delivery instant is the scheduler's answer with the backlog known at send time; a later arrival shares the following TTIs but does not move a delivery already reported.
  • Wideband scheduling: a UE's channel is flat across PRBs and constant between sends.
  • RLC AM bearers are lossless across a handover (TS 36.300 §10.1.2.3): packets wait out the interruption and are forwarded to the target cell.

Ignores.

  • Fast fading, frequency-selective scheduling, MIMO layers, beam management.
  • Radio-link failure and re-establishment.

Sources.

  • standard: TS 36.213, TS 36.101, TS 38.101-1, TS 38.306, TS 36.300
  • paper: Mogensen et al., VTC 2007-Spring
  • paper: Jalali, Padovani, Pankaj, VTC 2000 (proportional fair)

clock¶

clock/drift/none¶

The abstract tier's clock: node time equals GNSS time, drift-free. The baseline every drift figure is measured against, and what a scenario that is not studying time wants.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
drift 1 0.0 code: : clock/drift/none is drift-free by definition

Equations.

  • believed time: believed = true

Assumptions.

  • The node's clock is perfect, whatever its fix quality says.

Limitations.

  • A scenario that studies replay windows or generationTime plausibility must use clock/drift/tcxo-ocxo: this model cannot produce a stale or future timestamp at all.

Ignores.

  • Oscillator drift, holdover and time-transfer error.

clock/drift/tcxo-ocxo¶

A node's believed time: GNSS time while a satellite fix is valid, to within the committed 30 ns time-transfer error, and a free run at the oscillator's own fractional frequency error in holdover. The believed time feeds generationTime and the plausibility windows.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium, high; hosting in-process; draws random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
oscillator - tcxo datasheet: TXC automotive TCXO: ±0.5 to ±5.0 ppm over −40…+85 °C (G3) or +105 °C (G2). §3.8's default takes 2 ppm, the mid-range of the band; taking the mid-range is a design choice §3.8 records
fractional_error 1 2e-06 datasheet: TXC automotive TCXO: ±0.5 to ±5.0 ppm over −40…+85 °C (G3) or +105 °C (G2). §3.8's default takes 2 ppm, the mid-range of the band; taking the mid-range is a design choice §3.8 records
cited_band 1 {"ocxo": [1e-10, 5e-10], "tcxo": [5e-07, 5e-06]} datasheet: TXC automotive TCXO: ±0.5 to ±5.0 ppm over −40…+85 °C (G3) or +105 °C (G2). §3.8's default takes 2 ppm, the mid-range of the band; taking the mid-range is a design choice §3.8 records
pps_disciplined - true datasheet: 1PPS discipline is standard on commercial OBUs [Unex OBU-301E, Autotalks CRATON]
transfer_sigma ns 15.30640370773962 standard: GPS SPS PS 2020 Table 3.8-3: time transfer ≤ 30 ns at 95 %
ocxo_holdover_phase_error µs 1.5 datasheet: Rakon PPS-disciplined OCXO: ≤ 0.5 ppb (some ≤ 0.1 ppb), ≤ 1.5 µs phase error over 24-48 h holdover

Equations.

  • disciplined: believed = true + N(0, σ_transfer), σ_transfer = 30 ns / 1.96 while the fix is a satellite fix
  • holdover: offset(t) = offset(t_lost) + ε·(t − t_lost) ε is this unit's own fractional frequency error, drawn once and kept: a crystal runs consistently fast or slow, so the error grows as t and not as √t

Assumptions.

  • A unit's fractional frequency error is drawn once and kept, so holdover error grows linearly with the holdover interval.
  • The draws come from this model's own plug-in RNG domain, so they cannot depend on how often the GNSS model has drawn for the same node.
  • Only a satellite fix disciplines the clock: a node in dead reckoning is a node whose clock is free-running (FixQuality::is_satellite_fix).

Limitations.

  • No temperature model, no ageing, no Allan-variance structure: the drift is a constant fractional error, which is what the cited datasheets bound.

Ignores.

  • Short-term instability (phase noise, Allan deviation) and the temperature dependence the cited band brackets.

Sources.

  • datasheet: TXC automotive TCXO: ±0.5 to ±5.0 ppm over −40…+85 °C (G3) or +105 °C (G2). §3.8's default takes 2 ppm, the mid-range of the band; taking the mid-range is a design choice §3.8 records
  • datasheet: Rakon PPS-disciplined OCXO: ≤ 0.5 ppb (some ≤ 0.1 ppb), ≤ 1.5 µs phase error over 24-48 h holdover
  • standard: GPS SPS PS 2020 Table 3.8-3: time transfer ≤ 30 ns at 95 %

crypto-backend¶

crypto-backend/modeled¶

Models cryptography without executing it: a signature is a token derived from the signer's secret commitment and the message digest, verification compares tokens, and every size comes from the primitive descriptor tables. Verification outcomes and encoded sizes are identical to crypto-backend/real by construction (invariant I-S1).

version 3.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws random numbers.

Equations.

  • modelled secret commitment: commitment = SHA-256( commit-label ‖ seed ), recorded at keygen against the key's public material in a directory every backend instance shares
  • modelled signature token: token = SHA-256-CTR( sig-label ‖ commitment ‖ digest ), truncated to the descriptor's sig_bytes

Assumptions.

  • A token verifies exactly when it was produced by that key over that digest, which is also exactly when a real signature verifies — so the outcome sequence is identical (I-S1).
  • A modelled public key is 33 bytes beginning 0x02 or 0x03, the shape of a real compressed point, so every certificate and SPDU has the same encoded size as under the real backend.
  • Key seeds come from the same RNG domain, entity and draw count as the real backend, so switching modes does not shift any later random number in the run.
  • A token is keyed on the secret commitment, so producing one without the secret means inverting SHA-256. Forgery therefore fails for the same reason it fails in real mode, rather than because no API exposes it (v2.0.0).

Limitations.

  • No cryptographic strength is claimed beyond SHA-256 preimage resistance: a token is a hash image, not a signature, and a backend that holds the commitment can produce one for any digest. It models outcomes, not security.
  • Verification is answered from the commitment recorded when the key was generated, because computing it from public material alone would hand an attacker the same computation. The commitments therefore live in a directory shared by every backend instance in the process — the stand-in for the curve equation, which is likewise global and available to every relying party. What this models is a world in which a genuine public key is recognisable as one; what it does not model is a party that has to decide that from the key alone.
  • I-S1 is demonstrated end to end for ecdsa-p256-sha256 and ecqv-p256 only; the other catalogue primitives are exercised for supports/keygen agreement, not for a full signing trace, because the real backend implements no other family.
  • Falcon's variable signature length is modelled at its mean, because the descriptor's Variable { mean, max } has no distribution attached.

Ignores.

  • Everything cryptographic.

Sources.

  • standard: (crypto modes)
  • standard: (invariant I-S1)

crypto-backend/real¶

Executes real cryptography: ECDSA over NIST P-256 with RFC 6979 deterministic nonces, SHA-256, and SEC 4 ECQV implicit-certificate reconstruction. Refuses the post-quantum primitives, which have descriptors and cost tables but no implementation.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium, high; hosting in-process; draws random numbers.

Equations.

  • ECDSA signature: (r, s) over SHA-256(m) with k = HMAC-DRBG(d, m) per RFC 6979
  • ECQV reconstruction: Q_U = e·P_U + Q_CA, d_U = e·k_U + r (mod n), e = H(IC)

Assumptions.

  • Key seeds come from the scenario RNG (domain crypto, entity node), so real keys are reproducible across runs — a requirement of the determinism contract, not a weakening of the cryptography being simulated.
  • Signatures are RFC 6979 deterministic, so signing is a pure function.

Limitations.

  • Only primitive/ecdsa-p256-sha256 and primitive/ecqv-p256 are implemented; every other catalogue entry is refused with UnsupportedPrimitive.
  • A key seed is mapped onto 1..=n-1 by (seed mod (n-1)) + 1 rather than by rejection sampling, which keeps the draw count fixed at one 32-byte fill and leaves a negligible, documented bias.

Ignores.

  • Side channels, key storage, HSM command latency (the cost tables model the latency; this backend does not).

Sources.

  • standard: IEEE 1609.2 §5.3.1, §6.3.29
  • standard: RFC 6979
  • standard: SEC 4 §3.4-3.5
  • standard: FIPS 186-4
  • code: RustCrypto p256 0.13

dcc¶

dcc/etsi/adaptive-ts102687¶

The TS 102 687 adaptive approach: a smoothed CBR drives a clamped integral controller on δ, and the Annex B gatekeeper turns δ into the next gate-open time. The EN 302 571 floor is enforced on top.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
alpha - 0.016 [0.0, 1.0] standard: ETSI TS 102 687 V1.2.1 §5.2, §5.4 Table 3 and Annex B,
beta - 0.0012 [0.0, 1.0] standard: ETSI TS 102 687 V1.2.1 §5.2, §5.4 Table 3 and Annex B,
cbr_target - 0.68 [0.0, 1.0] standard: ETSI TS 102 687 V1.2.1 §5.2, §5.4 Table 3 and Annex B,
delta_max - 0.03 [0.0, 1.0] standard: ETSI TS 102 687 V1.2.1 §5.2, §5.4 Table 3 and Annex B,
delta_min - 0.0006 [0.0, 1.0] standard: ETSI TS 102 687 V1.2.1 §5.2, §5.4 Table 3 and Annex B,
g_max_plus - 0.0005 [0.0, 1.0] standard: ETSI TS 102 687 V1.2.1 §5.2, §5.4 Table 3 and Annex B,
g_max_minus - -0.00025 [-1.0, 0.0] standard: ETSI TS 102 687 V1.2.1 §5.2, §5.4 Table 3 and Annex B,
t_cbr_ms ms 100 standard: ETSI TS 102 687 V1.2.1 §5.2, §5.4 Table 3 and Annex B,
update_period_ms ms 200 standard: ETSI TS 102 687 V1.2.1 §5.2, §5.4 Table 3 and Annex B,
t_on_max_ms ms 4 standard: ETSI EN 302 571 V2.1.1 §4.2.10.2 Eq. 2-5 and TS 103 175 V1.1.1 §7.2 Eq. 1, REQ009/REQ022/REQ023. CTH 0.62 is a different tunable from the adaptive approach's 0.68 target; the two are independent.
duty_cycle_max - 0.03 [0.0, 1.0] standard: ETSI EN 302 571 V2.1.1 §4.2.10.2 Eq. 2-5 and TS 103 175 V1.1.1 §7.2 Eq. 1, REQ009/REQ022/REQ023. CTH 0.62 is a different tunable from the adaptive approach's 0.68 target; the two are independent.
t_off_min_ms ms 25 standard: ETSI EN 302 571 V2.1.1 §4.2.10.2 Eq. 2-5 and TS 103 175 V1.1.1 §7.2 Eq. 1, REQ009/REQ022/REQ023. CTH 0.62 is a different tunable from the adaptive approach's 0.68 target; the two are independent.
cth - 0.62 [0.0, 1.0] standard: ETSI EN 302 571 V2.1.1 §4.2.10.2 Eq. 2-5 and TS 103 175 V1.1.1 §7.2 Eq. 1, REQ009/REQ022/REQ023. CTH 0.62 is a different tunable from the adaptive approach's 0.68 target; the two are independent.
c_w - 1.0 [0.01, 1.0] standard: ETSI EN 302 571 V2.1.1 §4.2.10.2 Eq. 2-5 and TS 103 175 V1.1.1 §7.2 Eq. 1, REQ009/REQ022/REQ023. CTH 0.62 is a different tunable from the adaptive approach's 0.68 target; the two are independent.
t_off_limit_coefficient - 4000.0 standard: ETSI EN 302 571 V2.1.1 §4.2.10.2 and TS 103 175 V1.1.1 §7.2 Eq. 1: T_off = (1/C_w)·T_on·(4000·(CBR − CTH)/CBR − 1), CBR a plain fraction. Reproduces both of TS 103 175 Table 2's worked examples with C_w = 1 and T_on = 1 ms: 351.941 ms at CBR 0.68 against "about 351.9 ms" (0.012 %) and 692.333 ms at CBR 0.75 against "about 692.3 ms" (0.005 %).

Equations.

  • load smoothing: CBR_ITS-S = 0.5·CBR_ITS-S + 0.5·(CBR_L_0_Hop + CBR_L_0_Hop_prev)/2
  • delta update: δ_offset = min(β·(CBR_target − CBR_ITS-S), G_max+) if positive else max(β·(CBR_target − CBR_ITS-S), G_max−); δ = (1 − α)·δ + δ_offset; δ clamped to [δ_min, δ_max] Recomputed every 200 ms (2 × T_CBR).
  • gatekeeper: t_go = t_pg + min(max(T_on,pp/δ, 25 ms), 1 s)

Assumptions.

  • CBR is measured by the access layer per and handed in once per T_CBR.
  • T_on is known per queued packet from Phy::air_time.
  • δ starts at δ_max: an unloaded channel imposes nothing, and the loop reduces δ as the measured load rises.

Limitations.

  • No DCC_NET one-hop CBR sharing (TS 102 636-4-2) and no facilities-layer DCC_FAC.
  • The 200 ms update is measured from the first CBR report rather than from UTC mod 200 ms: the engine has no UTC that a plug-in may read.
  • EN 302 571's 0 < T_on <= 4 ms bound drops a frame outright rather than delaying it, so the largest admissible PSDU depends on the MCS: 3-bpsk-1/2 1482 B, 4.5-bpsk-3/4 2224 B, 6-qpsk-1/2 2304 B, 9-qpsk-3/4 2304 B, 12-16qam-1/2 2304 B, 18-16qam-3/4 2304 B, 24-64qam-2/3 2304 B, 27-64qam-3/4 2304 B. A signed CPM or a certificate-bearing CAM sent at the mandatory 3 Mbit/s rate is dropped by DCC before it reaches the PHY. Pairing a configured message size with an MCS is a scenario-validator check (the_admissible_frame_size_per_mcs_is_recorded).

Sources.

  • standard: ETSI TS 102 687 V1.2.1 §5.2, §5.4 Table 3 and Annex B,
  • standard: ETSI EN 302 571 V2.1.1 §4.2.10.2 Eq. 2-5 and TS 103 175 V1.1.1 §7.2 Eq. 1, REQ009/REQ022/REQ023. CTH 0.62 is a different tunable from the adaptive approach's 0.68 target; the two are independent.

dcc/etsi/reactive-ts102687¶

The TS 102 687 reactive approach: five states by channel load, each with a packet rate and an idle time, with the EN 302 571 floor on top.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
table - a1-ton-up-to-1ms ['a1-ton-up-to-1ms', 'a2-ton-up-to-500us'] standard: ETSI TS 102 687 V1.2.1 Annex A Tables A.1 and A.2 (informative). The named states belong to TS 102 687 Annex A, not to TS 103 175, which defines a continuous T_off bound and no states.

c_w - 1.0 [0.01, 1.0] standard: ETSI EN 302 571 V2.1.1 §4.2.10.2 Eq. 2-5 and TS 103 175 V1.1.1 §7.2 Eq. 1, REQ009/REQ022/REQ023. CTH 0.62 is a different tunable from the adaptive approach's 0.68 target; the two are independent.
t_off_limit_coefficient - 4000.0 standard: ETSI EN 302 571 V2.1.1 §4.2.10.2 and TS 103 175 V1.1.1 §7.2 Eq. 1: T_off = (1/C_w)·T_on·(4000·(CBR − CTH)/CBR − 1), CBR a plain fraction. Reproduces both of TS 103 175 Table 2's worked examples with C_w = 1 and T_on = 1 ms: 351.941 ms at CBR 0.68 against "about 351.9 ms" (0.012 %) and 692.333 ms at CBR 0.75 against "about 692.3 ms" (0.005 %).

Equations.

  • state table: Relaxed <30 %: 10 Hz, 100 ms; Active 1 30-39 %: 5 Hz, 200 ms; Active 2 40-49 %: 2.5 Hz, 400 ms; Active 3 50-60 %: 2 Hz, 500 ms; Restrictive >60 %: 1 Hz, 1,000 ms Table A.1 applies for T_on <= 1 ms, A.2 for T_on <= 500 µs.

Assumptions.

  • The state changes on every CBR report, with no hysteresis unless hold_ms is set.

Limitations.

  • Both tables are informative in the standard.
  • The state bands are read as percentages of the measured CBR, with the Active 3 upper bound inclusive.

Sources.

  • standard: ETSI TS 102 687 V1.2.1 Annex A Tables A.1 and A.2 (informative). The named states belong to TS 102 687 Annex A, not to TS 103 175, which defines a continuous T_off bound and no states.
  • standard: ETSI EN 302 571 V2.1.1 §4.2.10.2 Eq. 2-5 and TS 103 175 V1.1.1 §7.2 Eq. 1, REQ009/REQ022/REQ023. CTH 0.62 is a different tunable from the adaptive approach's 0.68 target; the two are independent.

dcc/sae/j2945-1-rate-power¶

SAE J2945/1 congestion control: the inter-transmission time from a smoothed neighbour count, the radiated power from the channel busy percentage through the SUPRA filter, and the two exceptions — a critical event and the tracking-error draw — that send a BSM early and at full power.

version 2.1.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

c_w - 1.0 [0.01, 1.0] standard: ETSI EN 302 571 V2.1.1 §4.2.10.2 Eq. 2-5 and TS 103 175 V1.1.1 §7.2 Eq. 1, REQ009/REQ022/REQ023. CTH 0.62 is a different tunable from the adaptive approach's 0.68 target; the two are independent.
t_off_limit_coefficient - 4000.0 standard: ETSI EN 302 571 V2.1.1 §4.2.10.2 and TS 103 175 V1.1.1 §7.2 Eq. 1: T_off = (1/C_w)·T_on·(4000·(CBR − CTH)/CBR − 1), CBR a plain fraction. Reproduces both of TS 103 175 Table 2's worked examples with C_w = 1 and T_on = 1 ms: 351.941 ms at CBR 0.68 against "about 351.9 ms" (0.012 %) and 692.333 ms at CBR 0.75 against "about 692.3 ms" (0.005 %).

Equations.

  • density smoothing: N_s(t) = λ·N(t) + (1 − λ)·N_s(t − 100 ms), N = vehicles within 100 m
  • rate control: MaxITT = 100 ms for N_s <= B, 100·N_s/B ms up to (vMaxITT/100)·B, vMaxITT above
  • power control: f(CBP) = vRPMax for CBP <= vMinCU, linear to vRPMin at vMaxCU, vRPMin above; per ordinary BSM RP = RP_prev + vSUPRAGain·(f(CBP) − RP_prev), the first at vRP; vRPMax for a critical or tracking-error BSM, which leaves RP_prev; TxPower = RP − MinSectorAntGain + CLoss
  • CBP smoothing: CBP(k) = w·RawCBP(k) + (1 − w)·CBP(k−1), w = vCBPWeightFactor 0.5, every 100 ms (SAE J2945/1 6.3.8.1 Eq. 2)
  • tracking error: TE = |p_local(t) − (p_b + v_b·(t − t_b)·(cos h_b, sin h_b))|, where (p_b, v_b, h_b, t_b) is the last BSM inferred received: a transmission is inferred received with probability 1 − Π, and after 3 successive negatives
  • tracking-error trigger: p(t) = 0 for TE < Tmin; 1 − exp(−α·(TE − Tmin)²) for Tmin <= TE < Tmax; 1 for TE >= Tmax; drawn every 100 ms
  • channel quality: Π(k) = min(0.3, w·Π(k−1) + (1 − w)·AVGPER(k)), AVGPER the mean PER of the RVs within 100 m over 5000 ms, every 1000 ms; w = 0 (SAE J2945/1 Eq. 4), or 0.9 under the FMVSS 150 NPRM preset

Assumptions.

  • MinSectorAntGain and CLoss are zero, as in both cited papers.
  • The neighbour count is the unit's own: the peers in its neighbour table within 100 m of its own position estimate.
  • The remote estimator coasts the last BSM inferred received in a straight line at its speed and heading (Ahmad 2019 Fig. 3); the local estimator is the unit's current position estimate.
  • Π is smoothed with the 0.9 weight on its previous value; the NPRM's equation is an image its text does not carry.

Limitations.

  • SAE J2945/1 itself is paywalled: every value is from the CAMP validation paper or the FMVSS 150 NPRM's transcription, and J2945/1's later revisions (2020, J2945/1B 2022) are not read.
  • λ: CAMP's J2945/1 table prints 0.05 and Rostami 2018 prints 0.5; the default is CAMP's, and J2945Params::ROSTAMI keeps the other.
  • ABS activation and traction loss are critical events in the standard; a unit here knows only its deceleration, so hard braking is the one it raises.

Sources.

  • paper: S. A. Ahmad, A. Hajisami, H. Krishnan, F. Ahmed-Zaid, E. Moradi-Pari (CAMP), "V2V System Congestion Control Validation and Performance", IEEE Trans. Veh.   • standard: NHTSA, Federal Motor Vehicle Safety Standards; V2V Communications, NPRM, 82 FR 3854 (12 January 2017), proposed 49 CFR 571.150 S5.3.4 and S5.5.1-S5.5.2, https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2017-01-12/html/2016-31059.htm. dcc/sae/j3161-1-rate¶

SAE J3161/1 congestion control for LTE-V2X: the BSM inter-transmit time from a smoothed count of the unique neighbours within 100 m, as J2945/1 sets it, with no power control; a critical event (hard braking) sends at 10 Hz while it lasts.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
lambda - 0.05 [0.0, 1.0] paper: A. Fouda, R. Berry, I. Vukovic, "Study of BSM Inter-Packet Gap Tails in C-V2X Networks", arXiv:2311.16904 (2023), §II-C Eq. 1-2 and Table II. The J3161/1 rate control as published: N_c the unique neighbours within r heard at least once in the previous 1000 ms, N_s = λ·N_c + (1 − λ)·N_s every 100 ms, I = 100 ms below B, 100·N_s/B up to Imax, Imax above; r, Imax, B, λ = 100 m, 600 ms, 25, 0.05. BSMs are generated at I and sent on the next SPS resource; a reserved resource with no new BSM goes unused.

tracking_error_trigger - false paper: A. Fouda, R. Berry, I. Vukovic, "Study of BSM Inter-Packet Gap Tails in C-V2X Networks", arXiv:2311.16904 (2023), §II-C Eq. 1-2 and Table II. The J3161/1 rate control as published: N_c the unique neighbours within r heard at least once in the previous 1000 ms, N_s = λ·N_c + (1 − λ)·N_s every 100 ms, I = 100 ms below B, 100·N_s/B up to Imax, Imax above; r, Imax, B, λ = 100 m, 600 ms, 25, 0.05. BSMs are generated at I and sent on the next SPS resource; a reserved resource with no new BSM goes unused.

Equations.

  • density smoothing: N_s(k) = λ·N_c + (1 − λ)·N_s(k − 1) every 100 ms, N_c = unique neighbours within r heard in the last 1000 ms
  • rate control: I(k) = 100 ms for N_s < B; 100·N_s/B ms for B <= N_s < (Imax/100)·B; Imax above

Assumptions.

  • The neighbour count is the unit's own: the peers in its neighbour table within 100 m of its own position estimate.
  • A critical event is J2945/1's: deceleration beyond 0.4 g (Ahmad 2019 §IV.A); the Qualcomm study sends critical BSMs at 10 Hz but does not restate the threshold.

Limitations.

  • SAE J3161/1 itself is paywalled: the rule and its constants are from Fouda 2023 and the Qualcomm 2020 study, both of which cite the standard.
  • No tracking-error transmission: neither source describes one for J3161/1. J2945Params::tracking_error_trigger turns J2945/1's on for a study.
  • The CR limits and the CBR measurement are the sidelink MAC's (radio.models.sidelink congestion_control), not this model's.

Sources.

  • paper: A. Fouda, R. Berry, I. Vukovic, "Study of BSM Inter-Packet Gap Tails in C-V2X Networks", arXiv:2311.16904 (2023), §II-C Eq. 1-2 and Table II. The J3161/1 rate control as published: N_c the unique neighbours within r heard at least once in the previous 1000 ms, N_s = λ·N_c + (1 − λ)·N_s every 100 ms, I = 100 ms below B, 100·N_s/B up to Imax, Imax above; r, Imax, B, λ = 100 m, 600 ms, 25, 0.05. BSMs are generated at I and sent on the next SPS resource; a reserved resource with no new BSM goes unused.
  • paper: Qualcomm Technologies, "C-V2X Congestion Control Study", 80-PE732-74 Rev. AA, 24 June 2020, §6-§8. Lab and field tests of J3161/1 congestion control on 50 C-V2X devices: rate control only; critical BSMs at 10 Hz for the event's duration; ITT backs off to 300 ms at twice and 600 ms at five times the 50-car load, and the CBR falls from 48 % and 87 % to about 24 %.
  • paper: S. A. Ahmad, A. Hajisami, H. Krishnan, F. Ahmed-Zaid, E. Moradi-Pari (CAMP), "V2V System Congestion Control Validation and Performance", IEEE Trans. Veh. Technol. 68(3), 2019, doi:10.1109/TVT.2019.2893042, §IV Eq. 5-10 and Table I ("J2945/1 Parameters"). Written by the consortium that drafted SAE J2945/1, validating production OBEs against it. detector¶

threat/detector/ts103759-observations¶

The ETSI TS 103 759 observation classes 1–5 with F2MD-style check semantics and the perception cross-check, normalised so that a score of ≈ 1 is the firing threshold. Runs alongside the legacy twelve, not instead of them.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

box_overlap_threshold - 0.5 paper: Kamel, Kaiser, Jemaa, Cincilla, Urien, 'Feasibility study of misbehavior detection mechanisms in cooperative intelligent transport systems', IEEE TVT 69(6), 2020 (F2MD check, aggregation and behavioural constants; F2MDParameters.h as read)
scoring - threshold ['threshold', 'catch'] paper: Kamel, Jemaa, Kaiser, Urien, 'CaTCh: a confidence range tolerant misbehavior detection approach', IEEE WCNC 2019

fusion - consecutive ['consecutive', 'windowed-mean', 'leaky-bucket'] paper: Kamel, Kaiser, Jemaa, Cincilla, Urien, 'Feasibility study of misbehavior detection mechanisms in cooperative intelligent transport systems', IEEE TVT 69(6), 2020 (F2MD check, aggregation and behavioural constants; F2MDParameters.h as read)
mean_history messages 3 paper: Kamel, Kaiser, Jemaa, Cincilla, Urien, 'Feasibility study of misbehavior detection mechanisms in cooperative intelligent transport systems', IEEE TVT 69(6), 2020 (F2MD check, aggregation and behavioural constants; F2MDParameters.h as read)
mean_window_s s 10.0 paper: Kamel, Kaiser, Jemaa, Cincilla, Urien, 'Feasibility study of misbehavior detection mechanisms in cooperative intelligent transport systems', IEEE TVT 69(6), 2020 (F2MD check, aggregation and behavioural constants; F2MDParameters.h as read)
bucket_coeff - 0.0005 paper: Kamel, Kaiser, Jemaa, Cincilla, Urien, 'Feasibility study of misbehavior detection mechanisms in cooperative intelligent transport systems', IEEE TVT 69(6), 2020 (F2MD check, aggregation and behavioural constants; F2MDParameters.h as read)
bucket_exponent - 10.0 paper: Kamel, Kaiser, Jemaa, Cincilla, Urien, 'Feasibility study of misbehavior detection mechanisms in cooperative intelligent transport systems', IEEE TVT 69(6), 2020 (F2MD check, aggregation and behavioural constants; F2MDParameters.h as read)
bucket_leak - 1.0 paper: Kamel, Kaiser, Jemaa, Cincilla, Urien, 'Feasibility study of misbehavior detection mechanisms in cooperative intelligent transport systems', IEEE TVT 69(6), 2020 (F2MD check, aggregation and behavioural constants; F2MDParameters.h as read)
bucket_threshold - 5.5066 paper: Kamel, Kaiser, Jemaa, Cincilla, Urien, 'Feasibility study of misbehavior detection mechanisms in cooperative intelligent transport systems', IEEE TVT 69(6), 2020 (F2MD check, aggregation and behavioural constants; F2MDParameters.h as read)
bucket_expiry_s s 10.0 code: (bucket removal after the 10 s window)

Equations.

  • range plausibility: |claim − self| / ART(environment)
  • acceptance range (ART): ART(env) = max over the tier-default LOS laws L of d solving P_tx + 2·G − PL_L(d) + 3·σ_L = S, with P_tx 23 dBm, G 3 dBi, S −92 dBm (ART_DERIVATION, recomputed from v2xw-radio by tests/ts103759.rs)
  • speed plausibility: |v_claimed| / max_plausible_speed
  • acceleration plausibility: |Δv/Δt| / (max_accel if Δv ≥ 0 else max_decel)
  • position consistency: Δs / (max_plausible_speed·Δt + Z·max(conf, floor))
  • position/speed consistency: |Δs − v̄·Δt| / (MAX_MGT_RNG + Z·max(conf, floor))
  • speed consistency: |Δv| / (max(MAX_MGT_RNG_UP, a_max)·Δt if Δv ≥ 0 else max(MAX_MGT_RNG_DOWN, b_max)·Δt), a_max and b_max from the vehicle classes
  • position/heading consistency: angle(heading_claimed, bearing(prev → now)) / MAX_HEADING_CHANGE, for Δt ≤ POS_HEADING_TIME and Δs > Z·max(conf, floor)
  • beacon frequency: (T_min − jitter_tolerance) / Δt_rx, from the message profile (BSM: (100 − 25) ms) — fires when the gap is shorter than the conforming minimum
  • interval windows: MAX_TIME_DELTA = 3.1·T_max, MAX_SA_TIME = 2.1·T_max, T_max the profile's longest conforming interval (F2MD's 3.1 s and 2.1 s at 1 Hz)
  • sudden appearance: on a first sighting after MAX_SA_TIME of listening and inside MAX_SA_RANGE: sudden_appearance_m / |claim − self|
  • map position plausibility: distance to nearest known road / MAX_DISTANCE_FROM_ROUTE
  • perception cross-check (class 4): for a claim inside the sensor's coverage: min(d(claim, nearest sensed object) / (Z·max(conf, floor)), hard_fail); Score = Σ NIS over the last n messages / χ²(4n, z_gate) (Wilson–Hilferty)
  • position/speed envelope (class 2 ): [d_min, d_max] reachable from v₀ to v₁ in Δt under a_env, b_env, v_max; / box_overlap_threshold
  • CaTCh scoring (scoring = catch): score = (1 − f)/(1 − θ), f the plausible share of the confidence disc or interval
  • windowed mean (fusion = windowed-mean): fires when mean(current score, ≤ mean_history earlier within mean_window_s) ≥ 1
  • leaky bucket (fusion = leaky-bucket): TMO ← max(0, TMO + coeff·e^(exponent·(1 − f_min)) − leak), f_min = min over checks of 1 − s·(1 − θ); the failing checks fire while TMO > bucket_threshold

Assumptions.

  • Every input is belief: the node's own position estimate, its own clock, its own map, its own sensors and the messages it verified (invariant I-T2).
  • A score of ≈ 1 is the firing threshold for every check, as in the legacy suite, so the two suites' fingerprints are comparable check by check.
  • A check whose input this receiver does not have is absent from the verdict, not scored zero, and the skip is counted.
  • The four verification states are handled separately: a deferred verification scores nothing at all.

Limitations.

  • The TS's DENM-specific observations (the 500 ms EEBL plausibility window, the detection-time consistency at 80 % of the minimum threshold, the same-event correlation distances of 1 km and 100 m) are not implemented here: they need the DENM event fields, which ObservedKind carries only as a cause code. The legacy suite's denmPlausibility check covers the speed contradiction.
  • The TS's event-trust threshold (ETR ≥ TrustThreshold) is left to the deployment, as the TS does records it as todo-calibrate.
  • Class 3 reads the map and the certificate region. Signal state is part of the class-3 LDM in the TS and does not reach a detector here: SPaT is encoded (v2xw_msg::j2735::spat) and broadcast (v2xw_node::rsu), but crate::obs carries no signal-state belief, so a false SPaT from a compromised road-side unit (crate::attack_rsu) cannot be cross-checked. Adding it means a LocalSignalState trait beside LocalEnvironment, on the same declared-capability footing.
  • The class-4 cross-check needs a perception model. With NoPerception it runs on nothing, and class4_skipped_no_perception is the count that says so; a run that reports a class-4 recall without reading that counter is reporting nothing.
  • The F2MD checks are re-implemented from the constant table and the published descriptions; no F2MD source was used. The scores are normalised on this crate's convention rather than F2MD's own, so a threshold matches but a score does not compare digit for digit with an F2MD run.
  • The acceptance range is the line-of-sight reach of the longest-reaching tier-default law. In urban and suburban areas that is TR 37.885's urban LOS law, whose 16.7 dB per decade puts the range near 42 km, so range plausibility is close to inert there: in a city the obstacle stack, not the range, ends a link. The range includes no fast-fading upside (a Nakagami up-fade at the high tier), and it is taken at 23 dBm; a fleet radiating the US 33 dBm EIRP ceiling reaches about 7 dB further and needs art_*_m raised.
  • The beacon-timing thresholds assume every sender in range uses the receiver's beacon_profile; a mixed BSM and CAM neighbourhood shares the 100 ms minimum but not the longest interval.
  • The per-message CPU cost is a placeholder, not a measurement. TVT 2020, WCNC 2019 and Bar-Shalom et al. 2001. F2MD was read for behaviour only and no code was copied. The Kalman check divides the broadcast position confidence by kalman_conf_divisor (2.4477, this engine's 95 % radius; F2MD uses the confidence as one σ), and the envelope tolerance uses it undivided. The box check needs the frame's vehicle size (FrameExtras) and is unchecked without it.
  • The suite is not yet selectable in detection.local, so its settings (including fusion and scoring) reach it only through Ts103759Params::apply / apply_label.

Sources.

  • standard: ETSI TS 103 759 V2.2.1 (2026-01), misbehaviour observation classes 1–5
  • code: (new detector families)
  • code: (detector/ts103759-observations, detector/f2md-checks)
  • code: (the perception model the class-4 check reads)
  • standard: ETSI TR 103 460 (misbehaviour-detection taxonomy: ART, eART, CoE, MPP, SAW)
  • standard: ETSI TS 103 324 (collective perception, the class-5 CPM check's subject)
  • paper: Kamel, Kaiser, Jemaa, Cincilla, Urien, 'Feasibility study of misbehavior detection mechanisms in cooperative intelligent transport systems', IEEE TVT 69(6), 2020 (F2MD check, aggregation and behavioural constants; F2MDParameters.h as read)
  • paper: Kamel, Jemaa, Kaiser, Urien, 'CaTCh: a confidence range tolerant misbehavior detection approach', IEEE WCNC 2019
  • paper: Bar-Shalom, Li, Kirubarajan, Estimation with Applications to Tracking and Navigation, Wiley 2001, §5.4.2 (NIS test) and §6.2.2 (white-noise acceleration); Wilson and Hilferty, PNAS 17, 1931 (χ² quantile)

emission¶

energy/emission/hbefa3¶

Tailpipe CO2, CO, HC, NOx, PM and fuel of a combustion-engined vehicle from its speed, acceleration and the road grade, by the polynomial fit of the HBEFA 3.1 emission factors (Krajzewicz et al. 2015) - the model SUMO ships as HBEFA3. 45 classes: passenger cars and light commercial vehicles, petrol and diesel, Euro 0 to 6; heavy trucks, urban buses and coaches.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
coefficients mg/s per term 45 classes x 6 quantities x 6 terms (table) paper: Krajzewicz D., Behrisch M., Wagner P., Luz R., Krumnow M. (2015) Second Generation of Pollutant Emission Models for SUMO. In: Behrisch M., Weber M. (eds) Modeling Mobility with Open Data, Lecture Notes in Mobility, Springer. the HBEFA 3.1 polynomial fits and their per-class coefficients

Equations.

  • effective acceleration: a' = a + g sin(atan(grade)) g = 9.80665 m/s2; a is the interval acceleration (crate::state)
  • emission rate: E = max(0, c0 + c1 a' v + c2 a'^2 v + c3 v + c4 v^2 + c5 v^3) / 3.6 [mg/s] v in m/s; one coefficient row per class and quantity; the fuel row is fuel mass

Assumptions.

  • a vehicle's class is its HBEFA class; the fleet section of the scenario chooses segment, fuel and Euro standard per vehicle
  • hot running emissions: the engine and after-treatment are at operating temperature

Limitations.

  • a polynomial fit: outside the speeds and accelerations of the HBEFA driving situations it was fitted to it extrapolates
  • no coasting fuel cut-off beyond the clamp at zero; SUMO's coasting rule is not reproduced (see the module documentation)
  • no two-wheeler classes; a motorcycle is unmodelled under this model
  • the intercept is the standstill rate, and it is high: PC_G_EU4 idles at 2.6 g/s CO2 and 0.84 g/s fuel (about 4 L/h), some four times a warm petrol car's measured idle (PHEMlight's PC_G_EU4 idle row: 0.22 g/s). Traffic that queues a lot reads high - on the EPA UDDS, a fifth of it stationary, 12 L/100 km against PHEMlight's 9 - and a V2X application that removes idling reads a larger saving under this model than under PHEMlight. SUMO's HBEFA3 behaves the same; compare energy/emission/phemlight for passenger cars

Ignores.

  • cold-start excess emissions
  • evaporative emissions
  • non-exhaust PM (brake, tyre and road wear)
  • ambient temperature and air-conditioning load
  • deterioration with mileage

Sources.

  • paper: Krajzewicz D., Behrisch M., Wagner P., Luz R., Krumnow M. (2015) Second Generation of Pollutant Emission Models for SUMO. In: Behrisch M., Weber M. (eds) Modeling Mobility with Open Data, Lecture Notes in Mobility, Springer. the HBEFA 3.1 polynomial fits and their per-class coefficients
  • code: Eclipse SUMO, src/utils/emissions/HelpersHBEFA3.cpp (myFunctionParameter[45][6][6]), https://github.com/eclipse-sumo/sumo, EPL-2.0 OR GPL-2.0-or-later. coefficient table transcribed by script, 45 classes x 6 pollutants x 6 coefficients; numeric data only, no code reused
  • dataset: INFRAS (2010) HBEFA - Handbook Emission Factors for Road Transport, version 3.1. the emission factors the polynomials were fitted to; not redistributed here

energy/emission/moves-opmode¶

Tailpipe CO2, CO, HC, NOx, PM2.5 and fuel of a light-duty vehicle by the US EPA MOVES method: each interval is binned into one of 23 running operating modes by speed and scaled tractive power, and charged that mode's rate. Default rates: MOVESTAR's light-duty vehicle and light-duty truck tables, distilled from MOVES.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
passenger_car.A kW s/m 0.156461 standard: US EPA, MOVES (Motor Vehicle Emission Simulator) technical documentation: running-exhaust operating modes binned by speed and scaled tractive power (STP, kW/tonne) from the source type's road-load coefficients A, B, C, mass m and fixed mass factor f. operating-mode definitions 0, 1, 11-16, 21-30, 33-40 and the scaled tractive power
passenger_car.B kW s2/m2 0.002002 standard: US EPA, MOVES (Motor Vehicle Emission Simulator) technical documentation: running-exhaust operating modes binned by speed and scaled tractive power (STP, kW/tonne) from the source type's road-load coefficients A, B, C, mass m and fixed mass factor f. operating-mode definitions 0, 1, 11-16, 21-30, 33-40 and the scaled tractive power
passenger_car.C kW s3/m3 0.000493 standard: US EPA, MOVES (Motor Vehicle Emission Simulator) technical documentation: running-exhaust operating modes binned by speed and scaled tractive power (STP, kW/tonne) from the source type's road-load coefficients A, B, C, mass m and fixed mass factor f. operating-mode definitions 0, 1, 11-16, 21-30, 33-40 and the scaled tractive power
passenger_car.m t 1.4788 standard: US EPA, MOVES (Motor Vehicle Emission Simulator) technical documentation: running-exhaust operating modes binned by speed and scaled tractive power (STP, kW/tonne) from the source type's road-load coefficients A, B, C, mass m and fixed mass factor f. operating-mode definitions 0, 1, 11-16, 21-30, 33-40 and the scaled tractive power
passenger_car.f t 1.4788 standard: US EPA, MOVES (Motor Vehicle Emission Simulator) technical documentation: running-exhaust operating modes binned by speed and scaled tractive power (STP, kW/tonne) from the source type's road-load coefficients A, B, C, mass m and fixed mass factor f. operating-mode definitions 0, 1, 11-16, 21-30, 33-40 and the scaled tractive power
passenger_truck.A kW s/m 0.22112 standard: US EPA, MOVES (Motor Vehicle Emission Simulator) technical documentation: running-exhaust operating modes binned by speed and scaled tractive power (STP, kW/tonne) from the source type's road-load coefficients A, B, C, mass m and fixed mass factor f. operating-mode definitions 0, 1, 11-16, 21-30, 33-40 and the scaled tractive power
passenger_truck.B kW s2/m2 0.002838 standard: US EPA, MOVES (Motor Vehicle Emission Simulator) technical documentation: running-exhaust operating modes binned by speed and scaled tractive power (STP, kW/tonne) from the source type's road-load coefficients A, B, C, mass m and fixed mass factor f. operating-mode definitions 0, 1, 11-16, 21-30, 33-40 and the scaled tractive power
passenger_truck.C kW s3/m3 0.000698 standard: US EPA, MOVES (Motor Vehicle Emission Simulator) technical documentation: running-exhaust operating modes binned by speed and scaled tractive power (STP, kW/tonne) from the source type's road-load coefficients A, B, C, mass m and fixed mass factor f. operating-mode definitions 0, 1, 11-16, 21-30, 33-40 and the scaled tractive power
passenger_truck.m t 1.86686 standard: US EPA, MOVES (Motor Vehicle Emission Simulator) technical documentation: running-exhaust operating modes binned by speed and scaled tractive power (STP, kW/tonne) from the source type's road-load coefficients A, B, C, mass m and fixed mass factor f. operating-mode definitions 0, 1, 11-16, 21-30, 33-40 and the scaled tractive power
passenger_truck.f t 1.86686 standard: US EPA, MOVES (Motor Vehicle Emission Simulator) technical documentation: running-exhaust operating modes binned by speed and scaled tractive power (STP, kW/tonne) from the source type's road-load coefficients A, B, C, mass m and fixed mass factor f. operating-mode definitions 0, 1, 11-16, 21-30, 33-40 and the scaled tractive power
rates g/h per mode 2 tables x 23 modes x 7 quantities (MOVESTAR) paper: Wang Z., Wu G., Scora G. (2020) MOVESTAR: An Open-Source Vehicle Fuel and Emission Model based on USEPA MOVES. arXiv:2008.04986; tables from https://github.com/ziranw/MOVESTAR-Fuel-and-Emission-Model (MOVESTAR_Python_v1.4/ EmsRate_1.csv, EmsRate_2.csv, VehicleSrcCoeff.csv). per-operating-mode rates for a light-duty vehicle and a light-duty truck, derived from MOVES (US federal work, public domain); the repository carries no licence file

Equations.

  • scaled tractive power: STP = (A v + B v^2 + C v^3 + m v (a + g sin theta)) / f [kW/tonne] v in m/s; A, B, C, m, f of the MOVES source type
  • operating mode: 0 braking (a <= -2 mph/s, or a < -1 mph/s for 3 s); 1 idle (v < 1 mph); 11-16, 21-30, 33-40 by speed band and STP bin
  • rate: E = rate[mode] / 3600 [g/s]; fuel = CO2 (12/44)(13.78/12) PM2.5 = elemental + organic

Assumptions.

  • the braking rule's three consecutive seconds are read as three seconds of continuous deceleration beyond 1 mph/s, which is the same thing at the 1 Hz MOVES assumes and well-defined at the engine's finer step
  • the rates are MOVESTAR's fleet averages; the fleet section's Euro class and fuel do not change them

Limitations.

  • light-duty only with the default tables; heavy-duty, bus, coach and motorcycle are unmodelled
  • the operating-mode rates are of one MOVES run (one calendar year, one fleet age distribution); a study for a given year and county should run MOVES on the operating-mode distribution instead

Ignores.

  • start and extended-idle emissions (MOVES start and hotelling processes)
  • evaporative, brake- and tyre-wear processes
  • temperature, humidity and fuel-formulation adjustments

Sources.

  • standard: US EPA, MOVES (Motor Vehicle Emission Simulator) technical documentation: running-exhaust operating modes binned by speed and scaled tractive power (STP, kW/tonne) from the source type's road-load coefficients A, B, C, mass m and fixed mass factor f. operating-mode definitions 0, 1, 11-16, 21-30, 33-40 and the scaled tractive power
  • paper: Wang Z., Wu G., Scora G. (2020) MOVESTAR: An Open-Source Vehicle Fuel and Emission Model based on USEPA MOVES. arXiv:2008.04986; tables from https://github.com/ziranw/MOVESTAR-Fuel-and-Emission-Model (MOVESTAR_Python_v1.4/ EmsRate_1.csv, EmsRate_2.csv, VehicleSrcCoeff.csv). per-operating-mode rates for a light-duty vehicle and a light-duty truck, derived from MOVES (US federal work, public domain); the repository carries no licence file

energy/emission/phemlight¶

Tailpipe CO2, CO, HC, NOx, PM and fuel of a passenger car from the engine power its road-load and inertia demand, read off measured emission curves against normalised power (PHEMlight, TU Graz). Fuel is cut off when the car decelerates harder than it would coast in gear. The two openly published vehicles: a Euro 4 petrol and a Euro 4 diesel passenger car.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
gravity m/s2 9.81 dataset: Eclipse SUMO, data/emissions/PHEMlight/PC_G_EU4.{PHEMLight.veh,csv,_FC.csv} and PC_D_EU4.*, with src/foreign/PHEMlight/cpp/CEP.cpp and Constants.cpp, https://github.com/eclipse-sumo/sumo (TU Graz PHEMlight data, EPL-2.0). vehicle, road-load, gear, drag, fuel and pollutant tables transcribed; the CEP algorithm re-implemented from its published description
air_density kg/m3 1.182 dataset: Eclipse SUMO, data/emissions/PHEMlight/PC_G_EU4.{PHEMLight.veh,csv,_FC.csv} and PC_D_EU4.*, with src/foreign/PHEMlight/cpp/CEP.cpp and Constants.cpp, https://github.com/eclipse-sumo/sumo (TU Graz PHEMlight data, EPL-2.0). vehicle, road-load, gear, drag, fuel and pollutant tables transcribed; the CEP algorithm re-implemented from its published description
normalising_speed m/s 19.444 dataset: Eclipse SUMO, data/emissions/PHEMlight/PC_G_EU4.{PHEMLight.veh,csv,_FC.csv} and PC_D_EU4.*, with src/foreign/PHEMlight/cpp/CEP.cpp and Constants.cpp, https://github.com/eclipse-sumo/sumo (TU Graz PHEMlight data, EPL-2.0). vehicle, road-load, gear, drag, fuel and pollutant tables transcribed; the CEP algorithm re-implemented from its published description
normalising_acceleration m/s2 0.45 dataset: Eclipse SUMO, data/emissions/PHEMlight/PC_G_EU4.{PHEMLight.veh,csv,_FC.csv} and PC_D_EU4.*, with src/foreign/PHEMlight/cpp/CEP.cpp and Constants.cpp, https://github.com/eclipse-sumo/sumo (TU Graz PHEMlight data, EPL-2.0). vehicle, road-load, gear, drag, fuel and pollutant tables transcribed; the CEP algorithm re-implemented from its published description
drivetrain_efficiency - 0.9 dataset: Eclipse SUMO, data/emissions/PHEMlight/PC_G_EU4.{PHEMLight.veh,csv,_FC.csv} and PC_D_EU4.*, with src/foreign/PHEMlight/cpp/CEP.cpp and Constants.cpp, https://github.com/eclipse-sumo/sumo (TU Graz PHEMlight data, EPL-2.0). vehicle, road-load, gear, drag, fuel and pollutant tables transcribed; the CEP algorithm re-implemented from its published description
zero_speed m/s 0.5 dataset: Eclipse SUMO, data/emissions/PHEMlight/PC_G_EU4.{PHEMLight.veh,csv,_FC.csv} and PC_D_EU4.*, with src/foreign/PHEMlight/cpp/CEP.cpp and Constants.cpp, https://github.com/eclipse-sumo/sumo (TU Graz PHEMlight data, EPL-2.0). vehicle, road-load, gear, drag, fuel and pollutant tables transcribed; the CEP algorithm re-implemented from its published description

Equations.

  • engine power: P = [ (m+L) g (f0 + f1 v + f4 v^4) v + rho/2 cd A v^3 + (m k_rot(v) + m_rot + L) a v + (m+L) g (grade%/100) v ] / 1000 / eta + P_aux kW; k_rot(v) interpolated from the vehicle's speed/gear table
  • fuel: FC = FC_curve(P / P_rated) * P_rated [g/h] idle row at |v| <= 0.5 m/s
  • pollutant: E_X = E_X_curve(P / P_drive) [g/h], P_drive = P(19.444 m/s, 0.45 m/s2, 0) light-vehicle normalisation; idle row at |v| <= 0.5 m/s
  • CO2 by carbon balance: CO2 = (FC f_C - CO 0.429 - HC 0.866) / 0.273 f_C = 0.865 petrol, 0.863 diesel
  • coasting cut-off: a < a_coast(v, grade) => every rate 0 a_coast from engine drag in gear, rolling, air and grade resistance (GetDecelCoast)

Assumptions.

  • hot running emissions
  • a Euro 0-3, 5 or 6 passenger car is computed with the Euro 4 vehicle of its fuel; the resolved class name is reported

Limitations.

  • two published vehicles only; light commercial, heavy-duty, bus, coach and motorcycle are unmodelled under this model (use energy/emission/hbefa3)

Ignores.

  • cold start
  • the PN and NO columns of the curves
  • non-exhaust PM
  • deterioration and NOx corrections (Deterioration.det, NOxCor.tno of PHEMlight5)

Sources.

  • paper: Hausberger S., Rexeis M., Zallinger M., Luz R. (2009) Emission Factors from the Model PHEM for the HBEFA Version 3. Report Nr. I-20/2009 Haus-Em 33/08/679, Institute for Internal Combustion Engines and Thermodynamics, TU Graz. PHEM's power-based engine-map method, of which PHEMlight is the normalised-power simplification
  • dataset: Eclipse SUMO, data/emissions/PHEMlight/PC_G_EU4.{PHEMLight.veh,csv,_FC.csv} and PC_D_EU4.*, with src/foreign/PHEMlight/cpp/CEP.cpp and Constants.cpp, https://github.com/eclipse-sumo/sumo (TU Graz PHEMlight data, EPL-2.0). vehicle, road-load, gear, drag, fuel and pollutant tables transcribed; the CEP algorithm re-implemented from its published description
  • paper: Krajzewicz D. et al. (2015) Second Generation of Pollutant Emission Models for SUMO. describes PHEMlight's integration into SUMO and its relation to HBEFA 3

energy¶

energy/ev/kurczveil¶

Traction-battery energy of a battery-electric vehicle by the energy balance of SUMO's battery device (Kurczveil et al. 2014): kinetic, potential and rotational energy change plus air, rolling and curve losses, divided by the propulsion efficiency when positive and multiplied by the recuperation efficiency when negative, plus constant consumers. SUMO's default vehicle.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

propulsion_efficiency - 0.98 [0.0, 1.0] code: Eclipse SUMO, src/utils/emissions/HelpersEnergy.{h,cpp} (myDefault* constants) and https://sumo.dlr.de/docs/Models/Electric.html. default vehicle: 1830 kg, 2.6 m2, cd 0.35, rotating mass 40 kg, roll 0.01, radial 0.1, 100 W constant, 0.98 propulsion, 0.96 recuperation, 35 kWh
recuperation_efficiency - 0.96 [0.0, 1.0] code: Eclipse SUMO, src/utils/emissions/HelpersEnergy.{h,cpp} (myDefault* constants) and https://sumo.dlr.de/docs/Models/Electric.html. default vehicle: 1830 kg, 2.6 m2, cd 0.35, rotating mass 40 kg, roll 0.01, radial 0.1, 100 W constant, 0.98 propulsion, 0.96 recuperation, 35 kWh
recuperation_decel_alpha m/s2 0.0 code: Eclipse SUMO, src/utils/emissions/HelpersEnergy.{h,cpp} (myDefault* constants) and https://sumo.dlr.de/docs/Models/Electric.html. default vehicle: 1830 kg, 2.6 m2, cd 0.35, rotating mass 40 kg, roll 0.01, radial 0.1, 100 W constant, 0.98 propulsion, 0.96 recuperation, 35 kWh
air_density kg/m3 1.2041 code: Eclipse SUMO, src/utils/emissions/HelpersEnergy.cpp. dry air at 20 C, as the code comment states
capacity_wh Wh 35000.0 [1.0, 1000000.0] code: Eclipse SUMO, src/utils/emissions/HelpersEnergy.{h,cpp} (myDefault* constants) and https://sumo.dlr.de/docs/Models/Electric.html. default vehicle: 1830 kg, 2.6 m2, cd 0.35, rotating mass 40 kg, roll 0.01, radial 0.1, 100 W constant, 0.98 propulsion, 0.96 recuperation, 35 kWh

Equations.

  • energy balance: dE = 1/2 (m + m_rot)(v1^2 - v0^2) + m g sin(theta) s + 1/2 rho A cd v^2 s + c_r m g s + c_rad m v^2 / r dt s distance, r = s/|dheading| clamped to [1e-4, 1e4] m, v the interval's mean speed
  • efficiencies: dE > 0: E_bat = dE / eta_prop; dE <= 0: E_bat = dE eta_recup exp(-alpha/|a|) alpha = 0 by default (SUMO's recuperationEfficiencyByDeceleration); alpha > 0 applies Fiori et al. 2016's deceleration-dependent regeneration
  • battery power: P = E_bat / dt + P_const W

Assumptions.

  • the interval's kinetic-energy change is exact (crate::state); SUMO reconstructs the previous speed as v - a dt, which is the same thing for its Euler update

Limitations.

  • constant efficiencies: no motor efficiency map, no dependence on torque or speed
  • SUMO's default vehicle is a generic car; set the parameters for a specific vehicle

Ignores.

  • battery internal resistance and temperature
  • HVAC load beyond the constant consumers
  • charging (no charging stations in this engine yet)

Sources.

  • paper: Kurczveil T., López P.A., Schnieder E. (2014) Implementation of an Energy Model and a Charging Infrastructure in SUMO. In: Behrisch M., Krajzewicz D., Weber M. (eds) Simulation of Urban Mobility (SUMO 2013), LNCS 8594, Springer. energy balance over one step: kinetic, potential, rotational, air, rolling and curve losses, constant consumers, propulsion and recuperation efficiencies
  • code: Eclipse SUMO, src/utils/emissions/HelpersEnergy.{h,cpp} (myDefault* constants) and https://sumo.dlr.de/docs/Models/Electric.html. default vehicle: 1830 kg, 2.6 m2, cd 0.35, rotating mass 40 kg, roll 0.01, radial 0.1, 100 W constant, 0.98 propulsion, 0.96 recuperation, 35 kWh

energy/ev/vt-cpem¶

Traction-battery power of a battery-electric vehicle by VT-CPEM (Fiori, Ahn & Rakha 2016): wheel power from inertia, rolling, aerodynamic and grade resistance (Rakha's resistance terms), through driveline, motor and battery efficiencies, with a regenerative-braking efficiency exp(-alpha/|a|) that recovers more the harder the vehicle brakes. Parameterised for a 2011-2013 Nissan Leaf.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

capacity_wh Wh 24000.0 [1.0, 1000000.0] datasheet: Nissan Leaf (2011-2015, ZE0/AZE0) 24 kWh lithium-ion traction battery, manufacturer specification. nominal capacity

Equations.

  • wheel power: P_w = (m a + m g cos(theta) Cr/1000 (c1 v_kmh + c2) + 1/2 rho A_f C_D v^2 + m g sin(theta)) v rolling term zero at standstill
  • motor power: P_w >= 0: P_m = P_w / (eta_D eta_EM); P_w < 0: P_m = P_w eta_D eta_EM eta_rb, eta_rb = exp(-alpha/|a|) for a < 0, 1 otherwise
  • battery power: P_b = P_m / eta_bat (P_m >= 0) or P_m eta_bat (P_m < 0), + P_aux

Assumptions.

  • a negative wheel power without deceleration (a constant-speed descent) regenerates at the driveline and motor efficiency with eta_rb = 1 - this build's reading where the paper's eta_rb is stated for braking

Limitations.

  • constant efficiencies apart from the regeneration term
  • parameterised for one vehicle; a bus or van needs its own parameters

Ignores.

  • battery temperature and ageing
  • HVAC load beyond the constant auxiliary power

Sources.

  • paper: Fiori C., Ahn K., Rakha H.A. (2016) Power-based electric vehicle energy consumption model: Model development and validation. Applied Energy 168:257-268. doi:10.1016/j.apenergy.2016.01.097. VT-CPEM: wheel power from the Rakha vehicle-dynamics resistance terms, driveline and motor efficiencies, regenerative-braking efficiency exp(-alpha/|a|); parameterised for a 2011-2013 Nissan Leaf
  • paper: Rakha H., Lucic I., Demarchi S.H., Setti J.R., Van Aerde M. (2001) Vehicle Dynamics Model for Predicting Maximum Truck Acceleration Levels. Journal of Transportation Engineering 127(5). rolling resistance R = 9.8066 m Cr/1000 (c1 v + c2), v in km/h; Cr = 1.75 (average pavement), c1 = 0.0328, c2 = 4.575 (radial tyres)

envelope¶

envelope/etsi-ts103097¶

The ETSI TS 103 097 profile of IEEE 1609.2 SignedData: the same structure with the profile's constraints enforced at signing time — generationTime mandatory, p2pcdLearningRequest forbidden, and a DENM carrying generationLocation and signing with a certificate.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Equations.

  • signed hash: H( H(tbsData) ‖ H(signer identifier input) ), SHA-256 [IEEE 1609.2 §5.3.1]
  • HashedId8: the low-order 8 bytes of SHA-256 over the canonical COER encoding [IEEE 1609.2a-2017 §6.3.25]

Assumptions.

  • The signer identifier input is the signer's certificate even when the wire carries only its digest, so attaching a digest changes the SPDU's size and nothing else.
  • rSig uses the x-only alternative, as 1609.2 §6.3.29 specifies for a generated ECDSA signature. All three 32-byte alternatives encode to 33 bytes, so the choice affects conformance and not size.
  • tbsData is re-encoded before hashing rather than sliced out of the received bytes, so a signature is always checked against the canonical encoding.

Limitations.

  • Only ECDSA over P-256 can be carried: IEEE 1609.2's Signature CHOICE has no post-quantum alternative and no extension defining one is standardised, so a post-quantum primitive is refused rather than mis-encoded.
  • Encrypted SPDUs, countersignatures and certificate requests are out of scope; only signedData is produced and parsed.
  • verify_plan checks revocation, the validity period, the SPDU PSID and the non-cryptographic 1609.2 chain rules (chain::check_links) along the chain up to a trust anchor or a verified cache entry, and plans one link operation per unverified certificate; it does not itself verify anything, which is the NodeRuntime's job (chain::validate is the cryptographic validator).

Ignores.

  • Region inheritance beyond the chain segment the receiver holds; geometric region containment (fails closed).

Sources.

  • standard: IEEE 1609.2 §5.3.1, §6.3.4, §6.3.29
  • standard: IEEE 1609.2a-2017 §6.3.25-6.3.26, §8.1-8.2.4
  • standard: ETSI TS 103 097 V2.1.1 §4.1, §5.1-5.2, §7.1
  • standard: (envelope overhead)

envelope/ieee-1609-2¶

IEEE 1609.2 SignedData: real COER encoding through the generated ASN.1 bindings, both signer identifiers, and a verification plan a node runtime can cost and queue.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

  • signed hash: H( H(tbsData) ‖ H(signer identifier input) ), SHA-256 [IEEE 1609.2 §5.3.1]
  • HashedId8: the low-order 8 bytes of SHA-256 over the canonical COER encoding [IEEE 1609.2a-2017 §6.3.25]

  • The signer identifier input is the signer's certificate even when the wire carries only its digest, so attaching a digest changes the SPDU's size and nothing else.
  • rSig uses the x-only alternative, as 1609.2 §6.3.29 specifies for a generated ECDSA signature. All three 32-byte alternatives encode to 33 bytes, so the choice affects conformance and not size.

  • Only ECDSA over P-256 can be carried: IEEE 1609.2's Signature CHOICE has no post-quantum alternative and no extension defining one is standardised, so a post-quantum primitive is refused rather than mis-encoded.
  • Encrypted SPDUs, countersignatures and certificate requests are out of scope; only signedData is produced and parsed.
  • verify_plan checks revocation, the validity period, the SPDU PSID and the non-cryptographic 1609.2 chain rules (chain::check_links) along the chain up to a trust anchor or a verified cache entry, and plans one link operation per unverified certificate; it does not itself verify anything, which is the NodeRuntime's job (chain::validate is the cryptographic validator).

Sources.
