  • standard: (envelope overhead)

fragmenter¶

fragmenter/cert-cycle-partial-hybrid¶

The NDSS 2024 Partially-Hybrid scheme: BSMs keep ECDSA signatures and the hybrid certificate is split into alpha fragments carried in the first alpha SPDUs of each five-SPDU (500 ms) certificate cycle, the rest carrying the certificate digest. P2PCD learning responses are split into beta fragments, each after a uniform 0-250 ms wait.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
tau_spdus - 5 [1, 50] paper: Twardokus, Bindel, Rahbari, McCarthy, "When Cryptography Needs a Hand: Practical Post-Quantum Authentication for V2V Communications", NDSS 2024 — §IV: the certificate cycle is tau = 5 SPDUs. VERIFIED (secondary); see and.
cycle_period_ms ms 500 [100, 5000] paper: Twardokus, Bindel, Rahbari, McCarthy, "When Cryptography Needs a Hand: Practical Post-Quantum Authentication for V2V Communications", NDSS 2024 — §IV: tau = 5 SPDUs = 500 ms at the 10 Hz BSM rate; the industry certificate cadence is every fifth SPDU. VERIFIED (secondary); see and.
hybrid_cert_bytes B 858 [1, 65535] paper: Twardokus, Bindel, Rahbari, McCarthy, "When Cryptography Needs a Hand: Practical Post-Quantum Authentication for V2V Communications", NDSS 2024 — Fig. 4: the Falcon-512 hybrid certificate is 858 B; the general form is 30 + pk + sig. VERIFIED (secondary); see and.
base_frame_bytes B 204 [0, 2304] paper: Twardokus, Bindel, Rahbari, McCarthy, "When Cryptography Needs a Hand: Practical Post-Quantum Authentication for V2V Communications", NDSS 2024 — Fig. 4: the four digest-carrying frames of the Falcon-512 cycle are 204 B each, which is what a cycle frame costs before a certificate fragment. VERIFIED (secondary); see and.
learning_response_fragments - 1 [1, 16] todo-calibrate: beta is reported by NDSS only through completion times (Falcon 250 ms, XMSS 375 ms, Dilithium about 500 ms, SPHINCS+ about 1,000 ms with 8 fragments); no size rule for beta was published, so the Falcon default of 1 is an inference from its 250 ms figure rather than a cited value. (calibration pending)
p2pcd_max_backoff_ms ms 250 [0, 5000] standard: IEEE 1609.2a-2017 §8.1-8.2.4 — P2PCD response backoff, restated by NDSS 2024 §II-A as uniform 0-250 ms with a response threshold of 3

Equations.

  • fragment count: alpha = ceil(cert_bytes / (cap - base_frame_bytes)), 1 <= alpha <= tau At the DSRC cap of 2,304 B with 204 B cycle frames, a Falcon-512 hybrid certificate of 858 B gives alpha = 1, which is the documented anchor. alpha > tau is refused: the next cycle would start before the certificate finished.
  • learning-response delay: T = sum_i U_i(0, p2pcd_max_backoff_ms), mean beta * 125 ms The uniform draws are supplied by the caller from its own RNG stream, so this model draws nothing (ADR 0004 §3).
  • loss amplification: P_sdu = 1 - prod_i (1 - p_i). Per-fragment p_i from the PHY, never the equal-p simplification. The product runs over fragments sorted by index, so the reduction is id-ordered. Independently interpretable segments report the content-weighted mean sum(w_i p_i) instead, because losing one segment loses only its own content.

Assumptions.

  • The messages are never fragmented; only the credential is. A BSM keeps its ECDSA signature and its ordinary size, which is what makes the scheme deployable.
  • The alpha fragments are equal in size, as NDSS describes, and are carried in the first alpha SPDUs of the cycle. This model splits them to within one byte so that they sum to exactly the certificate size.
  • A receiver needs every fragment of one cycle: the reassembly timeout is the cycle period itself (500 ms), after which the next cycle's fragments begin and a partial set is stale (invariant I-N2). That timeout is DERIVED from tau and the 10 Hz rate, not quoted.
  • This model draws no random numbers. The P2PCD backoff's uniform draws are arguments to learning_response_delay, supplied by the caller from its own RngDomain::Crypto stream.
  • The reassembly buffer holds at most 512 partly received certificates per node, which is not a scenario parameter: a set lives for one 500 ms cycle, so the bound does not bind at any density this simulator runs. A peer whose certificate arrives while 512 others are in flight is refused with DropCause::ReassemblyBufferFull rather than evicting one of them.

Limitations.

  • The NDSS frame totals (first frame 1,026 B, the other four 204 B) are whole frames — MAC, envelope, BSM and credential together — and this model returns credential sizes only. Reproducing them here would double-count bytes owned by v2xw-msg, v2xw-sec and v2xw-radio, which invariant I-N1 forbids; they are kept as constants for cross-checking.
  • beta, the learning-response fragment count, is not derived from a size rule (see its calibration plan).
  • The hybrid-certificate size formula '30 + pk + sig' and the 858 B Falcon-512 anchor disagree: with Falcon-512's pk of 897 B the formula gives 1 593 B, while 858 B is 30 + 162 (the ECDSA certificate) + 666 (the Falcon signature). The default is the 858 B Fig. 4 anchor and the other reading is a parameter override; alpha is 1 at the DSRC cap under both.
  • P2PCD's own protocol — learning requests, the response-count threshold of 3, the inline versus out-of-band flavours — is v2xw-proto's. This model supplies the fragmentation and the delay only.
  • At the C-V2X payload cap of 437 B the same certificate needs four fragments, which fits a five-SPDU cycle arithmetically; NDSS's conclusion that C-V2X cannot practically support PQC rests on the whole-frame sizes, which this model does not compute, so it is not reproduced as a refusal here.
  • Loss amplification assumes independence between fragments. It is violated when fragments share a fading state or a burst of interference, which makes the true loss LOWER than the formula predicts; AmplificationMeter::correlation_gap is how that shows up in the output.
  • Loss amplification does NOT assume an equal p per fragment: fragments differ in size and therefore in PER, so the model takes the per-fragment p_i from the PHY and computes P_sdu = 1 - prod(1 - p_i). The equal-p form 1 - (1 - p)^n is used only to reproduce §7.4's worked example.
  • Loss amplification assumes no retransmission. That is true for the group-addressed frames these strategies carry: 802.11 neither acknowledges nor fragments them at the MAC.
  • Loss amplification assumes a reassembly timeout longer than the spread of fragment arrivals. When it is not, timeouts add loss the formula does not model and the realised rate rises ABOVE the prediction.

Ignores.

  • The cryptographic work itself: key sizes, signing and verification cost are v2xw-sec's primitive descriptors.
  • The fully hybrid (dual-signature) and pure-PQ designs, which NDSS finds infeasible at the DSRC payload cap.
  • The 0.25-0.39 ms added per-BSM delay NDSS measures, which belongs to the node's compute model.

Sources.

  • paper: Twardokus, Bindel, Rahbari, McCarthy, "When Cryptography Needs a Hand: Practical Post-Quantum Authentication for V2V Communications", NDSS 2024 — §II-B, §III, §IV, §V-D — the Partially-Hybrid design and its measurements. VERIFIED (secondary); see and.
  • standard: IEEE 1609.2a-2017 §6.3.9, §8.1-8.2.4 — P2PCD, its backoff and its thresholds
  • paper: and — the fact sheets these anchors were extracted from
  • paper:, §7.4, §9.5

fragmenter/facilities-segmentation¶

ETSI facilities-layer segmentation (MAPEM layerID, CPM messageSegmentInfo): the service splits an oversize message into independently interpretable segments, each a complete separately signed message, so losing one segment loses only its own content.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
segmentation_threshold_bytes B 1100 [200, 2304] standard: ETSI TR 103 562 §5.5 — segmentation threshold used in the CPM simulation study. VERIFIED. The normative trigger is TS 103 324 §6.1.3.1's MTU_CPM; this threshold is lower and therefore the one that fires first.
per_segment_overhead_bytes B 121 [0, 512] todo-calibrate: The 121 B anchor is TR 103 562 Table 3's mandatory CPM DEs (header + management + station data), which is what each segment repeats; the messageSegmentInfo DE itself is not separately sized in any accessible source, so 121 B is a floor rather than a measured overhead. (calibration pending)
max_packet_lifetime_ms ms 1000 [100, 10000] standard: ETSI TS 103 324 §5.3.3 — GeoNetworking maximum packet lifetime for a CPM is at most 1 000 ms

Equations.

  • segment count: n = ceil(L / (min(threshold, MTU) - overhead)) MTU_CPM = MTU_AL - HD_CPM - HD_NT [TS 103 324 §6.1.3.1] is what the caller passes as MTU; the threshold is the ETSI simulation value of 1,100 B [TR 103 562 §5.5]. Payloads are split as equally as possible and sum to exactly L.
  • loss amplification: P_sdu = 1 - prod_i (1 - p_i). Per-fragment p_i from the PHY, never the equal-p simplification. The product runs over fragments sorted by index, so the reduction is id-ordered. Independently interpretable segments report the content-weighted mean sum(w_i p_i) instead, because losing one segment loses only its own content.

Assumptions.

  • Every segment is a complete, separately signed message, so a receiver interprets each one on its own: there is no reassembly, no reassembly buffer and no reassembly timeout (invariant I-N2: the timeout is 'none').
  • Content is divided as equally as possible across the segments. A real CPS divides by perceived object, so segment sizes vary with how many objects each carries; the equal split is the size-accurate stand-in.
  • The caller passes MTU_CPM (MTU_AL less the CPM's own and the networking/transport headers) as the MTU; this model does not compute it.

Limitations.

  • Loss does not amplify here, so the SDU-level figure this model reports is the payload-weighted mean of the per-segment losses, not 1 - prod(1 - p_i). The product is still reported as SduLossModel::p_any_fragment_lost — the chance that some content is missing — because that is the quantity a completeness metric needs.
  • No segment-count cap is enforced. TS 103 324's messageSegmentInfo constrains the number of segments, but the constraint was not extracted from an accessible source, so a configuration that produces an implausible number of segments is not refused (it is refused only above a u16 count).
  • MAPEM's layerID fragmentation and CPM's messageSegmentInfo segmentation are modelled by one size rule. The identifiers differ and so do the services; what they share — independently interpretable pieces, no reassembly — is what this model implements.
  • Loss amplification assumes independence between fragments. It is violated when fragments share a fading state or a burst of interference, which makes the true loss LOWER than the formula predicts; AmplificationMeter::correlation_gap is how that shows up in the output.
  • Loss amplification does NOT assume an equal p per fragment: fragments differ in size and therefore in PER, so the model takes the per-fragment p_i from the PHY and computes P_sdu = 1 - prod(1 - p_i). The equal-p form 1 - (1 - p)^n is used only to reproduce §7.4's worked example.
  • Loss amplification assumes no retransmission. That is true for the group-addressed frames these strategies carry: 802.11 neither acknowledges nor fragments them at the MAC.
  • Loss amplification assumes a reassembly timeout longer than the spread of fragment arrivals. When it is not, timeouts add loss the formula does not model and the realised rate rises ABOVE the prediction.

Ignores.

  • Object selection: which perceived objects go into which segment is the CPM generator's rule, not this model's.
  • The per-segment security envelope's own bytes, which are v2xw-sec's and reach this model inside the SDU size.

Sources.

  • standard: ETSI TS 103 301 §6.4.1 — RLT service fragmentation of MAPEM by layerID (ISO/TS 19091 Annex G)
  • standard: ETSI TS 103 324 V2.1.1 §6.1.2.1, §6.1.3.1, §5.3.3, §7.1 — CPM segmentation, MTU_CPM and the maximum packet lifetime
  • standard: ETSI TR 103 562 §5.5, Table 3 — the simulation segmentation threshold and the mandatory CPM containers
  • paper:, §7.4, §8.2

fragmenter/generic-sdu¶

Splits any SDU into ceil(L / MTU_eff) fragments with a four-byte fragment header (id 2 + index 1 + count 1, a design choice), and reassembles per (sender, SDU) with a timeout and tolerance for out-of-order arrival.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
fragment_header_bytes B 4 [0, 64] todo-calibrate: A design choice, not a standard: no standard defines this strategy. fixes the layout as id 2 + index 1 + count 1 = 4 B and requires it to be recorded on this card.. The field widths are enforced rather than decorative: the one-octet count caps the split at 255 fragments. (calibration pending)
reassembly_timeout_ms ms 1000 [50, 10000] todo-calibrate: Anchored on the GeoNetworking maximum packet lifetime for a CPM, 1 000 ms [ETSI TS 103 324 §5.3.3], which bounds how long an oversize message's pieces are on the air — but no source gives a reassembly timeout for this invented strategy. (calibration pending)
max_open_sdus - 64 [1, 4096] todo-calibrate: A bounded buffer is a modelling decision: an unbounded one would make a node's memory a function of how many peers sent oversize SDUs, which is exactly the denial-of-service a real implementation caps. (calibration pending)

Equations.

  • fragment count: n = ceil(L / (MTU - 4)), n <= 255 Payloads are split as equally as possible and sum to exactly L. An SDU that already fits the MTU is passed through whole, with no fragment header.
  • loss amplification: P_sdu = 1 - prod_i (1 - p_i). Per-fragment p_i from the PHY, never the equal-p simplification. The product runs over fragments sorted by index, so the reduction is id-ordered. Independently interpretable segments report the content-weighted mean sum(w_i p_i) instead, because losing one segment loses only its own content.

Assumptions.

  • Reassembly is keyed per (sender, SDU). The sender is the transmitting node at these tiers; at the high tier it becomes the sender's pseudonym digest, which is what a receiver actually observes.
  • Out-of-order arrival is normal and is tolerated: fragments are tracked as a set of indices, so any arrival order reassembles identically.
  • A duplicate fragment is tolerated and its bytes are counted once, because a repeated fragment is what a sender's own repetition looks like. This holds after the set has completed too: a completed (sender, SDU) is remembered for one reassembly timeout and a fragment arriving against it is reported duplicate, which is not a loss, rather than opening a second reassembly that could never finish.
  • The reassembly timer starts at the first fragment and is not extended, so the timeout bounds the whole set's lifetime — which is the condition assumption 4 is about (invariant I-N2).
  • A full buffer refuses the arriving SDU rather than evicting an older one, so the loss is attributed to the SDU that could not be admitted and does not depend on an eviction order.

Limitations.

  • The fragment header is invented (see its calibration note). Nothing interoperates with it.
  • The two-octet id field would alias after 65 536 outstanding SDUs from one peer. The model keys its buffer by the full SduId and caps the buffer at max_open_sdus, so the aliasing cannot occur here; a real 16-bit field would have to handle it.
  • No per-fragment timer: one deadline covers the whole set. Per-fragment timers are the high net tier.
  • No retransmission of a missing fragment. There is no mechanism to request one in any of the broadcast message sets this strategy would carry.
  • Loss amplification assumes independence between fragments. It is violated when fragments share a fading state or a burst of interference, which makes the true loss LOWER than the formula predicts; AmplificationMeter::correlation_gap is how that shows up in the output.
  • Loss amplification does NOT assume an equal p per fragment: fragments differ in size and therefore in PER, so the model takes the per-fragment p_i from the PHY and computes P_sdu = 1 - prod(1 - p_i). The equal-p form 1 - (1 - p)^n is used only to reproduce §7.4's worked example.
  • Loss amplification assumes no retransmission. That is true for the group-addressed frames these strategies carry: 802.11 neither acknowledges nor fragments them at the MAC.
  • Loss amplification assumes a reassembly timeout longer than the spread of fragment arrivals. When it is not, timeouts add loss the formula does not model and the realised rate rises ABOVE the prediction.

Ignores.

  • Per-fragment timers, P2PCD interaction and GeoNetworking forwarding, which are the high net tier.
  • Interleaving policy: which fragment goes out when is the sender's scheduling decision, not this model's.

Sources.

  • paper: — 'fragmenter/generic-sdu: split any SDU into ceil(L/MTU_eff) fragments with a 4-byte fragment header (id 2 + index 1 + count 1, a design choice recorded in the card); reassembly buffer per (sender digest, sdu id) with timeout; out-of-order tolerated'
  • standard: ETSI TS 103 324 §5.3.3 — the 1 000 ms GeoNetworking maximum packet lifetime the default timeout is anchored on
  • paper: — loss amplification

fragmenter/none¶

Does not fragment: an SDU above the MTU is dropped with DropCause::Mtu. The default for BSM and CAM, whose sizes are far below either network layer's MTU.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Equations.

  • loss amplification: P_sdu = 1 - prod_i (1 - p_i). Per-fragment p_i from the PHY, never the equal-p simplification. The product runs over fragments sorted by index, so the reduction is id-ordered. Independently interpretable segments report the content-weighted mean sum(w_i p_i) instead, because losing one segment loses only its own content.

Assumptions.

  • The message set this strategy carries fits the MTU: a BSM SPDU is 180-250 B and a secured CAM 182-807 B (§9.3), against 1,398 B for GeoNetworking and 1,400 B for the WSMP MIB default. An SDU above the MTU is therefore a modelling error or a deliberately oversize payload, and both are better reported than split.
  • No reassembly state exists, so nothing can time out. Invariant I-N2's reassembly timeout is 'none' for this model, which Fragmenter::reassembly_timeout returns as None.

Limitations.

  • There is no loss amplification to report: n = 1, so P_sdu = p. The formula is on this card so that a reader comparing the four strategies sees the same one (invariant I-N2).
  • A fragment from a peer configured with a different strategy is refused with DropCause::UnexpectedFragment rather than reassembled.
  • Loss amplification assumes independence between fragments. It is violated when fragments share a fading state or a burst of interference, which makes the true loss LOWER than the formula predicts; AmplificationMeter::correlation_gap is how that shows up in the output.
  • Loss amplification does NOT assume an equal p per fragment: fragments differ in size and therefore in PER, so the model takes the per-fragment p_i from the PHY and computes P_sdu = 1 - prod(1 - p_i). The equal-p form 1 - (1 - p)^n is used only to reproduce §7.4's worked example.
  • Loss amplification assumes no retransmission. That is true for the group-addressed frames these strategies carry: 802.11 neither acknowledges nor fragments them at the MAC.
  • Loss amplification assumes a reassembly timeout longer than the spread of fragment arrivals. When it is not, timeouts add loss the formula does not model and the realised rate rises ABOVE the prediction.

Ignores.

  • Every form of fragmentation: facilities-layer segmentation, certificate-cycle fragmentation and generic SDU fragmentation are the other three models.

Sources.

  • paper: — 'fragmenter/none: oversize SDUs are rejected (DropCause::Mtu); none; the default for BSM and CAM'

generator¶

generator/bsm-j2945-1¶

BSM generation at the J2945/1 nominal 10 Hz, Part I in every message; the inter-transmission-time control loop is the DCC family's.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
nominal_itt_ms ms 100 standard: SAE J2945/1 — 10 Hz nominal; VERIFIED via secondary sources (NDSS 2024 §II, Bindel 2021, Rostami 2018)
min_itt_ms ms 100 todo-calibrate: vMinITT 100 ms is implied rather than sourced ( marks it UNVERIFIED) (calibration pending)
max_itt_ms ms 600 todo-calibrate: vMaxITT 600 ms is implied rather than sourced ( marks it UNVERIFIED) (calibration pending)

Assumptions.

  • Part I is present in every BSM, which is what J2735 requires of BSMcoreData.

Limitations.

  • Part II is not generated: PathHistory (about 300 m, max 23 points) and PathPrediction are 's UNVERIFIED rows and arrive with the hand-written BSM codec.
  • The EEBL event flag at a deceleration of 0,4 g is not raised here; on_event carries the HardBraking event that will raise it.
  • The inter-transmission-time control of J2945/1 is not implemented here by design (see the type's documentation); DccState::t_off is honoured only as a floor.

Sources.

  • standard: SAE J2945/1 — BSM generation records which of its numbers are VERIFIED and which are not
  • paper: Rostami 2018; Bindel 2021; NDSS 2024 §II — 10 Hz confirmed by secondary sources

generator/cam-en302637-2¶

CAM generation rules of EN 302 637-2: the three dynamics triggers, the adaptive periodic trigger with its N_GenCam reset, and the 500 ms low-frequency container.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
t_gen_cam_min_ms ms 100 standard: ETSI EN 302 637-2 V1.4.1 §6.1.3
t_gen_cam_max_ms ms 1000 standard: ETSI EN 302 637-2 V1.4.1 §6.1.3
t_check_cam_gen_ms ms 100 standard: ETSI EN 302 637-2 V1.4.1 §6.1.3
n_gen_cam count 3 standard: ETSI EN 302 637-2 V1.4.1 §6.1.3
heading_threshold_deg degree 4.0 standard: ETSI EN 302 637-2 V1.4.1 §6.1.3
position_threshold_m m 4.0 standard: ETSI EN 302 637-2 V1.4.1 §6.1.3
speed_threshold_mps m/s 0.5 standard: ETSI EN 302 637-2 V1.4.1 §6.1.3
low_frequency_interval_ms ms 500 standard: ETSI EN 302 637-2 V1.4.1 §6.1.4

Equations.

  • trigger 1 (dynamics): |heading - heading_last| > 4 deg OR |pos - pos_last| > 4 m OR |v - v_last| > 0,5 m/s
  • T_GenCam update: trigger 1: T_GenCam := clamp(elapsed, T_GenCamMin, T_GenCamMax); after N_GenCam consecutive trigger-2 CAMs: T_GenCam := T_GenCamMax

Assumptions.

  • Every interval is measured on the sending node's own believed clock, not on simulated time, so a drifting or attacked clock changes the observed CAM rate.
  • T_GenCam_Dcc arrives through DccState::t_off and acts only as a floor on the inter-CAM interval; the rest of the DCC state machine is the DCC family's.

Limitations.

  • The special-vehicle container's own >= 500 ms cadence and the RSU CAM's >= 1,000 ms cadence are not implemented: this crate's builder fills neither container.
  • The <= 50 ms generation budget of §6.1.3 is not modelled as a delay; a CAM is assembled instantaneously.

Sources.

  • standard: ETSI EN 302 637-2 V1.4.1 §6.1.3, §6.1.4, §6.1.5
  • standard: C2C-CC RS 2037 RS_BSP_293, RS_BSP_297 — T_GenCam_Dcc = T_off, N_GenCam = pCamGenNumber
  • paper: C2C-CC TR 2052 Observation 10 — field mean CAM interval 0,33-0,47 s, the validation target

gnss¶

gnss/error/gauss-markov¶

A node's belief about its own position: a first-order Gauss-Markov bias per axis plus white noise, with outliers, degraded bursts and outages, whose scales are fitted at construction to the measured Reid 2019 error percentiles. The reported confidence comes from the receiver's own noise parameters and never from the bias it is carrying, which is the defect records in the legacy model.

version 1.2.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
reid_horizontal_percentiles m [[0.68, 3.07], [0.95, 5.3], [0.99, 9.38]] paper: Reid et al. 2019, measured automotive GNSS error percentiles
reid_lateral_percentiles m [[0.68, 1.92], [0.95, 3.88], [0.99, 5.74]] paper: Reid et al. 2019, measured automotive GNSS error percentiles
reid_longitudinal_percentiles m [[0.68, 2.11], [0.95, 4.44], [0.99, 7.95]] paper: Reid et al. 2019, measured automotive GNSS error percentiles
reid_vertical_percentiles m [[0.68, 4.59], [0.95, 9.42], [0.99, 12.83]] paper: Reid et al. 2019, measured automotive GNSS error percentiles
reid_rtk_horizontal_percentiles m [[0.68, 0.26], [0.95, 1.05], [0.99, 3.91]] paper: Reid et al. 2019, measured automotive GNSS error percentiles
sigma_white_horizontal m 1.6 datasheet: MathWorks gpsSensor defaults: DecayFactor 0.999 per sample, horizontal accuracy 1.6 m, vertical 3 m. the sample rate the decay factor applies at is not in the sheet; 1 Hz is gpsSensor's own default and is the assumption recorded here
sigma_white_vertical m 3.0 datasheet: MathWorks gpsSensor defaults: DecayFactor 0.999 per sample, horizontal accuracy 1.6 m, vertical 3 m. the sample rate the decay factor applies at is not in the sheet; 1 Hz is gpsSensor's own default and is the assumption recorded here
tau s 999.4999166249727 datasheet: MathWorks gpsSensor defaults: DecayFactor 0.999 per sample, horizontal accuracy 1.6 m, vertical 3 m. the sample rate the decay factor applies at is not in the sheet; 1 Hz is gpsSensor's own default and is the assumption recorded here
sigma_bias_horizontal m 0.0 paper: DERIVED: √(σ_fitted² − σ_white²) from the Reid fit and the gpsSensor white part. the arithmetic is in GaussMarkovGnss::new
sigma_bias_vertical m 0.0 paper: DERIVED: √(σ_fitted² − σ_white²) for the vertical axis
outage_p95 s {"rtk": 60.0, "sps": 7.0} paper: Reid et al. 2019, measured automotive GNSS error percentiles
env_scale 1 {"canyon-mitigated": 3.1172638436482085, "deep-canyon": 10.104234527687296, "... paper: Wen & Hsu urban-canyon means against the Reid open-sky mean. §3.8 marks the choice of class TODO: calibrate (a sky-view metric has still to be defined); the ratios themselves are measured
sigma_velocity m/s 0.1020426913849308 standard: GPS SPS PS 2020 Table 3.8-3: velocity ≤ 0.2 m/s at 95 %. read as the 95 % point of a zero-mean Gaussian: σ = 0.2/1.96
sigma_heading rad 0.0 todo-calibrate: heading noise has no figure in §3.8 at all (calibration pending)
outlier_rate 1 0.01 todo-calibrate: the multipath outlier rate has no measured source in the sheets; the frozen engine's value (run.py L300) is used (calibration pending)
outlier_magnitude m 12.0 code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L297-313
burst_rate 1 0.006 code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L297-313
burst_factor 1 6.0 code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L297-313
burst_duration s 3.0 code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L297-313
outage_rate 1 0.0 code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L297-313
faulty_bias_multiplier 1 5.0 code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L297-313
weather_error_multiplier 1 {"clear": 1.0, "fog": 2.0, "rain": 1.5, "snow": 2.5} code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L297-313
smoothing_tau_s s 0.0 standard: Hatch 1982, carrier smoothing of code; RTCA DO-229 100 s smoothing time constant (0: raw fixes). ASSUMPTION: a position-domain, Doppler-aided form of the code smoothing stands in for an automotive receiver's navigation filter
stated_sigma_m m 0.0 dataset: USDOT ITS DataHub CV-pilot BSMs, coreData.accuracy.semiMajor (one sigma): median 0.30 m Tampa (34,464 BSMs), 0.40 m NYC (65,533), 0.50 m Wyoming (6,597), 2.00 m Orem (4.1 M); 0 states the model's honest radius. measured with tools/realdata's normaliser over the downloaded extracts; the deployed devices state far less than Reid 2019's measured error
stated_sigma_log_sd 1 0.0 dataset: Tampa CV-pilot per-device semiMajor medians: p50 0.30 m, p90 0.85 m, so ln(0.85/0.30)/1.2816 = 0.81 (0: every device states the median, as Orem's do)
static_hold_mps m/s 0.0 datasheet: u-blox 8 / M8 Receiver Description, Static hold (0: off). ASSUMPTION: the threshold a preset sets is its own choice; the mechanism is the receiver's

Equations.

  • Gauss-Markov bias: b_{k+1} = a·b_k + q·N(0,1), a = exp(−Δt/τ), q = σ_b·√(1 − a²) q keeps the process stationary at var(b) = σ_b² for every Δt; R(Δt) = σ²·exp(−|Δt|/τ)
  • estimate: pos = truth + b + N(0, σ_w) (+ m_o at a uniform angle with probability p_o)
  • reported confidence: semi_major = semi_minor = 2.4477·√(σ_nom² + σ_b²) the receiver's own parameters; the legacy model used the realised bias, which is an oracle quantity (§2.10)
  • scale fit: σ = Σ k_p·v_p / Σ k_p², residual_p = σ·k_p − v_p k_p is the Rayleigh factor √(−2 ln(1−p)) for a horizontal magnitude and the folded-normal factor Φ⁻¹((1+p)/2) for a single axis
  • bias-white split: σ_b = √(max(0, σ² − σ_w²)) DERIVED from the fitted total and the cited white part

Assumptions.

  • The horizontal error is isotropic: one scale for both horizontal axes. The lateral and longitudinal fits are on the card so a reader can see how much anisotropy the measurement actually shows (about 10 %).
  • One Gaussian scale cannot reproduce three measured percentiles; the fit residuals are on this card rather than hidden.
  • The reported confidence uses the nominal σ, not the burst-inflated one: a real receiver under-reports during a multipath spike, which is what makes the sustained residual detectable (§2.10).
  • The gpsSensor decay factor is applied at a 1 Hz sample interval.

Limitations.

  • No satellite geometry, no multipath as a function of the real building geometry — only an environment class (§3.8 ignores).
  • Receiver filtering is modelled only as the first-order Doppler-aided smoothing and static hold of smoothing_tau_s and static_hold_mps, both off by default: raw fixes then disagree with their own velocity by metres between 10 Hz estimates (R-USFP).

Ignores.

  • Satellite geometry, true-geometry multipath and any receiver filter beyond the first-order smoothing.

Sources.

  • paper: Reid et al. 2019, measured automotive GNSS error percentiles
  • datasheet: MathWorks gpsSensor defaults: DecayFactor 0.999 per sample, horizontal accuracy 1.6 m, vertical 3 m. the sample rate the decay factor applies at is not in the sheet; 1 Hz is gpsSensor's own default and is the assumption recorded here

gnss/error/ou-bias-legacy¶

The frozen reference engine's GNSS model: an Ornstein-Uhlenbeck bias per axis, white noise scaled by a per-vehicle quality draw and the weather, multipath outliers and transient degraded bursts. Kept as the abstract-tier preset and as the parity oracle for the frozen corpus, with the three fixes records.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract; hosting in-process; draws random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source

weather_mult 1 {"clear": 1.0, "fog": 2.0, "rain": 1.5, "snow": 2.5} code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L297-313, L1874-1897. the frozen reference engine's measure

sigma_velocity m/s 0.1020426913849308 standard: GPS SPS PS 2020 Table 3.8-3: velocity ≤ 0.2 m/s at 95 %. the inventory's addition: the legacy model gave speed no noise at all
sigma_heading rad 0.0 todo-calibrate: no heading-noise figure exists in §2.10 or §3.8 (calibration pending)

Equations.

  • OU bias: bias_{k+1} = a·bias_k + q·N(0,1), a = exp(−dt/τ_b), q = σ_b·√(1 − a²)
  • noise scale: σ = σ_w · quality · weather_mult · (degrade_factor if in a burst) quality = 0.5 + Exp(λ = 1.2), drawn once per vehicle
  • reported confidence (corrected): semi_major = semi_minor = 2.448·√(σ_nom² + σ_b²) the legacy line used the realised bias — bias_x² + bias_y² — which is an oracle quantity; §2.10 records the replacement and this card is where it is visible

Assumptions.

  • The per-vehicle quality is drawn once, at the node's first estimate, from that node's own stream — so it does not depend on how many other nodes were sampled first.
  • The reported confidence is computed from the model's own parameters. This is the one deliberate difference from the frozen engine's arithmetic, and §2.10 records why.

Limitations.

  • Every default is code (legacy) with no measured backing; gnss/error/gauss-markov is the sourced replacement and the default for the medium and high tiers.
  • The bias is horizontal only, as in the legacy model: there is no vertical error.

Ignores.

  • Everything §3.8's measured model models.

backend/hsm-appliance-luna7¶

Network-attached HSM appliance (Thales Luna Network HSM 7, A750 enterprise tier). The brief does not split the ECC P-256 transactions-per-second figure into sign and verify, so it is applied to both and the choice is flagged; per-operation latency is not published and the service time is the derived reciprocal.

bs/lte-macro-generic¶

Generic LTE macro base station for the Uu top-up and report paths. Carries only 3GPP evaluation assumptions (macro Tx power, BS and UE noise figures, inter-site distance and the eNB-UE path-loss reference); every hardware, antenna and scheduling parameter is uncalibrated, which is the honest state of the cellular research sheets.

obu/cohda-mk5¶

Commercial DSRC OBU (Cohda MK5). The most widely fielded DSRC OBU of the USDOT pilots' era and the least documented for crypto: Cohda publishes CPU rating, memory, radio and GNSS figures but nothing about SXF1700 sign or verify throughput, so the verify rate is a flagged proxy from a later NXP baseband and the sign path is unknown.

version 2026-09-18.1; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

obu/generic-automotive-soc-no-hsm¶

Generic automotive SoC with no secure element or accelerator: the profile for scenarios asking what happens when all crypto runs in software. Anchored on the best-documented Cortex-A72-class figures (Raspberry Pi 4B OpenSSL and liboqs), which are developer-board benchmarks and not automotive parts, and are flagged as proxies throughout.

obu/pq-capable-hypothetical¶

The optimistic anchor is liboqs on a Raspberry Pi 5; the pessimistic bound is the NDSS 2024 measurement on production Cohda MK6 hardware, carried in obu/cohda-mk6c-qualcomm-9150.

obu/unex-obu-301-craton2¶

Reference OBU profile (Autotalks CRATON2 in the Unex OBU-301E). The only OBU in the hardware sheet publishing core count, clock and DMIPS, RAM and flash, HSM signing throughput and latency, hardware verification throughput, RX threshold, TX power, antenna gain and GNSS sensitivity. Two caveats: Autotalks itself publishes no numeric rates (the Unex integrator sheets quote them), and 128 MB of RAM is an order of magnitude below the Cohda units, which matters for store-size accounting.

version 2026-09-18.1; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

obu/unex-obu-301-craton2-software¶

The reference OBU (Unex OBU-301E, Autotalks CRATON2) with its security hardware set aside: every ECDSA operation runs in software on the application cores. This is the profile for studying verify-dominated cost — on one processor ECDSA verification costs about twice signing — where the hardware variant signs in a slow secure element and verifies on a line-rate accelerator. The software figures are Cortex-A53 proxies, flagged as such.

version 2026-09-18.1; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

rsu/commsignia-its-rs4¶

Roadside unit (Commsignia ITS-RS4), the most complete public RSU datasheet in the hardware sheet. Two figures are carried as the sheet carries them rather than reconciled: the brief's '<50 usec signing delay' is almost certainly an OCR error for the corroborated cross-vendor SLI97 figure of <50 ms, and the radio block is variant-dependent because the board takes interchangeable radios.

vru-device/handset-generic¶

A generic vulnerable-road-user device: a handset or a pedestrian beacon whose V2X application runs in software on its own application processor. Nothing about a real handset is published in the design set's hardware research, so the compute block is not-published and the two software-crypto figures are flagged proxies from the Cortex-A72-class benchmarks of obu/generic-automotive-soc-no-hsm. The radio block carries the pedestrian-UE transmit power, antenna gain and receiver noise figure of the 3GPP evaluation methodology, marked as assumptions rather than as device data. A handset with a hardware-backed keystore, or a C-V2X rather than an ITS-G5 radio, is a different profile and not this one.

version 2026-09-22.1; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

ma-pipeline¶

threat/ma/legacy-window¶

Windowed correlation of misbehaviour reports at the legacy operating point: k distinct trusted reporters, over distinct seconds, spanning a minimum time, inside a sliding window, with a reporter budget and a reputation cap.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
report_threshold_k - 3 todo-calibrate: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py :: PipelineConfig.report_threshold_k (legacy, uncited there). reproduced exactly for dataset conformance; the legacy engine gave no source for it (calibration pending)
revoke_min_seconds - 4 todo-calibrate: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py :: PipelineConfig.revoke_min_seconds (legacy, uncited there). reproduced exactly for dataset conformance; the legacy engine gave no source for it (calibration pending)
revoke_persist_s s 3.0 todo-calibrate: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py :: PipelineConfig.revoke_persist_s (legacy, uncited there). reproduced exactly for dataset conformance; the legacy engine gave no source for it (calibration pending)
revoke_window_s s 15.0 todo-calibrate: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py :: PipelineConfig.revoke_window_s (legacy, uncited there). reproduced exactly for dataset conformance; the legacy engine gave no source for it (calibration pending)
ma_defence - true code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py :: PipelineConfig.ma_defense (legacy)
reputation_max - 40 todo-calibrate: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py :: PipelineConfig.reputation_max (legacy, uncited there). reproduced exactly for dataset conformance; the legacy engine gave no source for it (calibration pending)
report_budget - 30 todo-calibrate: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py :: PipelineConfig.report_budget (legacy, uncited there). reproduced exactly for dataset conformance; the legacy engine gave no source for it (calibration pending)

Equations.

  • revocation condition: |{trusted reporters in window}| ≥ k ∧ |{distinct seconds}| ≥ n ∧ (t_last − t_first) ≥ persist

Assumptions.

  • A report names its subject by pseudonym digest only; the pipeline never resolves it to an actor, which is the protocol's identity-resolution flow.
  • Reports arrive with their own transport delay already applied, so ingest_time is authority time and the correlation window is measured in it.

Limitations.

  • No investigation stage: identity resolution and the revocation flow belong to v2xw-proto (§3.3).
  • No service model: the authority's own processing cost is not charged here.
  • Dismiss and Investigate are modelled as decisions the interface can express but the legacy operating point never produces; a replacement stage can.

Sources.

  • code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py :: the online MA decision pass + trusted (legacy)
  • code:

metric¶

metrics/energy¶

Integrates the selected emission and electric-vehicle energy models over every motor vehicle's ground-truth trajectory (gt.kinematics) and reports emissions, fuel, battery energy, state of charge and stops per window, per class, per lane and per vehicle.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
stop_speed_mps m/s 0.1 code: Eclipse SUMO documentation, Simulation/Output: halting below 0.1 m/s
rearm_speed_mps m/s 1.0 todo-calibrate: hysteresis so creeping in a queue is one stop, not several (calibration pending)
max_gap_s s 5.0 todo-calibrate: a longer gap between two records of one actor is two trips (calibration pending)
grade_clamp - 0.3 todo-calibrate: grades steeper than this are recording artefacts (calibration pending)
ev.initial_soc - 0.8 todo-calibrate: state of charge every EV starts with (calibration pending)
gasoline_g_per_l g/L 742.0 standard: EN 228:2012 (petrol density 720-775 kg/m3 at 15 C) and EN 590:2013 (diesel 820-845 kg/m3); point values 742 g/L and 836 g/L as SUMO's HelpersPHEMlight and HelpersHBEFA3 use them
diesel_g_per_l g/L 836.0 standard: EN 228:2012 (petrol density 720-775 kg/m3 at 15 C) and EN 590:2013 (diesel 820-845 kg/m3); point values 742 g/L and 836 g/L as SUMO's HelpersPHEMlight and HelpersHBEFA3 use them

Equations.

  • interval: v = (v0 + v1)/2, a = (v1 - v0)/dt, s = v dt, grade = dz/s (s >= 1 m, |grade| <= 0.3) between consecutive records of one actor at most 5 s apart
  • integration: mass += rate(class, state) dt; battery -= P_bat(state) dt
  • stop: a stop is counted when v < stop_speed after v > 1 m/s

Assumptions.

  • emission model energy/emission/hbefa3; EV model energy/ev/kurczveil
  • every vehicle's powertrain is drawn once from the fleet section, keyed by actor id

Limitations.

  • post-hoc: the energy state does not feed back into driving (an empty EV keeps moving), as in SUMO's battery device without its stop-on-empty option

Ignores.

  • charging
  • upstream emissions of fuel production and electricity generation

Sources.

  • paper: Krajzewicz D., Behrisch M., Wagner P., Luz R., Krumnow M. (2015) Second Generation of Pollutant Emission Models for SUMO. In: Behrisch M., Weber M. (eds) Modeling Mobility with Open Data, Lecture Notes in Mobility, Springer. the HBEFA 3.1 polynomial fits and their per-class coefficients
  • paper: Kurczveil T., López P.A., Schnieder E. (2014) Implementation of an Energy Model and a Charging Infrastructure in SUMO. In: Behrisch M., Krajzewicz D., Weber M. (eds) Simulation of Urban Mobility (SUMO 2013), LNCS 8594, Springer. energy balance over one step: kinetic, potential, rotational, air, rolling and curve losses, constant consumers, propulsion and recuperation efficiencies
  • standard: US EPA, MOVES (Motor Vehicle Emission Simulator) technical documentation: running-exhaust operating modes binned by speed and scaled tractive power (STP, kW/tonne) from the source type's road-load coefficients A, B, C, mass m and fixed mass factor f. operating-mode definitions 0, 1, 11-16, 21-30, 33-40 and the scaled tractive power

mobility¶

mobility/car-following/acc¶

Longitudinal acceleration of a vehicle under adaptive cruise control: the PATH multi-mode controller identified from production ACC field data. Radar only — no V2X. What every CACC in the library degrades to when its messages are lost or late.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source

weather.surface_friction 1 {"dry": null, "flooded": 0.4, "ice": 0.1, "snow": 0.25, "wet": 0.5} paper: Fricke, Traffic Accident Reconstruction, Northwestern University Traffic Institute, 1990: tyre-pavement friction ranges (wet 0.4-0.6, packed snow 0.2-0.3, ice 0.05-0.15). secondary: midpoints of ranges quoted in reconstruction practice, not re-verified against a primary friction survey. The deceleration cap is mu*g; a dry road is not capped
weather.sight_distance - {"deceleration_mps2": 3.4, "perception_reaction_s": 2.5} standard: AASHTO A Policy on Geometric Design of Highways and Streets (2018) §3.2.2, Eq. 3-2: stopping sight distance d = v*t + v^2/(2a); the desired speed is capped at the v that stops within the visibility
weather.road_context - {"freeway_rows_at_or_above_mps": 22.352} code: this crate: the FHWA table is by facility type, which a lane does not carry; lanes posted at 50 mph or more take the freeway rows, the rest the arterial rows

Equations.

  • speed control: a = −k_sc·(v − v0) (g > 120 m)
  • gap control: e = g − t_hw·v − d0, d0 = max(0, min(75/v − 5, 2)); a = k_Δv·Δv + k_e·e by mode (gap / collision avoidance / closing)
  • safety envelope: a ≤ (v_safe(emergency decel, Δt) + min(2 m/s, g) − v)/Δt

Limitations.

  • Between 100 m and 120 m the lower of speed and gap control applies, where SUMO keeps the previously applied law (per-vehicle memory this trait does not carry).
  • Virtual leaders (stop lines, signals, yields) are handed to the IDM with the same driver: the ACC law was identified on vehicle targets only.
  • String stable in the platoon experiment at 1.1 s (acceleration L2 gain 0.967), where Milanés & Shladover 2014 measured production ACCs to be string-unstable: the heavily damped gap-closing mode (0.8·Δv) dominates the response. Not tuned away; a calibration against their trajectories is the open item.

Sources.

  • paper: Milanés & Shladover 2014, Modeling cooperative and autonomous adaptive cruise control dynamic responses using experimental data, Transportation Research C 48:285-300 (DOI 10.1016/j.trc.2014.09.001)
  • paper: Xiao, Wang & van Arem 2017, Realistic car-following models for microscopic simulation of adaptive and cooperative adaptive cruise control vehicles, Transportation Research Record 2623:1-9 (DOI 10.3141/2623-01)
  • paper: Xiao, Wang, Schakel & van Arem 2018, Unravelling effects of cooperative adaptive cruise control deactivation on traffic flow characteristics at merging bottlenecks, Transportation Research C 96:380-397 (eq. 5, the dynamic margin d0)
  • code: SUMO src/microsim/cfmodels/MSCFModel_ACC.cpp and MSCFModel_CACC.cpp (EPL-2.0, read only, never vendored): gains, thresholds, override

mobility/car-following/cacc-milanes¶

Cooperative adaptive cruise control by the PATH field controller (Milanés & Shladover 2014): a 0.6 s time gap held with the predecessor's speed as received over V2X, degrading to ACC when messages are lost or late.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

weather.sight_distance - {"deceleration_mps2": 3.4, "perception_reaction_s": 2.5} standard: AASHTO A Policy on Geometric Design of Highways and Streets (2018) §3.2.2, Eq. 3-2: stopping sight distance d = v*t + v^2/(2a); the desired speed is capped at the v that stops within the visibility
weather.road_context - {"freeway_rows_at_or_above_mps": 22.352} code: this crate: the FHWA table is by facility type, which a lane does not carry; lanes posted at 50 mph or more take the freeway rows, the rest the arterial rows

Equations.

  • gap control: a = (k_p·e + k_d·ė)/0.1 s, e = g − h·v, ė = v_p(V2X) − v − h·a
  • speed control: a = −0.4·(v − v0) when g/v > 2 s, e > 1.66 m
  • degradation: no BSM/CAM from the radar target younger than coop.max_age_s (generation time) → the ACC law (mobility/car-following/acc), its time gap opening from the cooperative one to 1.1 s over degrade_ramp_s after the last message went stale

Assumptions.

  • The radar measures the true net gap and the true range rate; only the cooperative terms come from V2X.
  • A message is associated with the radar target by its claimed position (crate::coop), never by the sender's simulation identity.

Limitations.

  • No platoon management protocol (join, leave, split): a platoon is any chain of cooperative vehicles each associated with its predecessor.
  • The controller state is the previous applied acceleration; the engine's own jerk limit may differ from the law's command.

Sources.

  • paper: Milanés & Shladover 2014, Modeling cooperative and autonomous adaptive cruise control dynamic responses using experimental data, Transportation Research C 48:285-300 (DOI 10.1016/j.trc.2014.09.001)
  • paper: Milanés & Shladover 2014, Modeling cooperative and autonomous adaptive cruise control dynamic responses using experimental data, Transportation Research C 48:285-300 (DOI 10.1016/j.trc.2014.09.001)
  • paper: Xiao, Wang & van Arem 2017, Realistic car-following models for microscopic simulation of adaptive and cooperative adaptive cruise control vehicles, Transportation Research Record 2623:1-9 (DOI 10.3141/2623-01)
  • paper: Xiao, Wang, Schakel & van Arem 2018, Unravelling effects of cooperative adaptive cruise control deactivation on traffic flow characteristics at merging bottlenecks, Transportation Research C 96:380-397 (eq. 5, the dynamic margin d0)
  • code: SUMO src/microsim/cfmodels/MSCFModel_ACC.cpp and MSCFModel_CACC.cpp (EPL-2.0, read only, never vendored): gains, thresholds, override

mobility/car-following/cacc-path¶

Platooning by the PATH leader-predecessor controller: constant 5 m spacing held with both the predecessor's and the platoon leader's speed and acceleration as received over V2X; degrades to ACC when either is lost or late.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

xi 1 1.0 code: Plexe defaults caccC1 = 0.5, caccXi = 1, caccOmegaN = 0.2, caccSpacing = 5 m, engine lag 0.5 s. secondary: Plexe's documented defaults as recalled
omega_n rad/s 0.2 code: Plexe defaults caccC1 = 0.5, caccXi = 1, caccOmegaN = 0.2, caccSpacing = 5 m, engine lag 0.5 s. secondary: Plexe's documented defaults as recalled
spacing m 5.0 code: Plexe defaults caccC1 = 0.5, caccXi = 1, caccOmegaN = 0.2, caccSpacing = 5 m, engine lag 0.5 s. secondary: Plexe's documented defaults as recalled
actuator_lag s 0.5 code: Plexe defaults caccC1 = 0.5, caccXi = 1, caccOmegaN = 0.2, caccSpacing = 5 m, engine lag 0.5 s. secondary: Plexe's documented defaults as recalled

mobility/car-following/cacc-ploeg¶

Cooperative adaptive cruise control with acceleration feed-forward (Ploeg et al. 2011): the predecessor's received acceleration enters the controller directly, so a 0.7 s gap is string stable while messages arrive and the vehicle degrades to ACC when they do not.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source

weather.sight_distance - {"deceleration_mps2": 3.4, "perception_reaction_s": 2.5} standard: AASHTO A Policy on Geometric Design of Highways and Streets (2018) §3.2.2, Eq. 3-2: stopping sight distance d = v*t + v^2/(2a); the desired speed is capped at the v that stops within the visibility
weather.road_context - {"freeway_rows_at_or_above_mps": 22.352} code: this crate: the FHWA table is by facility type, which a lane does not carry; lanes posted at 50 mph or more take the freeway rows, the rest the arterial rows

Equations.

  • controller: h·u̇ = −u + k_p·e + k_d·ė + u_p(V2X), e = g − h·v, ė = v_p − v − h·a
  • degradation: no BSM/CAM from the radar target younger than coop.max_age_s (generation time) → the ACC law (mobility/car-following/acc), its time gap opening from the cooperative one to 1.1 s over degrade_ramp_s after the last message went stale

Assumptions.

  • The radar measures the true net gap and the true range rate; only the cooperative terms come from V2X.
  • A message is associated with the radar target by its claimed position (crate::coop), never by the sender's simulation identity.

Limitations.

  • No platoon management protocol (join, leave, split): a platoon is any chain of cooperative vehicles each associated with its predecessor.
  • The controller state is the previous applied acceleration; the engine's own jerk limit may differ from the law's command.

Sources.

  • paper: Ploeg, Scheepers, van Nunen, van de Wouw & Nijmeijer 2011, Design and experimental evaluation of cooperative adaptive cruise control, IEEE ITSC 2011, 260-265 (DOI 10.1109/ITSC.2011.6082981)
  • paper: Milanés & Shladover 2014, Modeling cooperative and autonomous adaptive cruise control dynamic responses using experimental data, Transportation Research C 48:285-300 (DOI 10.1016/j.trc.2014.09.001)
  • paper: Xiao, Wang & van Arem 2017, Realistic car-following models for microscopic simulation of adaptive and cooperative adaptive cruise control vehicles, Transportation Research Record 2623:1-9 (DOI 10.3141/2623-01)
  • paper: Xiao, Wang, Schakel & van Arem 2018, Unravelling effects of cooperative adaptive cruise control deactivation on traffic flow characteristics at merging bottlenecks, Transportation Research C 96:380-397 (eq. 5, the dynamic margin d0)
  • code: SUMO src/microsim/cfmodels/MSCFModel_ACC.cpp and MSCFModel_CACC.cpp (EPL-2.0, read only, never vendored): gains, thresholds, override

mobility/car-following/eidm¶

Longitudinal acceleration by the Extended IDM: the improved IDM, blended with the constant-acceleration heuristic, with a jerk-bounded rise in acceleration.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Equations.

  • improved IDM: z = s*/s; v≤v0: a(1−z²) if z≥1 else a_free(1 − z^(2a/a_free)); v>v0: a_free + a(1−z²) if z≥1 else a_free
  • CAH blend: a = (1−c)·a_IIDM + c·[a_CAH + b·tanh((a_IIDM − a_CAH)/b)] when a_IIDM < a_CAH
  • jerk bound: a ≤ a_prev + j_max·Δt (2·j_max while a_prev + j_max·Δt < 0)

Limitations.

  • Leaves out the EIDM's reaction-time action points, estimation errors and tanh drive-off profile: each needs per-vehicle memory the CarFollowing trait does not carry.

Sources.

  • paper: Salles, Kaufmann & Reuss 2020, Extending the Intelligent Driver Model in SUMO and Verifying the Drive Off Trajectories with Aerial Measurements, SUMO User Conference 2020, EPiC Series in Computing 72
  • paper: Treiber & Kesting 2013, Traffic Flow Dynamics, Springer, §11.3.7 (the improved IDM). secondary: the book as recalled and quoted, not re-read
  • code: SUMO src/microsim/cfmodels/MSCFModel_EIDM.cpp (EPL-2.0, read only): jerkmax = 3 m/s³, ClutchEngageSpeed = 0.5 m/s, c_coolness = 0.99

mobility/car-following/gipps¶

Longitudinal acceleration by Gipps's model: the lower of a free-acceleration speed and the highest speed from which the vehicle can stop behind a leader that brakes as hard as the follower expects. The car-following model of Aimsun.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

weather.sight_distance - {"deceleration_mps2": 3.4, "perception_reaction_s": 2.5} standard: AASHTO A Policy on Geometric Design of Highways and Streets (2018) §3.2.2, Eq. 3-2: stopping sight distance d = v*t + v^2/(2a); the desired speed is capped at the v that stops within the visibility
weather.road_context - {"freeway_rows_at_or_above_mps": 22.352} code: this crate: the FHWA table is by facility type, which a lane does not carry; lanes posted at 50 mph or more take the freeway rows, the rest the arterial rows

Equations.

  • free: v_a = v + 2.5·a·τ·(1 − v/V)·√(0.025 + v/V)
  • safe: v_b = −B·τ + √(B²τ² + B·[2(g − s0) − v·τ + v_l²/B̂]), B̂ = max(3, (B + 3)/2)
  • acceleration: a_out = (max(0, min(v_a, v_b)) − v)/τ

Assumptions.

  • Heavy classes keep their SUMO accel with Gipps's b = 2a: the paper calibrated one population (a choice).
  • s0 is the effective size less the class's body length, at least 1 m.

Limitations.

  • Parameter means are secondary-sourced; no per-driver distribution is drawn by the model itself (heterogeneity arrives through DriverProfile).

Sources.

  • paper: Gipps 1981, A behavioural car-following model for computer simulation, Transportation Research B 15(2):105-111, eqs. (1)-(5) and §4's parameter distributions. secondary: equations and parameter means as reproduced in Treiber & Kesting 2013 and the Aimsun model documentation; the paper was not re-read

mobility/car-following/idm¶

Longitudinal acceleration from the ego's speed, the net gap to the leader and the speed difference, by the Intelligent Driver Model. Every stopping rule in the crate — red signals, yielding at an unsignalised junction, curve-speed caps — is expressed as a virtual leader, so this one equation produces every deceleration a vehicle applies.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
preset - kesting-2010 paper: Kesting, Treiber & Helbing 2010, Phil. Trans. R. Soc. A 368, 4585 (arXiv:0912.3613), car and truck columns

weather.sight_distance - {"deceleration_mps2": 3.4, "perception_reaction_s": 2.5} standard: AASHTO A Policy on Geometric Design of Highways and Streets (2018) §3.2.2, Eq. 3-2: stopping sight distance d = v*t + v^2/(2a); the desired speed is capped at the v that stops within the visibility
weather.road_context - {"freeway_rows_at_or_above_mps": 22.352} code: this crate: the FHWA table is by facility type, which a lane does not carry; lanes posted at 50 mph or more take the freeway rows, the rest the arterial rows

Equations.

  • acceleration: a = a_max · [ 1 − (v/v0)^δ − (s*(v,Δv)/s)² ] s is the net gap (leader rear to ego front); on a free road the second interaction term is absent and a = a_max(1 − (v/v0)^δ)
  • desired minimum gap: s*(v,Δv) = s0 + s1·√(v/v0) + max(0, T·v + v·Δv / (2·√(a_max·b))) s1 = 0 in the base model (Treiber 2000 sets it so). The max(0, ·) is clamp_s_star: s* is a desired minimum gap and the equation reads it only as (s*/s)², so an unfloored negative value would brake a vehicle whose leader is pulling away
  • weather: v0 ← desired_speed_factor · min(v0_driver, v_limit); T ← headway_factor · T; b ← min(b, decel_cap) the order is a design choice recorded here: the legal limit and the driver's wish are reconciled first, then the weather slows the result

Assumptions.

  • One leader, no lateral coupling, instantaneous reaction.
  • The cited sets have a car column and a truck column, so the twelve vehicle classes of §2.7 fold onto the two; IdmPreset::profile documents the fold.
  • Between mobility steps, positions follow the published constant-velocity extrapolation rule, not this model's acceleration.

Limitations.

  • Known over-reaction to a cut-in without the Kesting 2010 enhancement.
  • clamp_s_star is true. With it false the interaction term of s* goes negative once the leader is T·2·√(a_max·b) faster — 5.0 m/s for the Kesting 2010 car, whatever the ego's own speed — s* is a negative distance past (s0 + T·v)·2·√(a_max·b)/v (6.4 m/s at v = 5 m/s), and past (2·s0 + T·v)·2·√(a_max·b)/v (7.7 m/s at v = 5 m/s) a faster leader produces more braking rather than less, down to −0.34 m/s² at v = 5 m/s behind a leader 25 m ahead at 30 m/s. It is true in every shipped preset for that reason.
  • No reaction time and no driver imperfection: both are the high tier's business (SUMO's sigma and action-step length).

Ignores.

  • Driver imperfection (sigma), action step length and sub-second reaction models (medium relative to high).

Sources.

  • paper: Kesting, Treiber & Helbing 2010, Phil. Trans. R. Soc. A 368, 4585 (arXiv:0912.3613), car and truck columns

mobility/car-following/krauss¶

Longitudinal acceleration by Krauß's stochastic, collision-free model: drive at the highest speed from which the vehicle can still stop behind a braking leader, less a random dawdle. SUMO's default car-following model.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium; hosting in-process; draws random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
sigma 1 0.5 [0.0, 1.0] code: SUMO src/microsim/cfmodels/MSCFModel_Krauss.cpp (EPL-2.0, read only, never vendored): dawdle2 and the vType defaults tau = 1 s, sigma = 0.5
tau s 1.0 code: SUMO src/microsim/cfmodels/MSCFModel_Krauss.cpp (EPL-2.0, read only, never vendored): dawdle2 and the vType defaults tau = 1 s, sigma = 0.5
a m/s² {"car": 2.6, "truck": 1.3} standard: SUMO vType defaults per vClass, crate::classes::VehicleClass::spec
b m/s² {"car": 4.5, "truck": 4.0} standard: SUMO vType defaults per vClass, crate::classes::VehicleClass::spec
s0 m {"car": 2.5, "truck": 2.5} standard: SUMO vType defaults per vClass, crate::classes::VehicleClass::spec

weather.heavy_intensity_threshold 1 0.5 [0.0, 1.0] todo-calibrate: the intensity at which the FHWA "light" rows give way to the "heavy" rows (calibration pending)
weather.surface_friction 1 {"dry": null, "flooded": 0.4, "ice": 0.1, "snow": 0.25, "wet": 0.5} paper: Fricke, Traffic Accident Reconstruction, Northwestern University Traffic Institute, 1990: tyre-pavement friction ranges (wet 0.4-0.6, packed snow 0.2-0.3, ice 0.05-0.15). secondary: midpoints of ranges quoted in reconstruction practice, not re-verified against a primary friction survey. The deceleration cap is mu*g; a dry road is not capped
weather.sight_distance - {"deceleration_mps2": 3.4, "perception_reaction_s": 2.5} standard: AASHTO A Policy on Geometric Design of Highways and Streets (2018) §3.2.2, Eq. 3-2: stopping sight distance d = v*t + v^2/(2a); the desired speed is capped at the v that stops within the visibility
weather.road_context - {"freeway_rows_at_or_above_mps": 22.352} code: this crate: the FHWA table is by facility type, which a lane does not carry; lanes posted at 50 mph or more take the freeway rows, the rest the arterial rows

Equations.

  • safe speed: v_safe = v_l + (g − v_l·τ) / ((v + v_l)/(2b) + τ), g = net gap − s0
  • desired speed: v_des = min(v + a·Δt, v0, v_safe)
  • dawdle: v' = max(0, v_des − σ·Δt·η·(v_des if v_des < a else a)), η ~ U[0,1)
  • acceleration: a_out = (v' − v)/Δt, clamped to [−b_emergency, a]

Assumptions.

  • The leader brakes no harder than the follower's own b (Krauß's symmetric assumption).
  • τ is the driver's time_headway_s, so weather and per-driver heterogeneity reach it.

Limitations.

  • Dawdling makes single-lane capacity lower and stop-and-go waves stronger than the IDM's; set sigma = 0 for a deterministic variant.
  • The thesis was not re-read: the safe speed is as SUMO documents it.

Sources.

  • paper: Krauß 1998, Microscopic Modeling of Traffic Flow: Investigation of Collision Free Vehicle Dynamics, PhD thesis, Univ. Köln (DLR-Forschungsbericht 98-08). secondary: the safe-velocity expression as SUMO's MSCFModel_KraussOrig1 and Krajzewicz et al. 2012 state it; the thesis itself was not re-read
  • code: SUMO src/microsim/cfmodels/MSCFModel_Krauss.cpp (EPL-2.0, read only, never vendored): dawdle2 and the vType defaults tau = 1 s, sigma = 0.5

mobility/car-following/w99¶

Longitudinal acceleration by Wiedemann's 1999 psycho-physical model: perception thresholds in the gap/speed-difference plane switch between free driving, approaching, following and emergency braking. The car-following model of PTV Vissim.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium; hosting in-process; draws random numbers.

The deceleration cap is mu*g; a dry road is not capped
weather.sight_distance - {"deceleration_mps2": 3.4, "perception_reaction_s": 2.5} standard: AASHTO A Policy on Geometric Design of Highways and Streets (2018) §3.2.2, Eq. 3-2: stopping sight distance d = v*t + v^2/(2a); the desired speed is capped at the v that stops within the visibility
weather.road_context - {"freeway_rows_at_or_above_mps": 22.352} code: this crate: the FHWA table is by facility type, which a lane does not carry; lanes posted at 50 mph or more take the freeway rows, the rest the arterial rows

Equations.

  • thresholds: sdxc = CC0 + CC1·v_slow; sdxo = sdxc + CC2; sdxv = sdxo + CC3(Δv − CC4); sdv = CC6·dx²/1e4; sdvc = CC4 − sdv; sdvo = sdv + CC5
  • ceiling: a_max(v) = CC8 + (CC9 − CC8)·min(v, 80 km/h)/(80 km/h)

Limitations.

  • Wiedemann's own 1999 equations are unpublished; this is the open executable reading (w99-demo / SUMO), with the acceleration ceiling interpolated between CC8 and CC9 as Vissim's manual defines them rather than w99-demo's unbounded sum.
  • Known to violate the standstill gap occasionally (SUMO sets collisionMinGapFactor 0.1 for it); the one-step guard of SUMO issue #10472 is kept.

Sources.

  • standard: PTV Vissim user manual, Wiedemann 99 car-following parameters CC0-CC9 (defaults). secondary: the default table as reproduced across the Vissim calibration literature; the manual was not re-read
  • code: w99-demo (glgh, 2016, MIT) as adopted by SUMO src/microsim/cfmodels/MSCFModel_W99.cpp (EPL-2.0, read only, 2026-10-07): the regime thresholds and accelerations

mobility/demand/od-gravity¶

Where a trip starts and ends: uniform or hop-decayed (gravity) destinations, optional perimeter origins, and the rush profile's commute-to-core bias.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
od_model - uniform code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L1741-1743 and roads.py random_trip
od_gravity_scale hops 2.0 code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L1741-1743 and roads.py random_trip
boundary_origins - false code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L1741-1743 and roads.py random_trip
rush_centre_bias 1 probability m(f) − 0.2 code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L1741-1743 and roads.py random_trip
boundary_margin_fraction 1 0.05 code: v2xw-mobility: this crate's geometric definition of the perimeter, a band of this fraction of the world's shorter side. the legacy engine defined the perimeter per topology in roads.py; a geometric band is topology-independent and is recorded here as this crate's choice
max_hops hops 12 code: an engineering bound on the gravity BFS: beyond 6·scale the weight is under 0.3 % of the nearest lane's
classes - ["car", "truck", "bus", "moto", "emergency"] code: : a lane carries the class mask that may use it

Equations.

  • gravity weight: w(h) = exp(−h / od_gravity_scale) h is the number of junction hops from the origin

Assumptions.

  • A trip's origin and destination are lanes the class may use and whose kind is for motor traffic, so a car is never given a sidewalk to start on.
  • One draw for the origin, one for the centre-bias coin and one for the destination, whichever law is in force.

Limitations.

  • Hops are counted on the junction graph in the direction of travel, so a destination reachable only against the flow has weight zero.

Trip arrivals as a thinned Poisson process: candidates at a boosted constant rate, kept with the probability the time-of-day shape gives, which is an exact simulation of the inhomogeneous process.

speed_law - {"law": "class-speed-dev"} code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L1705-1743 (demand_mult, the thinning loop and the trip-speed draw)
wall_clock_mapping - f = t / duration todo-calibrate: when t0 is a wall-clock date, §2.4 replaces f by clock hour / 24 so the rush peaks fall at 06:00 and 18:00 (calibration pending)
od - uniform code: the origin-destination model, carded separately as mobility/demand/od-gravity

Equations.

  • candidate process: inter-arrival ~ Exp(arrival_rate · cand_boost)
  • thinning: keep with probability m(f) / cand_boost, f = t / duration exact for the inhomogeneous process of rate arrival_rate·m(f) whenever cand_boost ≥ sup m

Assumptions.

  • The arrival cursor is model state, so the draw sequence does not depend on how the caller chopped time into windows (invariant I-M2).
  • Role coins (attacker, faulty, colluder) are drawn in the threat layer, not here.

Limitations.

  • A candidate boost below the shape's supremum under-represents the peaks; DemandProfile::supremum is what a scenario should set it from.

mobility/demand/tr36885-drop¶

The 3GPP evaluation drops: one instantaneous placement of vehicles along every drivable lane, spaced by the cited TR 36.885 or TR 37.885 law, for radio validation runs that must reproduce a published configuration.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
variant - tr36885 standard: 3GPP TR 36.885 Table A.1.2-1 [R2c]
mean_gap_time s 2.5 standard: 3GPP TR 36.885 Table A.1.2-1 [R2c]
min_gap m 0.0 standard: 3GPP TR 37.885 §6.1.2 [R2c]
speeds km/h {"selected": "tr36885-freeway-140", "tr36885_freeway": [70.0, 140.0], "tr3688... standard: 3GPP TR 37.885 §6.1.2 [R2c]
option_c_platoon - {"gap_m": 2.0, "size": 6, "type": "type-3"} standard: 3GPP TR 37.885 §6.1.2 [R2c]
validation_densities veh/km [{"density_veh_km": 50.0, "source": "todisco"}, {"density_veh_km": 100.0, "so... paper: Todisco et al. and Molina-Masegosa & Gozalvez, the C-V2X validation densities of R2d

Equations.

  • TR 36.885 spacing: gap ~ Exp(mean = 2.5 s × speed) spatial Poisson along the lane
  • TR 37.885 spacing: gap = max{2 m, Exp(mean = 2 s × speed)} bumper to bumper

Assumptions.

  • A drop is instantaneous: every vehicle exists from at, and the model produces nothing afterwards.
  • Each lane's gaps come from that lane's own RNG stream, so adding a lane to the world cannot change another lane's placement.
  • A per-lane speed table repeats when the world has more lanes than the TR specifies; the TRs fix the lane count and a wider road must reuse the table rather than invent an entry.

Limitations.

  • The drop places vehicles on the lanes the world has; it does not build the TR's own highway or urban geometry, which is 's business.

Sources.

  • standard: 3GPP TR 36.885 Table A.1.2-1 [R2c]

mobility/intersection/gap-acceptance-hcm¶

Right of way at an unsignalised junction by gap acceptance: a vehicle on the minor stream enters when the time gap to the nearest conflicting claimant it must give way to exceeds the critical gap for its movement, and vehicles queued behind it each need a follow-up time on top.

version 1.2.0; tiers medium; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source

standing_speed_mps m/s 0.1 code: this crate: at or below this speed a claimant closes no gap but still holds a claim, which is what breaks the cyclic give-way-to-the-right priority a four-way crossing produces. The reference engine has no junction stopped test to cite — it ordered claimants by (distance, vehicle id) with no conflict matrix (legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L2415-2440). the value is the 0.1 m/s desired-speed floor of the same engine's IDM port (max(0.1, v0), run.py L2276), reused so "not moving" is one number in this crate rather than two
legacy_closest_first - true code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L2415-2440, L2466-2470. the closest-first rule and the 2 m stop-line offset
patience_critical_gap_cv - 0.0 paper: SUMO P1 as re-specified and 00-adoption-plan. 15-0.2), impatience = waiting / time-to-impatience; accepted gaps shrink with waiting (Polus, Lazar & Livneh 2003, J. Transp. Eng. 129(2); Kittelson & Vandehey 1991). Re-implemented from the documented behaviour. SUMO was read for behaviour only and no code was copied. The cv and the floor are this crate's choices inside the quoted ranges, not field-calibrated
patience_time_to_impatience s 0.0 code: SUMO option --time-to-impatience, default 180 s (documented default); passenger vType decel 4.5 m/s². constants only, with a citation; no code read
patience_floor_ratio - 0.7 paper: SUMO P1 as re-specified and 00-adoption-plan. 15-0.2), impatience = waiting / time-to-impatience; accepted gaps shrink with waiting (Polus, Lazar & Livneh 2003, J. Transp. Eng. 129(2); Kittelson & Vandehey 1991). Re-implemented from the documented behaviour. SUMO was read for behaviour only and no code was copied. The cv and the floor are this crate's choices inside the quoted ranges, not field-calibrated
patience_foe_braking_decel m/s² 0.0 code: SUMO option --time-to-impatience, default 180 s (documented default); passenger vType decel 4.5 m/s². constants only, with a citation; no code read

Equations.

  • acceptance: enter ⟺ min_over_conflicting(t_to_stop_line) ≥ t_c + k·t_f k is the number of vehicles of the ego's own movement queued ahead of it; the composition t_c + k·t_f is the standard reading of the two-value model and is an assumption of this card, not a quoted formula

Assumptions.

  • Priority is a strict total order — conflicting claimant, then distance to the stop line, then actor id — so the yield relation is acyclic and the closest vehicle at every junction always makes progress.
  • A standing claimant closes no gap, so an all-way stop discharges rather than deadlocking — but it does hold a claim: among the ego and every conflicting claimant that is also standing, only the smallest (distance to the stop line, actor id) enters. That is a local minimum of a strict total order, so it is acyclic whatever the world's conflict matrix says pairwise, and it is what stops a cyclic give-way-to-the-right matrix discharging every claimant into the junction in the same step.
  • Whether the ego is on the minor stream is read from the world's conflict matrix (ego_must_yield), not guessed from geometry.

Limitations.

  • The critical-gap table is secondary (§2.3): every value here was read from a help page reproducing the HCM, not from the HCM.

Ignores.

  • Probabilistic right-of-way violation and pedestrian crossing gaps (medium relative to high). Impatience growth and the per-driver critical gap are parameters (patience_*), off by default.

Sources.

  • standard: Highway Capacity Manual critical gaps and follow-up times, as reproduced by the PTV VISUM help []. secondary: §2.3 flags the whole table, because the HCM and FHWA PDFs did not parse and the values were read from a help page that reproduces them

(2016) ch. 31 / 7th ed. ch. 19 permitted left-turn capacity: 2 sneakers per cycle complete the turn at the end of the permissive green (secondary: the commonly summarised value). 0 is off. Sneakers here start from the stop line, so only those that cross it before the red go
