Transp. Eng. 129(2); Kittelson & Vandehey 1991). Re-implemented from the documented behaviour. SUMO was read for behaviour only and no code was copied. The cv and the floor are this crate's choices inside the quoted ranges, not field-calibrated
patience_time_to_impatience s 0.0 code: SUMO option --time-to-impatience, default 180 s (documented default); passenger vType decel 4.5 m/s². constants only, with a citation; no code read
patience_floor_ratio - 0.7 paper: SUMO P1 as re-specified and 00-adoption-plan. 15-0.2), impatience = waiting / time-to-impatience; accepted gaps shrink with waiting (Polus, Lazar & Livneh 2003, J. Transp. Eng. 129(2); Kittelson & Vandehey 1991). Re-implemented from the documented behaviour. SUMO was read for behaviour only and no code was copied. The cv and the floor are this crate's choices inside the quoted ranges, not field-calibrated
patience_foe_braking_decel m/s² 0.0 code: SUMO option --time-to-impatience, default 180 s (documented default); passenger vType decel 4.5 m/s². constants only, with a citation; no code read

Equations.

  • phase: into = (t − offset) mod cycle; the phase is the first whose cumulative duration exceeds into % on f64 is exact in IEEE-754, so two engines agree on a phase boundary to the last bit (build decision D10)
  • yellow interval: y = clamp(t_react + v/(2·a), 3 s, 6 s) the ITE formula of §2.3 with the grade term G = 0 (flat); the constants are secondary
  • amber decision: stop ⟺ v²/(2a) ≤ gap to the stop line the dilemma zone the yellow interval is sized for

Assumptions.

  • Pre-timed, no actuation.
  • The generated plan splits movements into two phase groups by the axis of their approach, which is the two-phase plan netconvert generates for a crossroads.
  • The yellow interval assumes a flat grade (G = 0 in the ITE formula).
  • A permitted left turn shows GreenYield and gives way by the HCM signalised procedure's permitted left-turn critical headway (4.5 s).
  • A right turn on red, where the jurisdiction permits it, stops fully within 3 m of the line and then takes the HCM minor-street right-turn critical gap.

Ignores.

  • Actuated and coordinated control, and SUMO's request conflict evaluation inside the junction (medium relative to high).

Sources.

  • standard: FHWA Signal Timing Manual 2008 Ch. 5 (yellow 3-6 s guidance; ITE formula y = t + v/(2a + 2Gg) with t ≈ 1 s, a ≈ 10 ft/s²). secondary, not re-verified against the primary document

mobility/intersection/two-coloring-legacy¶

The abstract tier's intersection control: a stable 2-colouring of the junction graph, each axis green for half a 24 s cycle offset by the junction's colour, vehicles halting 2 m before the line and a 6 m/s cap through a bend. Kept as the parity oracle for a run against the frozen reference corpus.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
light_cycle s 24.0 code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L2267-2272, L2466-2470, L2479-2483. the frozen reference engine's signal abstraction
turn_speed m/s 6.0 code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L2267-2272, L2466-2470, L2479-2483. the frozen reference engine's signal abstraction
turn_min_angle deg 40.0 code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L2267-2272, L2466-2470, L2479-2483. the frozen reference engine's signal abstraction
cap_slight_turns - false code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L2267-2272, L2466-2470, L2479-2483. the frozen reference engine's signal abstraction
stop_line_offset m 2.0 code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L2267-2272, L2466-2470, L2479-2483. the frozen reference engine's signal abstraction

Equations.

  • green: offset = (phase mod 2)·half; east-west green ⟺ floor((t + offset)/half) is even, half = cycle/2 arithmetic only — no transcendental, so it is exact
  • colouring: breadth-first 2-colouring of the junction graph, junctions visited in id order on a grid this equals (i + j) mod 2

Assumptions.

  • The colouring is breadth-first and therefore a proper 2-colouring exactly on a bipartite topology; see the module documentation.
  • The bend threshold is applied through the world's TurnDirection bands rather than to a measured angle, because that is the discretisation the world carries; cap_slight_turns exposes the one band that straddles 40°.

Limitations.

  • No yellow, no all-red, no actuation, no pedestrians — every one of them is something §2.3 says this tier ignores.
  • On a non-bipartite junction graph some adjacent pair shares a colour, because no proper 2-colouring exists.

Ignores.

  • Everything the medium tier's signal and gap-acceptance models do.

Sources.

  • code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py

mobility/kinematic/lane-follow¶

The abstract tier: every actor follows its route at the smaller of its desired speed and the lane's limit, stops for nothing, and despawns when the route ends. Its trip length and time are exactly the route's, which is what makes it the baseline the medium tier's interaction is measured against.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
classes - ["car", "truck", "bus", "moto", "emergency"] code: : "Each actor follows its route at min(desired_speed, lane speed limit), stops for nothing, despawns at trip end"
max_lifetime s null code: an optional bound on a run's memory; unlimited by default

Equations.

  • speed: v = min(desired_speed, lane.speed_limit); s ← s + v·Δt

Assumptions.

  • Speed changes instantly at a lane boundary: there is no acceleration at all.
  • Kinematics are published in the same frame and at the same reference point as every other tier (invariant I-M4).

Limitations.

  • Vehicles pass through one another and through red lights, because neither is modelled.

Ignores.

  • Leader interaction, signals, gap acceptance, lane changes and weather — every one of them relative to the medium tier.

Sources.

  • code: : "Each actor follows its route at min(desired_speed, lane speed limit), stops for nothing, despawns at trip end"

mobility/lane-change/mobil¶

Discretionary lane changes by MOBIL: a safety criterion on the acceleration the change imposes on the new follower, and an incentive criterion that weighs the ego's own gain against a politeness-weighted sum of the two followers' losses. Every acceleration comes from the car-following model this instance was built with, evaluated with each vehicle's own driver parameters.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium; hosting in-process; draws random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
preset - kesting-2007 paper: Kesting, Treiber & Helbing 2007, Transportation Research Record 1999, 86-94 (DOI 10.3141/1999-10)
car_following - mobility/car-following/idm paper: Kesting 2007: MOBIL is defined on top of a car-following model
politeness 1 0.2 [-1.0, 1.0] todo-calibrate: Kesting 2007 sweeps p from 0 to 1 and states no single default; 0.2 is the reference engine's value (lane_change_politeness) (calibration pending)
threshold m/s² 0.1 paper: Kesting, Treiber & Helbing 2007, Transportation Research Record 1999, 86-94 (DOI 10.3141/1999-10)
b_safe m/s² 4.0 paper: Kesting, Treiber & Helbing 2007, Transportation Research Record 1999, 86-94 (DOI 10.3141/1999-10)
right_bias m/s² 0.3 paper: Kesting, Treiber & Helbing 2007, Transportation Research Record 1999, 86-94 (DOI 10.3141/1999-10)
asymmetric - false paper: Kesting, Treiber & Helbing 2007, Transportation Research Record 1999, 86-94 (DOI 10.3141/1999-10)
min_speed m/s 3.0 code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L2251-2258, L2306-2401. the reference engine's MOBIL, transition and cooldown
require_blocked_leader - false code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L2251-2258, L2306-2401. the reference engine's MOBIL, transition and cooldown
transition s 2.5 code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L2251-2258, L2306-2401. the reference engine's MOBIL, transition and cooldown
max_heading_dev deg 12.0 code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L2251-2258, L2306-2401. the reference engine's MOBIL, transition and cooldown
cooldown_factor 1 2.0 code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L2251-2258, L2306-2401. the reference engine's MOBIL, transition and cooldown
reconsider_rate 1/s 0.25 code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L2251-2258, L2306-2401. the reference engine's MOBIL, transition and cooldown
step s 0.1 code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L2251-2258, L2306-2401. the reference engine's MOBIL, transition and cooldown

Equations.

  • safety: ã_n ≥ −b_safe the new follower's acceleration after the change
  • incentive: (ã_c − a_c) + p·[(ã_n − a_n) + (ã_o − a_o)] + Δa_bias > Δa_th c ego, n new follower, o old follower. Δa_bias sits on the LEFT, with the gain: it is zero unless the asymmetric rule is enabled, and then it is +right_bias for a change to the right and −right_bias for one to the left, so the bias makes moving right EASIER — the keep-right rule of Kesting 2007. Printed on the right against the threshold it would read the other way round.
  • lateral transition: d(t) = d0 + (d1 − d0)·p²(3 − 2p), p = elapsed/T, T = max(T_lc, 1.5·Δ/(tan(dev_max)·max(v, 0.5))) smoothstep, so the lateral velocity is zero at both ends; the stretch keeps the peak lateral velocity 1.5·Δ/T inside the heading-deviation cap

Assumptions.

  • The change is instantaneous for the incentive test and is then integrated laterally over the transition.
  • Adjacency is within one edge, and an edge is one direction of travel, so a 'neighbour' is never an oncoming vehicle.
  • The cooldown and the lateral transition are actor state and live in the mobility engine; this model is stateless.

Limitations.

  • No cooperative or strategic (route-driven) changes: a vehicle that needs another lane to follow its route is served by the engine's mandatory-change rule, not by this incentive.

Ignores.

  • SUMO's cooperative helping, keep-right rule, speed-gain lookahead and sublane model (medium relative to high).

Sources.

  • paper: Kesting, Treiber & Helbing 2007, Transportation Research Record 1999, 86-94 (DOI 10.3141/1999-10)

mobility/lateral/sublane-sl2015¶

Where across its lane a vehicle drives: the lateral position with the highest expected speed among those that keep a lawful clearance to vehicles alongside, reached at a bounded lateral speed. With it, a car passes a cyclist within a wide lane, cyclists ride side by side with traffic, and motorcycles filter where the jurisdiction allows it.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
resolution m 0.4 code: SUMO src/microsim/lcmodels/MSLCM_SL2015.cpp (EPL-2.0, read only): updateExpectedSublaneSpeeds, forecastAverageSpeed, lcSpeedGainLookahead = 5 s, lcSpeedGainRight = 0.1; vType defaults maxSpeedLat = 1.0 m/s, minGapLat = 0.6 m
max_speed_lat m/s 1.0 code: SUMO src/microsim/lcmodels/MSLCM_SL2015.cpp (EPL-2.0, read only): updateExpectedSublaneSpeeds, forecastAverageSpeed, lcSpeedGainLookahead = 5 s, lcSpeedGainRight = 0.1; vType defaults maxSpeedLat = 1.0 m/s, minGapLat = 0.6 m
speed_gain_lookahead s 5.0 code: SUMO src/microsim/lcmodels/MSLCM_SL2015.cpp (EPL-2.0, read only): updateExpectedSublaneSpeeds, forecastAverageSpeed, lcSpeedGainLookahead = 5 s, lcSpeedGainRight = 0.1; vType defaults maxSpeedLat = 1.0 m/s, minGapLat = 0.6 m
speed_gain_threshold m/s 0.1 code: SUMO src/microsim/lcmodels/MSLCM_SL2015.cpp (EPL-2.0, read only): updateExpectedSublaneSpeeds, forecastAverageSpeed, lcSpeedGainLookahead = 5 s, lcSpeedGainRight = 0.1; vType defaults maxSpeedLat = 1.0 m/s, minGapLat = 0.6 m
lookahead m 100.0 code: the neighbour window; at least SL2015's 5 s lookahead at urban speeds
min_gap_lat m 0.6 code: SUMO src/microsim/lcmodels/MSLCM_SL2015.cpp (EPL-2.0, read only): updateExpectedSublaneSpeeds, forecastAverageSpeed, lcSpeedGainLookahead = 5 s, lcSpeedGainRight = 0.1; vType defaults maxSpeedLat = 1.0 m/s, minGapLat = 0.6 m
bicycle_passing_gap m 0.9144 standard: California Vehicle Code §21760 (Three Feet for Safety Act, 2014): a motorist passes a cyclist in the same direction at no less than three feet; most US states have the same rule
motorcycles_share - false standard: the jurisdiction's filtering rule (crate::rules): false unless both the scenario and the rules allow it
bicycles_share - true standard: Uniform Vehicle Code §11-1205: a cyclist rides as near the right side of the roadway as practicable, sharing the lane

Equations.

  • expected speed: v_exp(c) = min(v_max, min over leaders overlapping c of v_safe'); v_safe' = (t_c·v_safe + (2L − t_c)·v_l)/(2L) when t_c = gap/(v_max − v_l) < L
  • choice: argmax over feasible c of v_exp(c); leave the preferred position only for a gain above the threshold; ties: nearest preferred, nearest current, rightmost
  • motion: |dc/dt| ≤ maxSpeedLat

Limitations.

  • No expected-speed memory, no strategic or cooperative sub-lane motivation, no lcPushy/lcImpatience, no leaders on the next lane: SL2015's lateral choice reduced to its speed-gain core.
  • Moves vehicles within their lane only; lane changes remain MOBIL's.

Sources.

  • paper: Semrau & Erdmann 2016, Simulation framework for testing ADAS in Chinese traffic situations, SUMO 2016 - Traffic, Mobility, and Logistics, pp. 103-115 (the SL2015 sub-lane model)
  • code: SUMO src/microsim/lcmodels/MSLCM_SL2015.cpp (EPL-2.0, read only): updateExpectedSublaneSpeeds, forecastAverageSpeed, lcSpeedGainLookahead = 5 s, lcSpeedGainRight = 0.1; vType defaults maxSpeedLat = 1.0 m/s, minGapLat = 0.6 m
  • standard: California Vehicle Code §21760 (Three Feet for Safety Act, 2014): a motorist passes a cyclist in the same direction at no less than three feet; most US states have the same rule
  • standard: Uniform Vehicle Code §11-1205: a cyclist rides as near the right side of the roadway as practicable, sharing the lane

mobility/meso/queue¶

The mesoscopic tier: each edge is a chain of FIFO queues; a vehicle leaves a segment no earlier than its free-flow time, from the head only, a jam-dependent headway after the one before, into a segment with room, and through a signal only on green.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract; hosting in-process; draws random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
segment_length_m m 98.0 code: SUMO documentation, Simulation/Meso: --meso-edgelength 98, --meso-tauff 1.13, --meso-taufj 1.13, --meso-taujf 1.73, --meso-taujj 1.4, --meso-jam-threshold -1, --meso-junction-control
tau_ff_s s 1.13 code: SUMO documentation, Simulation/Meso: --meso-edgelength 98, --meso-tauff 1.13, --meso-taufj 1.13, --meso-taujf 1.73, --meso-taujj 1.4, --meso-jam-threshold -1, --meso-junction-control
tau_fj_s s 1.13 code: SUMO documentation, Simulation/Meso: --meso-edgelength 98, --meso-tauff 1.13, --meso-taufj 1.13, --meso-taujf 1.73, --meso-taujj 1.4, --meso-jam-threshold -1, --meso-junction-control
tau_jf_s s 1.73 code: SUMO documentation, Simulation/Meso: --meso-edgelength 98, --meso-tauff 1.13, --meso-taufj 1.13, --meso-taujf 1.73, --meso-taujj 1.4, --meso-jam-threshold -1, --meso-junction-control
tau_jj_s s 1.4 code: SUMO documentation, Simulation/Meso: --meso-edgelength 98, --meso-tauff 1.13, --meso-taufj 1.13, --meso-taujf 1.73, --meso-taujj 1.4, --meso-jam-threshold -1, --meso-junction-control
jam_threshold - null code: SUMO documentation, Simulation/Meso: --meso-edgelength 98, --meso-tauff 1.13, --meso-taufj 1.13, --meso-taujf 1.73, --meso-taujj 1.4, --meso-jam-threshold -1, --meso-junction-control
junction_control - true code: SUMO documentation, Simulation/Meso: --meso-edgelength 98, --meso-tauff 1.13, --meso-taufj 1.13, --meso-taujf 1.73, --meso-taujj 1.4, --meso-jam-threshold -1, --meso-junction-control

Equations.

  • exit time: t_exit = max(t_entry + ℓ / min(v_desired, v_max·f_speed), t_last + τ_xy / lanes, t_green, t_room) x, y ∈ {free, jammed} for this segment and the next
  • jam: jammed ⇔ occupancy > capacity · L / (v_max·τ_ff + L), L = 7.5 m SUMO's --meso-jam-threshold -1

Assumptions.

  • One FIFO queue per segment shared by its lanes (Eissfeldt's original), not SUMO's later per-lane queues; the headway is divided by the lane count.
  • A blocked departure (red, full segment, refusing boundary) is retried at the next mobility step; a free one keeps its exact instant.
  • Positions are the queue's placement, not a trajectory: free-flow motion to the earliest exit, then standing in the queue at the segment's end.

Limitations.

  • No lane changes, overtaking within a segment, or acceleration: speed is free-flow or zero.
  • Unsignalised junctions are only their headways: no priority between approaches.

Ignores.

  • Speed caps, stops, reroutes and closures sent as commands.
  • Weather.

Sources.

  • paper: Eissfeldt, N. G. (2003). Vehicle-based modelling of traffic: theory and application to environmental impact modelling. PhD thesis, Universität zu Köln.. the queue model: segment capacity, free-flow exit time, jam-dependent headways
  • paper: Krajzewicz, D., Erdmann, J., Behrisch, M., Bieker, L. (2012). Recent Development and Applications of SUMO. Int. J. Advances in Systems and Measurements 5(3&4), 128-138.. MESO, SUMO's mesoscopic mode
  • code: SUMO documentation, Simulation/Meso: --meso-edgelength 98, --meso-tauff 1.13, --meso-taufj 1.13, --meso-taujf 1.73, --meso-taujj 1.4, --meso-jam-threshold -1, --meso-junction-control

mobility/native/medium¶

The native mobility engine: it freezes every actor's state at the start of each step, asks the car-following, lane-change and intersection models for one decision per actor from that frozen state, integrates them all together, and publishes Kinematics for every actor with the spawn, despawn and signal-state lists. The freeze is what makes the update a Jacobi update and therefore independent of the order the actors are visited in (ADR 0004).

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium; hosting in-process; draws random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
step s 0.1 standard: ADR 0004 decision 2: mobility step default 100 ms, range 10-100 ms
car_following - mobility/car-following/idm code: (the native tiers) and (the Mobility trait and its invariants I-M1, I-M2, I-M4)
intersections - SignalsAndGapAcceptance code: (the native tiers) and (the Mobility trait and its invariants I-M1, I-M2, I-M4)
lane_changes - true code: (the native tiers) and (the Mobility trait and its invariants I-M1, I-M2, I-M4)
lookahead m 70.0 code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py: idm_lookahead_m 70 m
classes - ["car", "truck", "bus", "moto", "emergency"] code: (the native tiers) and (the Mobility trait and its invariants I-M1, I-M2, I-M4)
insertion_gap m 2.0 code: this crate: a trip whose origin is occupied is dropped rather than overlapped, and the drop is counted (dropped_trips)
stop_line_offset m 2.0 code: legacy/scms_sim_ref/mock_pipeline/run.py L2467
dynamic_rerouting - true code: (the native tiers) and (the Mobility trait and its invariants I-M1, I-M2, I-M4)
no_change_zone m 30.0 standard: MUTCD 2009 §3B.04: a solid lane line where crossing it is discouraged, as on the approach to a junction. the manual does not fix the length; 30 m is this crate's choice — the 2.5 s transition at 25 mph (28 m) plus margin — and the zone is stretched to the vehicle's own speed × transition time when longer
turn_lateral_accel m/s² 5.5 code: SUMO netconvert --junctions.limit-turn-speed, default 5.5 ("limits speed on junctions to an average lateral acceleration of at most FLOAT m/s^2")
junction_clearance - true standard: UVC §11-202(a)1 / NY VTL §1111(a)1: a driver facing a green shall yield to vehicles lawfully within the intersection; NY VTL §1175: no driver shall enter an intersection unless there is space on the opposite side
kerbside s, m, 1 {"bus_dwell_cv": 0.6, "bus_dwell_mean_s": 60.0, "bus_stop_spacing_m": 260.0, ... standard: crate::kerbside: bus dwell TCRP Report 100 (TCQSM 2nd ed.) Part 4, 60 s downtown default, c_v 0.6 (secondary); stop spacing, taxi share and dwell, delivery share and dwell are choices recorded on the module
driver_styles - true paper: crate::style: cautious / normal / assertive below the 15th, between, and above the 85th percentile of the free-speed draw; faster drivers follow closer (Evans & Wasielewski 1982, 1983; secondary); multipliers a choice that keeps the fleet means
heavy_turns - true standard: AASHTO Green Book 2018 §2.1.3: minimum turning paths drawn for 10 mph or less (secondary)
class_routing - true standard: the world's per-lane class masks (a bus lane admits buses and emergency vehicles, NYC Traffic Rules §4-12(m)); each class is routed on its own
reverse_order - false code: a test hook, not a model parameter: it walks the decision pass backwards so the Jacobi property can be asserted (ADR 0004)

Equations.

  • integration: v ← clamp(v + a·Δt, 0, v0); s ← s + v·Δt the new speed carries the step, which is the legacy engine's order [run.py L2493-2495] and what keeps a decelerating vehicle from rolling through a stop line
  • published position: pos = point on the driven path at s − length (walked back over the lanes behind); heading = atan2(front − rear) s is the front bumper and Kinematics::pos is the rear-axle centre; the class table gives no wheelbase, so the rear bumper stands in for the rear axle. The heading is the body's, from rear to front, so it turns continuously through a junction. The legacy parity mode keeps the reference engine's placement, clamped to the front's lane
  • stop-line braking: a = max(a_IDM, −v²/(2·(s − s0))) for a standing virtual obstacle a virtual stop-line obstacle is braked for at max(a_IDM, −v²/(2·(s − s0))): the constant-acceleration heuristic (Kesting, Treiber & Helbing 2010, Phil. Trans. R. Soc. A 368:4585) for a standing obstacle, which stops at the line with the least deceleration that does, instead of the IDM's over-reaction to an obstacle that appears at a signal change
  • turn speed: v_turn = min(v_limit, sqrt(a_lat · L / |Δψ|)) a junction connector of length L turning through Δψ, driven at an average lateral acceleration a_lat = 5.5 m/s² (SUMO netconvert --junctions.limit-turn-speed default)
  • anticipation: v0 ← min(v0, sqrt(v_next² + 2·b·d)) for each lane ahead on the route within the lookahead, d metres away with limit (or turn speed) v_next: the vehicle slows at its comfortable deceleration b before the boundary instead of having its speed cut at it

Assumptions.

  • Every decision in a step is a function of the start-of-step state of every actor (the Jacobi update, ADR 0004).
  • Actor ids are assigned in demand-stream order (invariant I-M2), and every output list is ordered by actor id (I-M1).
  • Between steps, consumers extrapolate at constant velocity (invariant I-M4).
  • A red signal, a junction to give way at and a curve-speed cap all reach the vehicle as a virtual leader, so one equation produces every deceleration.
  • The published reference point is the rear bumper, standing in for the rear-axle centre.
  • Outside the legacy parity mode: a lane change is refused inside the no-change zone before a junction, while the body still overhangs the previous lane, when the target lane cannot make the route's next movement, and when another vehicle has already taken the same gap this step (actor-id order); the route is shifted onto the new lane movement by movement.
  • Outside the legacy parity mode: a vehicle holds at the stop line while a vehicle already inside the junction on a crossing movement has not cleared the shared conflict zone, or while its exit has no room behind a standing queue; inside the junction it gives way at a crossing to a vehicle that reaches it first, and follows one merging ahead onto the same exit.
  • A vehicle that chose to go on amber is committed to clearing the junction and does not slow for the turn ahead until it has entered.
  • Vulnerable road users (VruPopulation): pedestrians are placed on a random walkable lane and walk a random chain of connected walkable lanes with the social-force model; cyclists ride between random bicycle-admitting lanes on the bicycle lane graph with the car-following model and the SUMO bicycle vType (20 km/h, 1.2 m/s², 3.0 m/s², 0.5 m, tau 1 s). Each one that finishes is replaced.
  • A trip whose origin has no room waits, oldest first, up to 120 s (SUMO's insertion queue), holding the actor id it was given when it first asked.

Limitations.

  • A lane change moves the vehicle into the target lane's frame at the start of the transition and slides the lateral offset to zero over it; the vehicle is therefore in the target lane for the whole transition, which is the legacy engine's behaviour and not a sublane model.
  • A trip whose origin is occupied is dropped rather than queued.

Ignores.

  • Everything the SUMO tier does that the native models do not: driver imperfection, action step length, cooperative lane changes, the junction request matrices, pedestrian striping.

Sources.

  • code: (the native tiers) and (the Mobility trait and its invariants I-M1, I-M2, I-M4)

mobility/routing/dijkstra¶

Dijkstra on the lane graph with edge cost = length / speed limit, honouring turn restrictions and lane-class masks.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
classes - ["car"] code: : a lane carries the class mask that may use it
allow_uturn - false code: (edge cost = length / speed limit; closures raise the cost to infinity), from roads.py CustomNetwork []
max_settled - 2000000 code: an engineering bound, not a model parameter: it stops a pathological query rather than shaping a route

Equations.

  • edge cost: c(lane) = length_m / speed_limit_mps free-flow travel time; a closed lane has no cost at all and is not in the graph, which is how "raise the cost to infinity" is implemented without infinities in the arithmetic

Assumptions.

  • A node is a lane and an arc is a permitted connection, which is the only graph a turn restriction can be expressed on.
  • Equal-cost paths are resolved by lane id, so the route is a pure function of the world and the costs.
  • A motorised vehicle may only use a lane whose kind is for motor traffic, whatever the lane's class mask says.

Limitations.

  • One shortest path: no route-choice heterogeneity, no stochastic assignment ( lists this as ignored by all tiers).

Ignores.

  • SUMO's duarouter and its rerouters, which serve the high tier.

Sources.

  • code: (edge cost = length / speed limit; closures raise the cost to infinity), from roads.py CustomNetwork []

mobility/tiered/meso-micro¶

Microscopic traffic (the native engine) inside a focus region and the mesoscopic queue network everywhere else, with vehicles handed across the boundary under one id, route and driver, and spill-back crossing it both ways.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium, abstract; hosting in-process; draws random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
region m {"centre_m": [0.0, 0.0], "kind": "circle", "radius_m": 500.0} code: this crate: the coupling rules of crate::meso::tiered (hand-in on admission, hand-out with holds), with 's focus region as the region
meso - {"jam_threshold": null, "junction_control": true, "segment_length_m": 98.0, "... code: see mobility/meso/queue's card

Assumptions.

  • A lane is microscopic when its midpoint is in the region; a junction's internal lane when the junction is.
  • A vehicle handed in enters at the start of its next lane at the smaller of its desired speed and the lane's limit, within the step it left its queue.
  • A vehicle handed out keeps its position; the queue gives it a free-flow time over the rest of the segment from the end of the step.

Limitations.

  • A hand-in is timed to the step it happens in, not to its instant within it.
  • Pedestrians and cyclists stay in the microscopic tier wherever they are.

Sources.

  • code: this crate: the coupling rules of crate::meso::tiered (hand-in on admission, hand-out with holds), with 's focus region as the region

signals/nema-dual-ring¶

The NEMA dual-ring eight-phase actuated controller (traffic.signals.control: nema): two rings timing concurrently and crossing each barrier together, minimum green, passage, gap-out and max-out, yellow and red clearance, protected and protected-permissive lefts with the flashing yellow arrow, pedestrian intervals on push-button calls, coordination with force-offs, and priority service; the rings are re-projected into each mapped junction's plan, and junctions that do not map keep the single-ring controller. Re-implemented from FHWA STM 2nd ed. and NEMA TS 2-2016; SUMO was read for behaviour only.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Equations.

  • gap-out: a green ends when green ≥ min_green, no detection for passage_s, and a conflicting call waits STM 2nd ed. §5.3.2
  • max-out: a green ends at max_green after a conflicting call STM 2nd ed. §5.3.3, Exhibit 6-9
  • barrier: ring 1 and ring 2 leave a barrier group only when both have ended their clearance STM 2nd ed. §5.1.2, Exhibit 5-3
  • yellow change: y = t + v / (2a + 2Gg) STM 2nd ed. Equation 6-1 (ITE)

Sources.

  • standard: FHWA Signal Timing Manual 2nd ed. (NCHRP Report 812, 2015): ch. 5 (§5.1.2 Exhibit 5-3 dual ring, barriers; §5.3 actuated operation), ch. 6 (Exhibits 6-3, 6-4, 6-9; Equation 6-1). each timing default cites its exhibit in crate::nema::PhaseTiming
  • standard: MUTCD 2009 ch. 4D (signal indications, flashing yellow arrow) and 4E (pedestrian intervals, 3.5 ft/s walking speed)
  • standard: NEMA TS 2-2016 (paywalled; not read: the STM is the working text). cited for the controller and conflict-monitor terms only
  • code: SUMO NEMA controller documentation (parameter names and behaviour only). read for behaviour only; no code was copied

net¶

net/gn-btp/en302636¶

Exact ETSI GeoNetworking and BTP header sizes (Basic 4, Common 8, LPV 24, SPV 20, SHB 40, TSB 40, GBC/GAC 56, GUC 60, BEACON 36, BTP 4, LLC/SNAP 8) and the itsGnMaxSduSize MTU rule. Fragments nothing: oversize content is segmented at the facilities layer.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
its_gn_max_sdu_size B 1398 [64, 9000] standard: ETSI EN 302 636-4-1 V1.4.1 Annex H item 8 (itsGnMaxSduSize = 1 398 octets)
mtu_al_bytes B 1500 [64, 9000] standard: ETSI EN 302 636-4-1 V1.4.1 Annex H item 8 quotes MTU_AL = 1 500 in the itsGnMaxSduSize calculation; §9.2.3 gives the rule MTU_GN <= MTU_AL - GEO_MAX
max_gn_header_bytes B 88 [60, 255] standard: ETSI EN 302 636-4-1 V1.4.1 Annex H item 9 (itsGnMaxGeoNetworkingHeaderSize = 88)
max_gn_security_bytes B 0 [0, 255] standard: ETSI EN 302 636-4-1 V1.4.1 Annex H item 8 (GNSEC_MAX = 0 in the quoted calculation)

Equations.

  • header size: H = GN(transport) + BTP + [LLC/SNAP] GN(SHB) = 4 + 8 + 24 + 4 = 40; GN(GBC) = 4 + 8 + 2 + 2 + 24 + 4 + 4 + 2 + 2 + 2 + 2 = 56; GN(GUC) = 4 + 8 + 2 + 2 + 24 + 20 = 60; BTP-A and BTP-B are 4 each. Below a secured CAM on ITS-G5: 8 + 40 + 4 = 52.
  • MTU rule: MTU = min(itsGnMaxSduSize, MTU_AL - GN_MAX - GNSEC_MAX) = min(1398, 1500 - 88 - 0) = 1398 EN 302 636-4-1 §9.2.3 states MTU_GN <= MTU_AL - GEO_MAX; Annex H items 8-9 give itsGnMaxSduSize = 1 398 and GN_MAX = 88, both VERIFIED. The identity '1 398 = 1 500 - 88 - 0' quoted by does not evaluate (1 500 - 88 = 1 412), so the two verified numbers are kept separate and the smaller binds. GN_MAX is a provisioned ceiling that already includes a security allowance, which is why it exceeds the largest header this model sizes (GUC 60 + BTP 4).

Assumptions.

  • ITS-G5 framing: the eight LLC/SNAP octets are counted at this layer when NetMeta::include_llc_snap is set, which is the default for the GeoNetworking constructors, because the documented 52-octet figure below a secured CAM includes them. Invariant I-N1 requires that the MAC model then does not count them again.
  • One position vector per packet, of the size its transport type's table specifies; the optional GN extension headers (secured-packet, IPv6 adaptation) are out of scope at these tiers.
  • Header sizes are exact; the header's octets are not synthesised (see the crate documentation).

Limitations.

  • The LLC/SNAP figure of 8 octets is DERIVED from IEEE 802.2 plus SNAP (DSAP/SSAP 0xAA, control 0x03, OUI 00-00-00, EtherType). EN 302 663 §4.3.1 Figure 3 shows "IEEE/ISO/IEC 8802-2 with SNAP" but does not quote the octet count, and TS 102 636-4-2 V1.1.1 does not contain it.
  • The 52-octet total below a secured CAM is DERIVED by addition from three VERIFIED sizes; no capture was measured against it.
  • The identity '1 398 = 1 500 - 88 - 0' that and quote for itsGnMaxSduSize does not evaluate: 1 500 - 88 is 1 412, and 1 398 + 88 is 1 486, so the MTU_AL the MIB's own subtraction used was not 1 500. The two Annex H values (1 398 and 88) are VERIFIED and are both kept; the MTU is the smaller of the MIB value and §9.2.3's rule, and no source is stretched to make the quoted subtraction come out.
  • GeoNetworking does not fragment: the full text of EN 302 636-4-1 V1.4.1 contains zero occurrences of "fragment" (VERIFIED by absence). An oversize SDU is refused here, and oversize content is segmented at the facilities layer (MAPEM layerID, CPM messageSegmentInfo) by the fragmenter/facilities-segmentation model.
  • The CAM (2001), DENM (2002) and SSEM (2008) BTP ports are attributed to TS 103 248, which was not extracted, and are UNVERIFIED. A port changes no header size — a BTP header is four octets whatever the port — so the exposure is demultiplexing inside the model only.

Ignores.

  • GN forwarding: GBC multi-hop forwarding, the DENM keep-alive forwarding algorithm, the location table, duplicate-packet detection and the contention-based forwarding timers, all of which belong to the high net tier (§7.5).
  • DCC_NET, the GeoNetworking CBR-sharing mechanism of TS 102 636-4-2.
  • The secured-packet extension header: the security envelope's own bytes are v2xw-sec's and arrive here inside the SDU.

Sources.

  • standard: ETSI EN 302 636-4-1 V1.4.1 (2020-01) §9.5-9.7, §9.2.3, Tables 11-17, Annex H. Header sizes and the MTU rule, all VERIFIED.
  • standard: ETSI EN 302 636-5-1 V2.2.1 (2019-05) §7.2-7.3, Tables 1-3. BTP-A and BTP-B, 4 octets each, VERIFIED.
  • standard: ETSI TS 103 301 CSP_PortNo tables — well-known BTP ports (MAPEM 2003, SPATEM 2004, IVIM 2006, SREM 2007, RTCMEM 2013)
  • paper: -F.3 — the fact sheet these sizes were extracted from

net/wsmp/1609-3¶

Exact IEEE 1609.3 WSMP v3 header sizes: the one-octet N-header, the optional three-octet channel / data-rate / transmit-power extensions, the TPID octet, the p-encoded PSID and the variable length field. Exposes the WSM payload cap and fragments nothing.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
max_wsm_payload_bytes B 1400 [1, 2302] standard: IEEE 1609.3-2020 MIB default maximum WSM payload (1,400 B); 2,302 B supported; MSDU cap 2,304 B. Quoted by USDOT CTI 4501 v01.01 §4.3.3.1.3.1 and by NDSS 2024; the 1609.3 clause itself is paywalled (VERIFIED secondary).

Equations.

  • header size: H = 1 + (k > 0) + 3*k + 1 + p(PSID) + p(len) [+ 8 if LLC/SNAP counted here] k is the number of present N-header extensions (0..3), preceded by their one-octet count when k > 0 (UPER SEQUENCE OF, 1609.3 wsm.asn); p is the IEEE 1609.3 p-encoding width in octets. A BSM with PSID 0x20 and a payload of at least 128 B gives 1+0+1+1+2 = 5.
  • p-encoding width: p(v) = 1 for v <= 0x7F, 2 for v <= 0x407F, 3 for v <= 0x20407F, 4 for v <= 0x1020407F Prefix bits plus the previous range's exclusive end as an offset; the top of the four-octet range is the documented PSID maximum 0x1020407F.

Assumptions.

  • Only the PSID-addressed WSMP transport forms are modelled. IEEE 1609.3 also offers UDP/IPv6 and TCP/IPv6, which puts outside Phases 1-2.
  • The TPID octet is counted once and its value is not modelled, because both PSID-only forms cost the same single octet.
  • Header sizes are exact; the header's octets are not synthesised (see the crate documentation). The SDU's bytes are the codec's own.

Limitations.

  • The width of the WSMP-T length field is DERIVED. gives a one-octet length in the 4-octet minimum and gives a two-octet length for a BSM with a payload of at least 128 B; the p-encoding boundary at 0x80 is the only rule that yields both, and the standard's clause for it is not accessible.
  • The snippet "minimum 5 bytes ... rarely exceeding 20" attributed to the 1609.3 text is UNVERIFIED and is not used for anything.
  • The two payload maxima and the MSDU cap are not mutually consistent. A 2,302 B "supported" WSM payload plus even a minimum 4-octet WSMP header is 2,306 B, two octets above the 2,304 B MSDU cap, so the figures come from different accounting conventions (payload versus MSDU). The model enforces max_wsm_payload_bytes and leaves the MSDU cap to the MAC model, which is where the frame is assembled.
  • WSMP does not fragment, so this layer refuses an oversize SDU rather than splitting it: finds no fragmentation field in ShortMsgNpdu, ShortMsgData is annotated "maximum size is given by access technology", and the Wireshark dissector has no reassembly (VERIFIED by absence; the clause number is UNVERIFIED).

Ignores.

  • IEEE 1609.4 channel switching: the channel-number extension is sized, never acted on.
  • WSA/WSM service advertisement semantics, which belong to the generator family.

Sources.

  • code: Wireshark epan/dissectors/packet-wsmp.c (dissect_wsmp_v3), epan/etypes.h. Field-by-field layout and EtherType 0x88DC.
  • standard: IEEE 1609.3 ASN.1 modules wsm.asn and wee.asn (ShortMsgNpdu, ShortMsgTpdus, ShortMsgNextensions, VarLengthNumber)
  • standard: USDOT CTI 4501 v01.01 §4.3.3.1.3.1 — WSM payload maxima
  • paper: — the fact sheet these sizes were extracted from

primitive¶

primitive/aes-128¶

Sizes, NIST security level and per-hardware-profile cost anchors for primitive/aes-128. Declares cost and size; computes no cryptography.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Assumptions.

  • Priced per 16-byte block call; sig_bytes is the block. The key schedule is not priced separately (see the rows' notes).
  • hw-profile/camp-planning-2ghz: "81 MB/s encryption on a 2 GHz processor", chunk size not stated; read per block
  • hw-profile/cortex-a53-pi3b: the 16-byte openssl speed column: one block per call, the pattern of the linkage-value and butterfly constructions; the key schedule is outside the timed loop, so a construction that rekeys per call (a linkage seed as the AES key) is under-costed by one key expansion per call
  • hw-profile/cortex-a53-pi3bplus: the 16-byte openssl speed column: one block per call, the pattern of the linkage-value and butterfly constructions; the key schedule is outside the timed loop, so a construction that rekeys per call (a linkage seed as the AES key) is under-costed by one key expansion per call
  • hw-profile/cortex-a72-pi4-openssl-1.1.1d: the 16-byte openssl speed column: one block per call, the pattern of the linkage-value and butterfly constructions; the key schedule is outside the timed loop, so a construction that rekeys per call (a linkage seed as the AES key) is under-costed by one key expansion per call
  • hw-profile/cortex-a76-pi5-mbedtls-3.6.6: AES-128-GCM throughput stands in for raw AES blocks: GCM is one AES call per block plus GHASH, so the per-block figure is an upper bound of the AES call
  • hw-profile/cortex-a76-pi5-wolfssl-5.9.1: AES-128-GCM throughput stands in for raw AES blocks: GCM is one AES call per block plus GHASH, so the per-block figure is an upper bound of the AES call
  • hw-profile/cortex-m33-stm32h563-250mhz-mbedtls: AES-128-CBC: one AES call per block
  • hw-profile/cortex-m33-stm32h563-250mhz-wolfssl: AES-128-CBC: one AES call per block
  • hw-profile/cortex-m4-nrf52840-64mhz: the CCM call is 20 AES calls (SP 800-38C): CBC-MAC over B0, 16 blocks of the 2-byte-length-prefixed 250-byte associated data and 1 payload block, plus CTR over A0 and 1 payload block; the figure includes CCM's own bookkeeping, so the per-call cost is an upper bound
  • hw-profile/i9-11950h-mbedtls: AES-128-GCM throughput stands in for raw AES blocks: GCM is one AES call per block plus GHASH, so the per-block figure is an upper bound of the AES call
  • hw-profile/i9-11950h-wolfssl: AES-128-GCM throughput stands in for raw AES blocks: GCM is one AES call per block plus GHASH, so the per-block figure is an upper bound of the AES call
  • hw-profile/x86-64-openssl-1.1.1f-cookbook: openssl speed -evp aes-128-cbc (AES-NI), 16-byte column

Limitations.

  • Declares sizes and costs only; a CryptoBackend decides whether the primitive is actually executed.

  • code: gist dchest/6b624752d3b54200362f1e031c790f3d, openssl speed, OpenSSL 1.1.0f armhf (sha256 adb49576… 3B.txt, 3c8184c2… 3B-Plus.txt)

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Assumptions.

  • sig_bytes is the 16-byte tag. Priced as its exact AES-call count, 2·⌈n/16⌉ + 2 for an n-byte plaintext with no associated data, times the AES-128 per-call figure on the same profile; an HSM that publishes a per-transaction rate is charged that instead.

  • hw-profile/thales-luna7-a790: AES-GCM transactions per second, payload size unstated: charged once per CCM call whatever its length, with GCM standing in for CCM (both one AES pass plus a MAC pass)

primitive/ecdsa-p256-sha256¶

Sizes, NIST security level and per-hardware-profile cost anchors for primitive/ecdsa-p256-sha256. Declares cost and size; computes no cryptography.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Assumptions.

  • 66 encoded bytes under IEEE 1609.2: Signature choice tag 1 + rSig (EccP256CurvePoint choice tag 1 + 32) + sSig 32.
  • cert_bytes is the DERIVED explicit-certificate size of §9.2 (NDSS's 30 + pk + sig accounting gives 162 for the same certificate).
  • Key generation, where a profile publishes none, is charged as one signature: a fixed-base multiplication plus an inversion, so an upper bound (WP-C4).
  • hw-profile/camp-planning-2ghz: CAMP's own planning assumption, stated as "about"
  • hw-profile/cohda-mk6-botan: the published verify figure of 0.001 ms is anomalous — three orders of magnitude faster than the sign on the same silicon — and is recorded as published rather than corrected
  • hw-profile/cortex-a53-pi3b: indicative only
  • hw-profile/cortex-a53-pi3bplus: indicative only
  • hw-profile/cortex-a72-pi4-openssl-1.1.1d: operating system not stated by the source
  • hw-profile/cortex-m33-stm32h563-250mhz-wolfssl: the same blog's NUCLEO-F446ZE rows are UNVERIFIED and are not used
  • hw-profile/thales-luna7-a750: the brief gives one ECC P-256 transactions-per-second figure, not split into sign and verify; applied to both, as backend/hsm-appliance-luna7 does
  • hw-profile/thales-luna7-a790: the brief gives one ECC P-256 transactions-per-second figure, not split into sign and verify; applied to both, as backend/hsm-appliance-luna7 does
  • hw-profile/x86-64-openssl-1.1.1f-cookbook: CPU not stated by the source

Limitations.

  • Declares sizes and costs only; a CryptoBackend decides whether the primitive is actually executed.

primitive/ecdsa-p384¶

Sizes, NIST security level and per-hardware-profile cost anchors for primitive/ecdsa-p384. Declares cost and size; computes no cryptography.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Assumptions.

  • Covers brainpoolP384r1 too gives the two the same sizes.
  • listed no P-384 anchor; WP-C4 adds the published openssl speed and HSM figures. The P-256 anchors are never a proxy: P-384 is materially slower.
  • §9.4 states the level as "192-bit" rather than as a NIST category; 3 is the category that 192-bit classical strength sits in.
  • hw-profile/cortex-a72-pi4-openssl-1.1.1d: recorded as published: OpenSSL 1.1.1d signs P-384 slower here than 1.1.0f does on the slower Pi 3B (no constant-time nistp384 assembly in 1.1.1d)
  • hw-profile/thales-tct-t5000: one ECC P-384 tps figure, applied to sign and verify
  • hw-profile/x86-64-openssl-1.1.1f-cookbook: CPU not stated by the source

Limitations.

  • Declares sizes and costs only; a CryptoBackend decides whether the primitive is actually executed.

Sources.

  • standard: IEEE 1609.2 Ieee1609Dot2BaseTypes.asn
  • standard: SEC 1 §2.3.3
  • code: gist dchest/6b624752d3b54200362f1e031c790f3d, openssl speed, OpenSSL 1.1.0f armhf (sha256 adb49576… 3B.txt, 3c8184c2… 3B-Plus.txt)
  • code: gist dchest/6b624752d3b54200362f1e031c790f3d, openssl speed, OpenSSL 1.1.0f armhf (sha256 adb49576… 3B.txt, 3c8184c2… 3B-Plus.txt)
  • code: gist HimaJyun/f05d3017dfb05a4ccb0def010bb2c91a, openssl speed, OpenSSL 1.1.1d (sha256 50bcb60a… Pi4B-OpenSSL.txt)
  • datasheet: Thales TCT HSM family sheet, T-5000 Enterprise, ECC P-384
  • datasheet: Ristic, OpenSSL Cookbook, 'Performance' chapter, OpenSSL 1.1.1f

primitive/ecies-p256¶

Sizes, NIST security level and per-hardware-profile cost anchors for primitive/ecies-p256. Declares cost and size; computes no cryptography.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Assumptions.

  • sign is encryption, verify decryption. Priced by its recipe (one fixed-base and one variable-base multiplication to encrypt, the variable-base one to decrypt, and six SHA-256 calls for KDF2 and MAC1) except where an HSM publishes a rate. Left out: the 16-byte XOR and the on-curve check of v.
  • sig_bytes 65 is the COER EciesP256EncryptedKey with v compressed (33 + 16 + 16).
  • hw-profile/thales-luna7-a790: 56,000 TPS ECIES P-256 decrypt from a cluster of 8 A790 appliances, divided by 8 (derived per appliance); the brief's workload is 5G SUCI concealment, whose ECIES differs from 1609.2's only in KDF parameters

Limitations.

  • Declares sizes and costs only; a CryptoBackend decides whether the primitive is actually executed.

Sources.

  • standard: SEC 1 §3.3.1
  • standard: IEEE 1609.2 §5.3.5
  • datasheet: Thales Luna HSM for 5G performance brief

primitive/ecqv-p256¶

Sizes, NIST security level and per-hardware-profile cost anchors for primitive/ecqv-p256. Declares cost and size; computes no cryptography.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Assumptions.

  • pk_bytes 33 is the reconstruction value, a compressed point (§9.2, VERIFIED).
  • cert_bytes 80 is the DERIVED implicit pseudonym certificate size of §9.2; cross-checks are Rostami's 250 - 180 = 70 B delta and VSC-A's 117.
  • No published ECQV timing was found, so the cost is the recipe of the operations 1609.2's ECQV performs (02 §6): reconstruction is one variable-base point multiplication (priced as ECDH P-256) and two SHA-256 calls; issuance one fixed-base multiplication and the same hashes. Left out: one point addition and the decompression of P_U (a field square root), measured on the host by benches/primitives.rs at a few per cent of the multiplication.
  • WP-C4 replaced the earlier proxy (the ECDSA P-256 verify figure, a double multiplication): reconstruction now costs about as much as ECDH, not as an ECDSA verification.

Limitations.

  • Declares sizes and costs only; a CryptoBackend decides whether the primitive is actually executed.

Sources.

  • standard: SEC 4 §3.4-3.5
  • standard: SEC 1 §2.3.3

primitive/hybrid-falcon512-ecdsa-p256¶

Sizes, NIST security level and per-hardware-profile cost anchors for primitive/hybrid-falcon512-ecdsa-p256. Declares cost and size; computes no cryptography.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Assumptions.

  • pk 930 = 897 + 33 raw and sig 730 = 666 + 64 raw, as §9.4 derives them; the maximum follows from Falcon's unpadded 752 + 64.
  • sk 1,313 = 1,281 + 32, derived.
  • No benchmark of the hybrid as a unit was found. Signing or verifying one means doing both halves, so its cost is the sum of the Falcon-512 and ECDSA P-256 anchors on the same hardware profile.

Limitations.

  • Declares sizes and costs only; a CryptoBackend decides whether the primitive is actually executed.

Sources.

  • paper: derived from PQClean falcon-padded-512 + SEC 1

primitive/hybrid-mldsa44-ecdsa-p256¶

Sizes, NIST security level and per-hardware-profile cost anchors for primitive/hybrid-mldsa44-ecdsa-p256. Declares cost and size; computes no cryptography.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Assumptions.

  • pk 1,345 = 1,312 ML-DSA-44 + 33 compressed P-256, raw; the composite encoding is 1,377.
  • sig mean 2,484 = 2,420 + 64 raw; the composite maximum is 2,492.
  • sk 2,592 = 2,560 + 32, derived.
  • No benchmark of the hybrid as a unit was found. Signing or verifying one means doing both halves, so its cost is the sum of the ML-DSA-44 and ECDSA P-256 anchors on the same hardware profile.

Limitations.

primitive/linkage-value¶

Sizes, NIST security level and per-hardware-profile cost anchors for primitive/linkage-value. Declares cost and size; computes no cryptography.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Assumptions.

  • keygen advances both LAs' seeds one i-period (two SHA-256 calls), sign and verify compute one lv(i, j) (two AES calls) — the LA's work, and a receiver's per-entry, per-j CRL expansion. Left out: the 9-byte XOR.

Limitations.

  • Declares sizes and costs only; a CryptoBackend decides whether the primitive is actually executed.

primitive/ml-dsa-44¶

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Assumptions.

  • Cost rows citing the Dilithium round-3 specification are for the round-3 parameter set, whose sizes differ slightly from the FIPS 204 ones above.

  • hw-profile/skylake-ref-c: Dilithium2; the round-3 sizes differ slightly from FIPS 204 ML-DSA-44

Limitations.

primitive/sha-256¶

Sizes, NIST security level and per-hardware-profile cost anchors for primitive/sha-256. Declares cost and size; computes no cryptography.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Assumptions.

  • sig_bytes is the digest length. Priced per 64-byte compression call after padding (WP-C4); a verify plan's hashes cost time on every profile with a row, and are unknown — not free — on one without.
  • hw-profile/aurix-tc3xx-hsm-100mhz: 65 clock cycles per 512-bit block on the SHA-224/256 accelerator; the same document's '<2 µs latency per block' is an upper bound including setup and is not charged
  • hw-profile/cortex-a53-pi3b: the 64-byte openssl speed column: two compression calls per message, the size class of the hashes a 1609.2 receiver runs (tbsData, a certificate, KDF2 and MAC1 inputs); the longer columns amortise a per-call overhead a short V2X hash pays
  • hw-profile/cortex-a53-pi3bplus: the 64-byte openssl speed column: two compression calls per message, the size class of the hashes a 1609.2 receiver runs (tbsData, a certificate, KDF2 and MAC1 inputs); the longer columns amortise a per-call overhead a short V2X hash pays
  • hw-profile/cortex-a72-pi4-openssl-1.1.1d: the 64-byte openssl speed column: two compression calls per message, the size class of the hashes a 1609.2 receiver runs (tbsData, a certificate, KDF2 and MAC1 inputs); the longer columns amortise a per-call overhead a short V2X hash pays
  • hw-profile/cortex-m4-nrf52840-64mhz: the Oberon software figure, for the software profile; the CryptoCell-310 hardware figure is not this profile's path

Limitations.

  • Declares sizes and costs only; a CryptoBackend decides whether the primitive is actually executed.

Sources.

  • standard: FIPS 180-4
  • datasheet: Infineon AURIX TC3xx HSM training document
  • code: gist dchest/6b624752d3b54200362f1e031c790f3d, openssl speed, OpenSSL 1.1.0f armhf (sha256 adb49576… 3B.txt, 3c8184c2… 3B-Plus.txt)
  • code: gist dchest/6b624752d3b54200362f1e031c790f3d, openssl speed, OpenSSL 1.1.0f armhf (sha256 adb49576… 3B.txt, 3c8184c2… 3B-Plus.txt)
  • code: gist HimaJyun/f05d3017dfb05a4ccb0def010bb2c91a, openssl speed, OpenSSL 1.1.1d (sha256 50bcb60a… Pi4B-OpenSSL.txt)
  • datasheet: wolfSSL vs MbedTLS benchmark, 2026-06-26, 1024-byte blocks
  • datasheet: wolfSSL vs MbedTLS benchmark, 2026-06-26, 1024-byte blocks

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Assumptions.

  • The SHAKE parameter set has identical sizes; only the hash family differs.
  • publishes cost anchors for the 128s and 128f parameter sets only; the 192 and 256 sets carry no cost anchor and none is inferred, because SLH-DSA's cost scales with its tree parameters and not with its signature size.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Assumptions.

  • The SHAKE parameter set has identical sizes; only the hash family differs.
  • publishes cost anchors for the 128s and 128f parameter sets only; the 192 and 256 sets carry no cost anchor and none is inferred, because SLH-DSA's cost scales with its tree parameters and not with its signature size.
  • hw-profile/cohda-mk6-liboqs: published as "SPHINCS+" with no parameter set; the 5.485 ms sign is anomalous next to the reference 2.7e9 cycles and is recorded as published rather than corrected
  • hw-profile/xeon-e3-1220-ref-c: simple variant

Limitations.

  • Declares sizes and costs only; a CryptoBackend decides whether the primitive is actually executed.

Sources.

  • standard: FIPS 205 Table 2
  • paper: NDSS 2024 Table V
  • code: pqm4 benchmarks.csv, clean implementation
  • paper: SPHINCS+ round-3.1 specification Table 6
  • paper: SPHINCS+ round-3.1 specification Table 4

antenna/vehicle/tr37885-option1¶

Vehicle antenna patterns from TR 37.885 Option 1: a rooftop panel omnidirectional in azimuth on cars and vans, front and rear panels on trucks and buses, and the vertical pattern of every element, relative to the element's 3 dBi maximum.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
theta_3db_deg deg 90.0 standard: 3GPP TR 37.885 V15.3.0 (2019-06) §6.1.4, Tables 6.1.4-8 and 6.1.4-9 (Option 1, 6 GHz), via ATIS.3GPP.37.885.V1530
sla_v_db dB 20.0 standard: 3GPP TR 37.885 V15.3.0 (2019-06) §6.1.4, Tables 6.1.4-8 and 6.1.4-9 (Option 1, 6 GHz), via ATIS.3GPP.37.885.V1530
phi_3db_deg deg 120.0 standard: 3GPP TR 37.885 V15.3.0 (2019-06) §6.1.4, Tables 6.1.4-8 and 6.1.4-9 (Option 1, 6 GHz), via ATIS.3GPP.37.885.V1530
a_m_db dB 20.0 standard: 3GPP TR 37.885 V15.3.0 (2019-06) §6.1.4, Tables 6.1.4-8 and 6.1.4-9 (Option 1, 6 GHz), via ATIS.3GPP.37.885.V1530

Equations.

  • vertical: A_E,V(θ) = −min{12·((θ − 90°)/θ3dB)², SLA_V}, θ3dB = 90°, SLA_V = 20 dB
  • horizontal: rooftop: A_E,H = 0; front-rear: A_E,H(φ) = −min{12·(φ/φ3dB)², A_m} per panel at 0° and 180°, φ3dB = 120°, A_m = 20 dB, the better panel taken
  • combined: A(θ, φ) = −min{−(A_E,V + A_E,H), A_m}

Assumptions.

  • A front-rear vehicle uses the better of its two panels toward the other end (selection); TR 37.885 leaves the TXRU mapping to the proponent.
  • The class-to-type mapping: car, van and motorcycle are Type 2 (rooftop), truck and bus Type 3 (front and rear).

Limitations.

  • No measured installed pattern: the roof's own shaping (ripple of a few dB, the 5GAA P-190033 Figure 10 patterns) is not modelled.

Sources.

  • standard: 3GPP TR 37.885 V15.3.0 (2019-06) §6.1.4, Tables 6.1.4-8 and 6.1.4-9 (Option 1, 6 GHz), via ATIS.3GPP.37.885.V1530

protocol¶

protocol/etsi/ts102941¶

ETSI TS 102 941 ITS PKI: enrolment, standard authorization, butterfly authorization with a separate ticket download, ECTL and CA-CRL distribution, and the TS 103 759 reporting path, as flows over queued Enrolment, Authorization, Trust-List and Misbehaviour Authorities. Message structures are hand-written and every certificate in them is sized by the real COER encoder, because build decision D5 defers the ETSI PKI ASN.1 on an inner-subtyping construct.

version 0.2.0; tiers abstract; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
ec_validity_days d 1095 standard: EU C-ITS Certificate Policy 1.1 Table 11
at_validity_days d 7 standard: EU C-ITS Certificate Policy 1.1 §7.2.1
at_concurrent - 100 standard: EU C-ITS Certificate Policy 1.1 §7.2.1 (≤ 100); ETSI TR 103 415 Table A.2 gives 20 parallel in the C2C-CC profile
at_preload_days d 90 standard: EU C-ITS Certificate Policy 1.1 §7.2.1
etsi_subject_attributes_bytes B 64 todo-calibrate: no clause fixes the container's size (calibration pending)
backend_servers - 4 todo-calibrate: no source picks a deployment size (calibration pending)
link_latency_ms ms 10 todo-calibrate: no published EA/AA topology (calibration pending)
overhead_us us 1000 todo-calibrate: no published EA/AA transaction rates (calibration pending)
ctl_validity_days d 90 standard: EU C-ITS Certificate Policy 1.1 §2.2: trust-material update cadence <= 3 months, with stations obliged to update within 1 week (via )
etsi_butterfly_response_bytes B 16 todo-calibrate: no clause this build can read fixes the currentI / requestHash / nextDlTime block of a ButterflyAuthorizationResponse (calibration pending)
etsi_ctl_framing_bytes B 8 todo-calibrate: no clause this build can read fixes the trust-list framing: nextUpdate, ctlSequence and the list container (calibration pending)
etsi_report_payload_bytes B 1200 todo-calibrate: TS 103 759 §4.2.4 requires the evidence to carry the original received messages with their AT and does not fix how many (calibration pending)
butterfly_batch - 20 todo-calibrate: TS 102 941 §6.2.3.5 gives the butterfly expansion its shape and no ticket count (calibration pending)
at_download_batch - 20 todo-calibrate: as butterfly_batch: no clause fixes a batch size (calibration pending)
ctl_entries - 8 todo-calibrate: how many certificate authorities the European Certificate Trust List holds is a deployment fact, not a clause (calibration pending)
ca_crl_entries - 1 todo-calibrate: how many certificate authorities are revoked is a deployment fact (calibration pending)
report_pre_processing - true standard: ETSI TS 103 759 §4: pre-processing before collection is optional, which is why this is a flag and not a stage every run emits
authority_hw_profile - hw-profile/i9-11950h-wolfssl datasheet: wolfSSL benchmark, Intel i9-11950H: the authorities' ECDSA cost; no EA or AA transaction rate is published
station_hw_profile - hw-profile/cohda-mk6-botan datasheet: Cohda MK6 with Botan: a station's cryptography runs on its on-board unit, not on a server

Equations.

  • passive eviction bound: lag ≤ preload_horizon + ticket_validity (EUCP §7.2.1: ≤ 3 months + 1 week)

Assumptions.

  • There is no per-vehicle CRL: TS 102 941 §6.1.4 NOTE 4 states that revocation of authorization tickets is not possible because passive revocation is preferred. The CA-only CRL of §6.3.5 is the one active list, and it revokes authorities.
  • The Misbehaviour Authority decides on the first report. Report clustering, windowed correlation and the investigation are the MaPipeline family's, and a second threshold here would give a run two answers to 'was this device reported enough'.
  • Pre-processing is charged as a second pass through the authority's service queue with a zero delay: TS 103 759 §4 makes it optional and gives it no duration, so the work is charged and no latency is invented.
  • A trust-list run distributes to one station, as the SCMS plug-in's CRL distribution does: the stages from downloaded onwards are per-node, and a run that fanned out to a fleet would no longer match its own declaration.

Limitations.

  • Message framing is hand-written sums of cited field sizes, not encoder output, because build decision D5 defers the ETSI PKI ASN.1 on an inner subtyping construct at EtsiTs102941MessagesItss.asn:105:6. Every certificate inside those messages is sized by the real COER encoder, so the framing is the modelled part and it is the smaller part of every message that carries a certificate.
  • The RSU delta-CTL broadcast path of Annex D.3 — single hop, no segmentation, stations re-broadcast unmodified — is not a flow here: the air interface is the node and radio crates', and v2xw-node's roadside runtime carries the role. What this crate models is the CPOC-to-station path.
  • The manufacturer SOC interface and the Distribution Centre are declared as roles and have no flows: TS 102 941 §6.2.2's canonical-key bootstrap happens before enrolment and the DC is a cache in front of the CPOC.
  • Re-enrolment reuses the enrolment flow rather than modelling the 'outer signature with the current EC' variant of §6.2.3.2, which changes which key signs and not the shape of the exchange.

Sources.

  • standard: ETSI TS 102 941 V2.2.1 (2022-11)
  • standard: ETSI TS 102 940 V2.1.1 (2021-07)
  • standard: ETSI TS 103 097 V2.1.1 (2021-10)
  • standard: European Commission, Certificate Policy for Deployment and Operation of European C-ITS, Release 1.1, 2018-06

protocol/scms/camp¶

The CAMP SCMS / IEEE 1609.2.1 credential-management protocol: nine online backend roles as queued nodes, butterfly-key pseudonym provisioning, two-Linkage-Authority identity resolution, and linked-CRL revocation that is forward-only by construction.
