repo_url_bytes B 64 todo-calibrate: no published value in the design set (calibration pending)
batch_container_bytes B 76 todo-calibrate: no published value in the design set (calibration pending)
report_payload_bytes B 524 standard: SCMS Manager Misbehavior Report Specification v1.0 §1-3 and ASN.1 v1.1, encoded by mbr::encode_report: the reference report (1VInconsistent, two signed BSMs and the accused's certificate) is 524 B COER, pinned by tests/mbr.rs. A real report is charged its own bytes
linkage_chain_identifier_bytes B 16 todo-calibrate: no published value in the design set (calibration pending)
etsi_subject_attributes_bytes B 64 todo-calibrate: no published value in the design set (calibration pending)

Equations.

  • butterfly expansion: B(i,j) = A + f1(ck,(i,j))·G; Q(i,j) = P + f2(ek,(i,j))·G; b'(i,j) = a + f1(ck,(i,j)) + c
  • linkage value: lv(i,j) = plv1(i,j) XOR plv2(i,j), plv_x(i,j) = AES_{ls_x(i)}(la_id_x ‖ j) truncated to 72 bits
  • seed chain: ls_x(i) = H(la_id_x ‖ ls_x(i-1)) truncated to 128 bits; a CRL entry publishes ls_x(i_rev) and never ls_x(0), which is what makes revocation forward-only
  • CRL processing cost: per entry per i-period: 2 SHA-256 chain steps and 2·jmax AES blocks
  • link delay: t = latency + 8·bytes / bandwidth, in integer nanoseconds

Assumptions.

  • Root CA, the Policy Generator and the electors are offline, as in the CAMP PoC, so they place no messages on any link.
  • Service times are deterministic sums of per-operation costs plus a fixed overhead: the medium tier without its stochastic variant, whose log-normal parameters the design leaves todo-calibrate.
  • SHA-256 and AES-128 have no cost anchor on any hardware profile, so their operations are counted and charged zero time rather than given an invented rate.

Limitations.

  • Availability (entity outages) is a declared hook and is not modelled: leaves the two-state Markov parameters todo-calibrate.
  • Epidemic V2V CRL exchange and RSU-relayed provisioning are not built. CRL distribution has both paths: the cellular fetch from the CRL Store and the roadside broadcast, the latter as a point-to-point link at the OFDM rate. Contention, fragmentation and the loss process on that link belong to the radio crates, so a scenario that needs them drives the broadcast through the engine's PHY rather than through this link.
  • The CRL expansion cost is counted and charged zero time, because no hardware profile publishes a SHA-256 or AES-128 anchor. A device's processed and enforced stages therefore share their downloaded instant, and the expansion's operation counts — not its duration — are what the metric reports.
  • The P2PCD and certificate-attachment policies are declared as parameters here and enforced by the envelope in v2xw-sec, not by this crate.

Ignores.

  • Certificate encryption is modelled by its size and its cost, not by encrypting: the crypto-mode equivalence guarantee (I-S1) makes the two indistinguishable in the event log.

Sources.

  • paper: Brecht et al., 'A Security Credential Management System for V2X Communications', IEEE T-ITS 2018, arXiv:1802.05323
  • standard: CAMP VSC5, SCMS PoC Implementation EE Requirements and Specifications, Release 1.1, 2016-05-04
  • paper: USDOT ITS-JPO, SCMS Technical Primer, FHWA-JPO-19-775, 2019
  • standard: CAMP VSC5, SCMS PoC Implementation EE Requirements and Specifications Supporting SCMS Software Release 1.2.2, 2016-11-15
  • standard: SCMS Manager, IEEE 1609.2.1 Profile v1.0.3, 2023-03-15
  • standard: SCMS Manager, Provider Requirements v1.1, 2024-08-09
  • standard: SCMS Manager, EE Certificate Rollover (Re-Enrollment) Technical Standard v1.0
  • standard: SCMS Manager, Misbehavior Report and Application Specification v1.0
  • standard: Every default with its status and source: docs/reference/scms-constants.md (WP-D3)
  • standard: IEEE 1609.2-2016 and 1609.2a-2017
  • standard: ETSI TS 103 759 V2.1.1 (misbehaviour report payload)
  • paper: Simplicio et al., ACPC, ePrint 2018/324 §2 (CRL expansion cost)

safety-app¶

safety-app/eebl-vsca¶

Emergency electronic brake light, receiving half: estimates a peer's deceleration from its claimed speeds, applies the TS 103 759 plausibility window and the forward-collision geometry, and warns when a relevant peer ahead is braking at or above 0.4 g.

version 0.1.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
decel_threshold_mps2 m/s2 3.92 standard: SAE J2945/1 BSM Part II emergency-brake event flag at 0.4 g = 3.92 m/s² [ and §11, both VERIFIED]
estimate_window_ms ms 500 standard: ETSI TS 103 759 V2.2.1: the EEBL plausibility gate requires the preceding vehicle's deceleration to be positive within 500 ms before the claimed detectionTime []
hold_ms ms 500 todo-calibrate: no source gives how long a brake-event warning stands after the last supporting claim (calibration pending)

Equations.

  • deceleration estimate: a = (v(t₁) − v(t₂)) / (t₂ − t₁) over two consecutive claims, with 0 < t₂ − t₁ ≤ estimate_window_ms and both times the sender's own

Assumptions.

  • A receiver at this fidelity has no brake-event flag: BSM Part II is not encoded ( marks its cadence UNVERIFIED) and a CAM's basic vehicle container carries none, so the deceleration is differentiated from claimed speeds.
  • The interval is measured between the two claimed generation times, so a peer with a drifting clock produces a wrong deceleration — which is the real consequence and the one a clock attack should have.

Limitations.

  • A two-point difference of a quantised speed is noisy: a CAM's speed is reported to 0.01 m/s and a 100 ms interval turns one quantum into 0.1 m/s², so the estimate is usable at the 0.4 g threshold and not below it.
  • The host-side half — broadcasting a self-generated brake event — is the generator's, not this application's.

Sources.

  • paper: CAMP VSC-A final report, DOT HS 811 492A (application definition)
  • standard: ETSI TS 103 759 V2.2.1 §4-7

safety-app/fcw-vsca¶

Forward-collision warning over the neighbour table: warns of an impending rear-end collision with a peer ahead in the same direction, and emits the belief-side time to collision and required deceleration.

version 0.1.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
ttc_warn_s s 1.5 todo-calibrate: marks the FCW time-to-collision threshold UNVERIFIED (calibration pending)
ttc_imminent_s s 0.75 todo-calibrate: no source gives the boundary between a warning and an imminent-collision alert (calibration pending)
corridor_half_width_m m 0.9 todo-calibrate: marks the VSC-A zone geometry TODO: calibrate (calibration pending)
heading_tolerance_rad rad 0.7853981633974483 todo-calibrate: no source gives the heading agreement that makes a peer 'same direction' (calibration pending)
range_m m 150.0 todo-calibrate: the evaluation horizon is an implementation choice, not a published one (calibration pending)

Equations.

  • time to collision: ttc = gap / (v_ego − v_peer·cos(Δheading)), evaluated only while closing
  • required deceleration: a_req = closing² / (2·gap) — the rate the ego would need, not the rate the peer was observed to apply
  • same-direction corridor: |(p_peer − p_ego)·l̂| ≤ corridor_half_width_m and |Δheading| ≤ heading_tolerance_rad, with l̂ the ego's lateral unit vector

Assumptions.

  • A peer's lane is unknown to a receiver, so 'same lane' is a corridor in the ego's own heading frame.
  • Closing speed uses the peer's claimed speed projected onto the ego's heading; the peer's own acceleration is not in a CAM's or a BSM's mandatory content at this tier.

Limitations.

  • Gaps are between reported reference points, so vehicle length is not accounted for and every time to collision is correspondingly optimistic — the same limitation v2xw-metrics records for the ground-truth ttc_min.
  • required_decel_mps2 is the deceleration the ego would need, not FHWA-HRT-08-051's DRAC, which is the deceleration the second vehicle was observed to apply. The two are not interchangeable and the field name says which this is.
  • Every numeric trigger is uncalibrated: 's FCW row has no verified number in it.

Sources.

  • paper: CAMP VSC-A final report, DOT HS 811 492A (application definition)
  • paper: FHWA-HRT-08-051, Surrogate Safety Assessment Model (time to collision, 1.5 s conflict threshold)

safety-app/ima-vsca¶

Intersection movement assist over the neighbour table: extrapolates both paths at constant velocity, warns when the ego and a crossing peer reach the conflict point together, and emits the belief-side post-encroachment time.

version 0.1.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
tti_warn_s s 4.0 todo-calibrate: marks the IMA time-to-intersection threshold UNVERIFIED and notes it is 'not in the accessible report' (calibration pending)
pet_warn_s s 1.5 todo-calibrate: no source gives the post-encroachment time at which an intersection application should warn records the PET threshold itself as UNVERIFIED (calibration pending)
pet_conflict_s s 5.0 todo-calibrate: records the PET threshold as UNVERIFIED (calibration pending)
moving_threshold_mps m/s 0.5 todo-calibrate: no source gives the speed below which a crossing path is undefined (calibration pending)
range_m m 150.0 todo-calibrate: the evaluation horizon is an implementation choice (calibration pending)

Equations.

  • conflict point: solve p_ego + t·v_ego = p_peer + s·v_peer by Cramer's rule; a determinant below 1e-9 is treated as parallel
  • post-encroachment time: pet = |t_ego − t_peer|, the interval between one road user leaving the conflict point and the other arriving

Assumptions.

  • Both paths are straight and both speeds constant over the prediction horizon, which is the constant-velocity extrapolation VSC-A's own IMA description assumes for a stop-sign-controlled or uncontrolled intersection.
  • The intersection's geometry is not read: the conflict point is where the two reported paths cross, which needs no map and therefore works on a node that has none.

Limitations.

  • Road users are points: neither vehicle's extent beyond the conflict point is accounted for, so the post-encroachment time is an over-estimate of the safety margin in exactly the way v2xw-metrics records for its cell-based pet.
  • A curving approach is a straight line here, so a vehicle turning into the ego's path is seen late.
  • Every numeric trigger is uncalibrated: 's IMA row has no verified number in it.

Sources.

  • paper: CAMP VSC-A final report, DOT HS 811 492A (application definition)
  • paper: FHWA-HRT-08-051, Surrogate Safety Assessment Model (post-encroachment time)

verification-policy¶

verification-policy/on-demand¶

Verify only the messages a safety application marks relevant; deliver the rest to the applications flagged as unverified.

version 0.1.0; tiers medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
relevance_threshold ratio 0.5 [0.0, 1.0] todo-calibrate: the relevance score above which a message is verified (calibration pending)

Limitations.

  • A message delivered unverified is delivered: an application acting on it is acting on an unauthenticated claim, and the unverified_ratio telemetry field is what makes that visible.

Sources.

  • paper: Krishnan & Weimerskirch, 'Verify-on-Demand', SAE Int. J. Passenger Cars - Mech. Syst. 4:536-546, 2011 [, cited via ePrint 2022/133 ref. 7]

verification-policy/prioritized¶

Order the verification queue by application relevance and by how close the sender claims to be; drop the oldest entry when the queue overflows.

version 0.1.0; tiers medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
horizon_m m 300.0 [1.0, 10000.0] todo-calibrate: distance beyond which proximity adds no priority (calibration pending)

Equations.

  • priority: priority = round(1000 * relevance) + round(1000 * max(0, 1 - d/horizon)), where d is the distance from the node's believed position to the claimed one

Assumptions.

  • Distance is computed from the claimed position, because that is all a receiver has before verification — which means an attacker can raise its own priority by claiming to be close, and that is a real property of the policy rather than a modelling error.

Sources.

  • todo-calibrate: (prioritized policy: 'priority by relevance score and distance; oldest-drop when the queue exceeds queue_depth')

verification-policy/verify-all¶

Verify every received SPDU in arrival order; drop on queue overflow. Nothing unverified reaches an application.

version 0.1.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Assumptions.

  • An overflowing queue refuses the newcomer rather than shedding the oldest, so the messages that survive are the ones that arrived while there was room.

Sources.

  • standard: IEEE 1609.2-2022 clause 5.2: the default receiver behaviour, against which the two relaxations below are measured

vru¶

vru/pedestrian/social-force¶

Pedestrians by the Helbing and Molnár force model: a relaxation towards the desired direction, an elliptical pairwise repulsion that looks one step ahead, a border repulsion from the kerbs of the walkable lane, and vehicles as borders with the SUMO crossing-gap threshold. Pedestrians walk the world's sidewalk and crossing lanes.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium; hosting in-process; draws random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
desired_speed m/s {"mean": 1.34, "std": 0.26} paper: Helbing & Molnár 1995, citing Henderson 1971/1974
max_speed_factor 1 1.3 paper: Helbing & Molnár, "Social force model for pedestrian dynamics", Phys. Rev. E 51, 4282 (1995); arXiv:cond-mat/9805244
tau s 0.5 paper: Helbing & Molnár, "Social force model for pedestrian dynamics", Phys. Rev. E 51, 4282 (1995); arXiv:cond-mat/9805244
V0 m²/s² 2.1 paper: Helbing & Molnár, "Social force model for pedestrian dynamics", Phys. Rev. E 51, 4282 (1995); arXiv:cond-mat/9805244
sigma m 0.3 paper: Helbing & Molnár, "Social force model for pedestrian dynamics", Phys. Rev. E 51, 4282 (1995); arXiv:cond-mat/9805244
U0 m²/s² 10.0 paper: Helbing & Molnár, "Social force model for pedestrian dynamics", Phys. Rev. E 51, 4282 (1995); arXiv:cond-mat/9805244
R m 0.2 paper: Helbing & Molnár, "Social force model for pedestrian dynamics", Phys. Rev. E 51, 4282 (1995); arXiv:cond-mat/9805244
step_width s 2.0 paper: Helbing & Molnár, "Social force model for pedestrian dynamics", Phys. Rev. E 51, 4282 (1995); arXiv:cond-mat/9805244
field_of_view deg 200.0 paper: Helbing & Molnár, "Social force model for pedestrian dynamics", Phys. Rev. E 51, 4282 (1995); arXiv:cond-mat/9805244
behind_weight 1 0.5 paper: Helbing & Molnár, "Social force model for pedestrian dynamics", Phys. Rev. E 51, 4282 (1995); arXiv:cond-mat/9805244
jmCrossingGap m 10.0 code: SUMO jmCrossingGap default 10 m [, §B13]
elliptical - true paper: Helbing & Molnár, "Social force model for pedestrian dynamics", Phys. Rev. E 51, 4282 (1995); arXiv:cond-mat/9805244
jaywalk_probability 1 0.0 code: a switch, off by default: no calibrated rate of crossing against the signal is cited, so none is assumed
max_wait_s s 120.0 code: an engineering bound, two 60 s cycles, so a pedestrian at a crosswalk that never shows walk does not wait forever; not a behavioural value
kerb_margin m 0.3 code: where a waiting pedestrian stands, back from the end of the pavement lane
interaction_radius m 10.0 code: an engineering bound: with σ = 0.3 m the repulsion beyond a few metres is numerically zero
speed_law - helbing1995 paper: Knoblauch, Pietrucha & Nitzburg, Field studies of pedestrian walking speed and start-up time, TRR 1538 (1996): means 4.95 / 4.11 ft/s, 15th percentiles 4.09 / 3.19 ft/s for pedestrians under 65 / 65 and over; σ derived assuming normality
older_share 1 0.15 code: a choice near New York City's share of residents 65 and over (about one in six); no age count of Midtown's sidewalks was read
red_crossing_share 1 0.0 paper: calibrated against Basch, Ethan, Zybert & Basch, Pedestrian behavior at five dangerous and busy Manhattan intersections, J. Community Health 40:789 (2015): of 21,760 pedestrians, about 2,319 crossings (10.6 %) began on don't-walk
startup_median_s s 0.0 code: a choice below the HCM's 3.2 s pedestrian platoon start-up time; Knoblauch et al. 1996 measured start-up time but their values could not be read
gap_margin_s s 2.0 standard: HCM pedestrian critical headway t_c = L/S_p + t_s (two-way stop-controlled pedestrian mode); t_s, the start-up and end clearance time, is to be measured locally and is not re-verified here
crowd_slowing - false paper: Weidmann 1993, Transporttechnik der Fussgänger (ETH IVT Schriftenreihe 90): v = v0·(1 − exp(−1.913·(1/ρ − 1/5.4))) (secondary: the parameters as the pedestrian-flow literature quotes them); local density over a 5 m circle clipped to the walkway, a choice
group_shares 1 [1.0, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0] paper: Moussaïd, Perozo, Garnier, Helbing & Theraulaz, The walking behaviour of pedestrian social groups and its impact on crowd dynamics, PLoS ONE 5:e10047 (2010): more than half of pedestrians walk in groups on a workday; the per-size split is a choice matching that share
midblock - {"corridor_width_m": 1.0, "diagonal_max_deg": 35.0, "driver_yield_probability... code: crate::vru::midblock: the rate, the stopped-traffic factor and the driver yield probability are choices; the gap rule is the HCM critical headway, lane by lane
fluctuation m/s² 0.0 todo-calibrate: Helbing & Molnár write "+ fluctuations" and records no magnitude (calibration pending)

Equations.

  • acceleration: dv/dt = (v0·e − v)/τ + Σ f_αβ + Σ f_αB + fluctuations
  • pedestrian repulsion: f_αβ = −∇_r V(b), V(b) = V0·exp(−b/σ), b = ½√((|r| + |r − y|)² − |y|²), y = v_β·Δt·e_β the closed-form gradient ∇_r b = ((|r| + |r−y|)/(4b))·(r̂ + (r−y)ˆ) is the standard reduction and is transcribed, not quoted: records the parameter table and the model's form, not this expression. elliptical = false falls back to the isotropic b = |r|.
  • border repulsion: f_αB = (U0/R)·exp(−d/R) away from the border
  • field of view: w = 1 if e·f ≥ |f|·cos(φ), else c 2φ = 200°, c = 0.5

Assumptions.

  • Pedestrians walk sidewalk and crossing lanes of the world; the lane's own width gives the two borders.
  • The lateral offset is clamped to the walkable half-width at the end of every step. The border potential is soft, so a finite step could otherwise cross a kerb; the clamp makes "pedestrians stay on walkable lanes" an invariant rather than a tendency.
  • A pedestrian about to step onto a crossing waits at the kerb unless its pedestrian signal shows walk (or it has none) and no vehicle is on the crosswalk or too close to yield (UVC §11-502(b), §11-203; the kinematic test of vru::crosswalk). One already on a crossing walks on. The wait is a desired speed that falls to zero over the last 2 m, and the kerb is a hard stop.
  • Crossing against the signal: the legacy switch jaywalk_probability (off by default) draws once per pedestrian per crosswalk; the observed behaviour instead makes red_crossing_share of pedestrians violators, who start on flashing or steady don't-walk only into a gap the HCM critical headway t_c = L/S_p + t_s accepts (L the crosswalk, S_p their own speed). Either still requires no vehicle hazard.
  • Mid-block crossing (crate::vru::midblock): a constant hazard per metre of eligible sidewalk walked, raised beside stopped traffic; straight or diagonal; the pedestrian waits at the kerb for a lane-by-lane (rolling) gap and walks a straight path to the far sidewalk, off every lane.
  • Groups (observed behaviour): a group shares one walk, starts side by side, walks at its slowest member's pace and takes its leader's compliance; a follower crosses mid-block where its leader does.
  • Every pedestrian reads the start-of-step positions of the others and of the vehicles, so the update is the same Jacobi update the vehicles use.

Limitations.

  • No jam state (§2.5 ignores it). A group is held together only by its shared walk and pace, not by an attraction term.
  • A pedestrian crossing mid-block walks a straight line at its desired speed and does not stop or run in the carriageway; the vehicles stop for it.
  • The attraction term of the 1995 model (shop windows, companions) is not implemented: it is optional in the paper and has no cited parameters.

Ignores.

  • Group behaviour, jam states and SUMO's stripe discretisation (medium relative to high).

Sources.

  • paper: Helbing & Molnár, "Social force model for pedestrian dynamics", Phys. Rev. E 51, 4282 (1995); arXiv:cond-mat/9805244

world¶

world/source/opendrive¶

Reads an ASAM OpenDRIVE file by converting it with netconvert --opendrive-files and importing the result, with the converter's own log copied into the provenance.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
pedestrian_layer - true code: : SUMO net.xml is the interchange format, and the table of what a SUMO import preserves and loses
roads_licence - UNSTATED code: : "the format has no separate license". The network's data licence depends on where it came from, so the importer claims none unless the caller states one.
default_lane_width_m m 3.2 todo-calibrate: sumo-net default_lane_width_m (calibration pending)
default_speed_mps m/s 13.89 todo-calibrate: sumo-net default_speed_mps (calibration pending)
signal_head_height_m m 5.0 todo-calibrate: sumo-net signal_head_height_m (calibration pending)

Equations.

  • request bit order: bit(s, link) = s[len(s) − 1 − link] SUMO writes a <request>'s response and foes strings with the highest link index first, so link 0 is the last character. Reading them left to right imports a junction's right of way exactly backwards.
  • internal chain: one connector lane per movement: the via lane's shape, concatenated with every internal lane the chain continues through
  • junction area: convex hull of the file's own shape, the junction's position and every lane end that meets it

Assumptions.

  • A net.xml's coordinates are Cartesian metres in the network's own frame, so the importer translates and never projects.
  • Lane index 0 is the rightmost in the direction of travel, which is SUMO's own convention and this project's (vwp-v1 §4.3).
  • A banned turn is the absence of a <connection>: netconvert has already applied every turn restriction by the time it writes the network.
  • A <request>'s response names the links this one gives way to, and every response is also a foe. A response without a foe is reported.
  • allow beats disallow when a lane states both, and a lane that states neither admits every class, which is SUMO's own default.
  • SUMO is installed: the conversion is netconvert's, not this crate's, and the world is only as good as that converter.

Limitations.

  • Buildings, land use and material classes are not in the format, so a SUMO world has none; the propagation environment class is the caller's default for the whole world rather than defaulted from lane density, which §1.1 records as TODO: calibrate.
  • An internal junction — the waiting position inside a large intersection — has no equivalent in this model, so a movement's chain of internal lanes is concatenated into one connector and the count is reported. A vehicle therefore cannot wait inside the junction.
  • A &lt;tlLogic&gt; that controls links at several junctions becomes one plan per junction with the same cycle and phases. The programme's identity is lost, so a coordinated corridor's junctions are no longer visibly one controller.
  • A per-connection class restriction cannot be expressed: a Connection carries no class mask, so the movement stays permitted unless it admits no class at all, and the loss is counted.
  • Geometry is not cut at a requested boundary — elements are kept or dropped whole — so a bounded import's extent can exceed the request by one edge.
  • &lt;location projParameter&gt; is not evaluated, so the metre frame stays in SUMO's projection while the geodetic anchor comes from origBoundary. The disagreement between that projection and this crate's local tangent plane is UNVERIFIED.
  • A walking area is an area in SUMO and a polyline here, so its geometry is the area's outline traversed as a path.
  • Everything netconvert itself drops is dropped here, including the pedestrian network where OpenDRIVE does not carry it as lanes and a signal's timing where the file only references it. Its log is the record of what was lost.
  • The intermediate network is written to a file named for the input's SHA-256 and deleted afterwards unless the caller keeps it, so the import is reproducible but not hermetic.

Ignores.

  • &lt;roundabout edges&gt;: only its nodes are read, to set the junction control.
  • Lane acceleration, changeLeft, changeRight, endOffset, customShape and the &lt;neigh&gt; element.
  • Actuated, delay-based and NEMA traffic-light programmes are carried into World::signal_programmes with their phases' minDur/maxDur (and NEMA's name, vehext, yellow, red) and &lt;param&gt; A delay-based programme runs as actuated (delay-based-as-actuated`), and SUMO's induction-loop detectors are not imported: the engine's own detectors call the phases.

Sources.

  • code: SUMO Road Networks documentation: the elements <edge>, <lane>, <junction>, <connection>, <tlLogic>, <request>
  • code: : SUMO net.xml is the interchange format, and the table of what a SUMO import preserves and loses
  • code: netconvert import formats: plain XML, OSM, VISUM, Vissim, OpenDRIVE, MATSim, SUMO, Shapefile, RoboCup, DlrNavteq/GDF

world/source/osm¶

Imports an OpenStreetMap XML extract into a lane-level world: drivable, pedestrian and cycle networks, junctions with connectors and conflict matrices, synthesised fixed-time signals, buildings with sourced heights, land-use zones and a full provenance record.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

cycle_buffer_m m 0.914 standard: NYC DOT Street Design Manual, Bike Lane Table: conventional lane 5-6 ft; one-way protected lane 4 ft minimum plus a 3 ft minimum buffer, 7-8 ft in all (lane 5.5 ft, the midpoint; track 5 ft plus 3 ft, the 8 ft end)
angled_parking_depth_m m 5.5 code: this importer's choice: an 18 ft stall depth for diagonal and perpendicular parking, which the Manhattan extract does not have
split_by_lanes - true standard: Webster's method shares green by critical flow ratio; with no flows in an OSM extract the lanes on each phase's widest approach stand in for its capacity need (HCM 2016 Ch. 19 saturation flow is per lane)
progression_speed_mps m/s null standard: null = each link's speed limit. NYC DOT '25 MPH Signal Retiming' (2014 onward) sets avenue progression to the 25 mph limit; NYC DOT's Green Wave (press release 2026-05-07) times 1st, 2nd and 8th Avenues and Hudson Street for 15 mph. Manhattan's signals are pre-timed on 90-150 s cycles (NYC DCP 61st Street FEIS ch. 14); 90 s, the low end, is the cycle_s default
pedestrian_walk_s s 7.0 standard: MUTCD 2009 §4E.06: walk interval at least 7 s; pedestrian clearance timed at 3.5 ft/s, which may run through the yellow change and red clearance intervals
pedestrian_speed_mps m/s 1.067 standard: MUTCD 2009 §4E.06: walk interval at least 7 s; pedestrian clearance timed at 3.5 ft/s, which may run through the yellow change and red clearance intervals
height_tag_rule - height dataset: OSM Simple 3D Buildings (height, building:levels, min_height, building:min_level),
building_part_parent_rule - "greatest shared footprint area, at least 0.50 of the part; among outlines co... code: OSM Simple 3D Buildings says a building:part subdivides the building=* outline it lies in, which is a statement about two polygons, so the parent is chosen by clipped overlap area rather than by testing one point of the part. The thresholds are read off the Phase 1 Manhattan extract, where they have nothing near them: of 4 373 parts, every one that has a parent is 0.934 or more covered by it and the runner-up is 0.32 or less, and of the nine parts that more than one outline claims the covering fraction is either 1.0000 to four decimals or 0.9553 or less. The tie-breaks are each forced by a real case: the United Nations Secretariat inside the United Nations Headquarters campus for nesting, Rose Hill against the 156 m² building beside it for subdivision
building_parts_as_max_height - outline height = max(own height, tallest building:part folded into it) code: OSM Simple 3D Buildings says a building:part subdivides its outline, so the structure's top is its tallest part's top. Collapsing the parts to that one number is this importer's simplification: Building has no sub-volume field and the vwp-v1 building record has no min_height column, so a stepped tower is one prism and the setback above a podium is filled in
metres_per_level m 3.0 todo-calibrate: world/source/osm metres_per_level (calibration pending)
default_height_m m 10.0 todo-calibrate: world/source/osm default_height_m (calibration pending)
highway_class_defaults - SUMO osmNetconvert.typ.xml todo-calibrate: world/source/osm highway_class_defaults (calibration pending)
bbox_clip_margin_m m 50.0 todo-calibrate: world/source/osm bbox_clip_margin_m (calibration pending)
roof_spire_fraction - 0.5 todo-calibrate: world/source/osm roof_spire_fraction (calibration pending)
crossing_width_m m 4.0 todo-calibrate: world/source/osm crossing_width_m (calibration pending)
signal_head_height_m m 5.0 todo-calibrate: world/source/osm signal_head_height_m (calibration pending)

Equations.

  • lane offset: d_k = -(W/2 - (k + 0.5) w) from the way centreline, positive to the left of travel, W the carriageway width and k the lane index from the right
  • junction trim: each approach is cut back by r_j = clamp(max incident W/2 (+ 4.2 m stop-line setback at 3+ arms), 1 m, 25 m); then each edge end is pulled back by up to 7 m until every turn has R tan(theta/2) of tangent, R = 6.4 m (AASHTO P)
  • connector: the corner of the two tangent lines rounded into the largest circular arc between the lane ends (AASHTO simple curve), sampled every 6 deg; parallel offset lanes take a reverse curve; motor lane vertices rounded the same way up to R = 50 m
  • amber: per phase group, y = t + v / (2a + 2 G g) with t = 1 s, a = 3 m/s^2, G the approach grade, clamped to [3, 6] s (ITE 2020; MUTCD 2009 4D.26)
  • all-red: per phase group, r = (W + L) / v with W the connector length, L = 6.1 m, at most 6 s (ITE 2020; MUTCD 2009 4D.26)
  • building height: height tag; else building:levels x metres_per_level; else default_height_m; then, where roof:height > roof_spire_fraction x height, cut back to height - roof:height
  • lane width: mean of width:lanes; else width / total lanes; else the lane_width_m option; else the class default

Assumptions.

  • Right-hand traffic: lane 0 is the rightmost in the direction of travel, and a left turn is the movement that crosses opposing traffic.
  • A way's centreline is the centre of its carriageway, so lanes are laid out symmetrically about it.
  • A node shared by two or more ways of the same family is a junction; every other node is shape.
  • Signal plans are invented: the source carries presence only. Every plan is two-phase, pre-timed, uncoordinated (offset 0) and permissive for turns that cross opposing traffic.
  • The ground is flat at z = 0. layer, bridge and tunnel are read and keep two ways from being merged, but do not separate geometry vertically: ways on different levels connect only where they share a node, which they do not.

Limitations.

  • Dual carriageways are imported as two one-way streets, because the sausage-link merge is not implemented; a divided boulevard therefore has two centrelines and a median that is not modelled.
  • A class default speed is jurisdictional, so there is NO default preset: an import is refused until the scenario names one. sumo-german is SUMO's free-flow design speeds and keeps a netconvert comparison reproducible, and is not an urban legal limit; urban-us-nyc is the NYC statutory default. The report and the provenance name the preset, count the lanes that took a default, and warn when those defaults sit far above the limits the source itself states.
  • building:part geometry is not kept, only its HEIGHT. A part inside a building=* outline is folded into the outline so that one structure is one obstacle, and the outline's height becomes the taller of its own and the tallest part inside it (HeightSource::FromParts). The per-part volume is dropped, because the world model has no field for a sub-volume and the vwp-v1 building record has no min_height column to carry one: a stepped tower is therefore ONE prism as tall as its tallest part, so an obstacle model sees the setback above a podium filled in and over-estimates mass at high altitude. A part with no outline over it is kept as a building and counted.
  • A polygon the tags place below ground, or that is a station or public-transport area with no building tag, is not imported at all — not as a zero-height obstacle and not as land use.
  • A driving lane shorter than min_useful_lane_m is kept and counted rather than merged into its neighbour: merging changes the topology a turn restriction and a conflict matrix were built against, and dropping it disconnects whatever is on the other side.
  • A movement whose two lane ends are within a millimetre has no connector lane and therefore no conflict-matrix row, because the matrix is indexed by connector. It is still subject to turn restrictions, and it is counted as zero-length-connector.
  • Footways parallel to a road are imported where they are mapped, and are not snapped to the road; a city that maps sidewalks both ways will have both.
  • Dog-leg junctions are imported as two junctions a few metres apart.
  • A via-way turn restriction is counted and not applied: it spans more than one junction and has no single movement to ban.
  • Two opposing left turns are recorded as conflicting with no precedence, because neither the turn ranking nor priority-to-the-right settles them.

Ignores.

  • Terrain: no DEM is read, and every z is 0.
  • Traffic signal timing, phase counts and coordination, which the source does not carry.
  • Bus lanes, parking lanes, turn pockets and lane widths per lane: every lane of a way gets the same width and access.
  • Public transport routes, barriers, kerbs and traffic calming.

Sources.

  • code: osm2streets README (lane inference and the simplification list),
  • code: SUMO netconvert option defaults (--junctions.join 10 m, --tls.guess-signals 25 m, --tls.cycle.time 90 s, --tls.green.time 31 s, --tls.allred.time 0 s, --tls.yellow.min-decel 3 m/s^2), and §2.3
  • dataset: OSM Simple 3D Buildings (height, building:levels, min_height, building:min_level),
  • standard: FHWA Signal Timing Manual 2008 Ch. 5 (amber 3-6 s, all-red guidance),
  • standard: AASHTO Green Book (9-12 ft lanes; 12 ft on freeways and major arterials) and NACTO Urban Street Design Guide (10-11 ft urban street lanes)
  • standard: New York City citywide 25 mph default speed limit (NYC Local Law 139 of 2014, under NY Vehicle & Traffic Law §1643) and the NY State statutory maximum of 55 mph (NY VTL §1180(b))
  • code: SUMO default vType passenger length 5.0 m: a lane shorter than one vehicle cannot hold one, which is the threshold short-driving-lane is counted against

world/source/procedural-grid¶

A rectangular lattice of two-way streets with signalised or priority junctions, generated deterministically from its parameters: the reference world for tests, calibration runs and the TR 36.885 urban grid scenario.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
cols - 6 code: legacy engine run.py L412-424, preset legacy

block_buildings - false code: legacy engine run.py L412-424, preset legacy
rsu_at_junctions - false code: legacy engine run.py L412-424, preset legacy
speed_limit_mps m/s 13.89 todo-calibrate: procedural-grid speed_limit_mps (calibration pending)
cycle_s s 60.0 todo-calibrate: procedural-grid cycle_s (calibration pending)
amber_s s 3.0 todo-calibrate: procedural-grid amber_s (calibration pending)
ew_green_share - 0.5 standard: Webster (1958), Road Research Technical Paper 39, and HCM 6th ed. ch. 19: effective green is split in proportion to the critical flow ratios; equal demand on both streets gives 0.5. The accepted range 0.1-0.9 leaves each street at least 5.4 s of green at the 60 s default cycle
signal_head_height_m m 5.0 todo-calibrate: procedural-grid signal_head_height_m (calibration pending)
crossing_width_m m 4.0 todo-calibrate: procedural-grid crossing_width_m (calibration pending)
building_height_m m 20.0 todo-calibrate: procedural-grid building_height_m (calibration pending)
rsu_antenna_height_m m 6.0 todo-calibrate: procedural-grid rsu_antenna_height_m (calibration pending)
corner_radius_m m 0.0 standard: AASHTO Green Book 2018 Ch. 9: passenger-car edge radius for a 90° urban turn, 15 ft (4.5 m) — secondary; 0 in the presets, which reproduce reference layouts
pedestrian_lanes - true standard: MUTCD 2009 §4E.06 (walk ≥ 7 s, clearance at 3.5 ft/s, ≥ 3 s buffer) for the pedestrian intervals; §3B.18 crosswalk placement at the corner. The walk network itself is the generator's geometry, not a sourced value
bicycles_on_roads - false standard: UVC §11-1202: a person riding a bicycle upon a roadway has the rights and duties of a driver; off in the presets, which reproduce motor-only reference layouts
rsu_antenna_gain_dbi dBi 5.0 todo-calibrate: procedural-grid rsu_antenna_gain_dbi (calibration pending)

Equations.

  • lane offset: d_k = (n − 0.5 − k) · w, to the right of travel
  • turn geometry: B(t) = (1−t)² P₀ + 2(1−t)t C + t² P₁, C at the intersection of the two tangent lines, sampled at 7 points
  • junction position: (x_i, y_j) = (half + i · block_x, half + j · block_y), half = n · w

Assumptions.

  • Right-hand traffic: lane 0 is the rightmost in the direction of travel, an east-bound lane lies south of its street's centreline.
  • Every street is two-way with the same number of lanes each way, every junction is a right-angled crossroads, and the ground is flat at z = 0.
  • Turning movements are right from the rightmost lane, straight from every lane, left from the leftmost. No U-turns are generated.
  • Signalisation, when on, is a two-phase fixed-time plan with permissive left turns and no coordination between junctions (offset 0 everywhere).
  • A procedural world has no real location: its origin is (0, 0) and its projection is nominal.

Limitations.

  • A perfect lattice is not a city: no dead ends, no one-way streets, no varying block sizes, no dual carriageways, no gradient. Use world/source/osm for a real network.
  • Two opposing left turns are marked as conflicting with no precedence, because neither the turn ranking nor priority-to-the-right settles them.
  • Buildings, when generated, are one box per block at a single invented height, which is a propagation obstacle of the right shape and the wrong size.

Ignores.

  • Sidewalks are a setback for the buildings, not lanes: no pedestrian network is generated.
  • Terrain: the generator produces no DEM, and every z is 0.

Sources.

  • code: legacy engine run.py L412-424, preset legacy
  • standard: 3GPP TR 36.885 Table A.1.2-1, preset tr36885-urban

world/source/procedural-radial¶

The classic radial city: spokes from a central plaza crossed by concentric ring roads, generated deterministically from its parameters.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
arms - 6 code: legacy engine roads.py L683-702 spider_graph(arms, rings, block), with arms/rings/block from grid_w/grid_h/grid_block_m
rings - 6 code: legacy engine roads.py L683-702 spider_graph(arms, rings, block), with arms/rings/block from grid_w/grid_h/grid_block_m
ring_spacing_m m 120.0 code: legacy engine roads.py L683-702 spider_graph(arms, rings, block), with arms/rings/block from grid_w/grid_h/grid_block_m
lanes_per_direction - 1 code: legacy engine roads.py L683-702 spider_graph(arms, rings, block), with arms/rings/block from grid_w/grid_h/grid_block_m
lane_width_m m 3.5 code: legacy engine roads.py L683-702 spider_graph(arms, rings, block), with arms/rings/block from grid_w/grid_h/grid_block_m
dropout - 0.0 code: legacy engine roads.py L683-702 spider_graph(arms, rings, block), with arms/rings/block from grid_w/grid_h/grid_block_m
signalised - false code: legacy engine roads.py L683-702 spider_graph(arms, rings, block), with arms/rings/block from grid_w/grid_h/grid_block_m
netgenerate_spider.arms - 8 code: legacy/reference/sumo/mapgen.py L234-241: netgenerate --spider --spider.arm-number --spider.circle-number --spider.space-radius 100 --no-turnarounds --default.lanenumber 2, keys such as spider_8a4c
netgenerate_spider.ring_spacing_m m 100.0 code: legacy/reference/sumo/mapgen.py L234-241: netgenerate --spider --spider.arm-number --spider.circle-number --spider.space-radius 100 --no-turnarounds --default.lanenumber 2, keys such as spider_8a4c
speed_limit_mps m/s 13.89 todo-calibrate: procedural-radial speed_limit_mps (calibration pending)
cycle_s s 60.0 todo-calibrate: procedural-radial cycle_s (calibration pending)
amber_s s 3.0 todo-calibrate: procedural-radial amber_s (calibration pending)
signal_head_height_m m 5.0 todo-calibrate: procedural-radial signal_head_height_m (calibration pending)
junction_radius_factor - 1.5 todo-calibrate: procedural-radial junction_radius_factor (calibration pending)

Equations.

  • ring node position: (x, y) = (span + r·s·cos(2πa/arms), span + r·s·sin(2πa/arms)), span = rings·s
  • ring chord length: L = 2·r·s·sin(π/arms) — the polygon side, not the arc
  • dropout selection: ring edge i is removed when floor((i+1)·dropout) > floor(i·dropout)

Assumptions.

  • Right-hand traffic: lane 0 is the rightmost in the direction of travel.
  • Every street is two-way with the same number of lanes each way, and the ground is flat at z = 0.
  • Ring roads are chords through the ring nodes, so ring r is a regular arms-gon rather than a circle.
  • Turning movements are right from the kerb lane, straight on from every lane, left from the median lane; no turnarounds are generated, which is netgenerate's --no-turnarounds.
  • The dropout selection is deterministic decimation. The legacy engine drew the removed edges at random; this generator does not, so that a radial world needs no seed and is a pure function of its parameters.

Limitations.

  • The centre is a single junction with arms approaches. At eight arms and two lanes each way its connectors are geometrically crowded: a real radial city has a roundabout or a plaza there, and this generator has neither.
  • A chord ring with few arms is visibly a polygon, and its junction angles are the polygon's, not a circle's.
  • No buildings, no crossings, no sidewalks and no RSU sites: the grid generator offers those, this one does not.

Ignores.

  • Terrain: every z is 0 until a DEM is attached (see crate::dem).

Sources.

  • code: legacy engine roads.py L683-702 spider_graph(arms, rings, block), with arms/rings/block from grid_w/grid_h/grid_block_m
  • code: legacy/reference/sumo/mapgen.py L234-241: netgenerate --spider --spider.arm-number --spider.circle-number --spider.space-radius 100 --no-turnarounds --default.lanenumber 2, keys such as spider_8a4c

world/source/procedural-random¶

An irregular road network grown from one junction by random attachment: oblique approaches, three-arm junctions, cul-de-sacs and uneven blocks, reproducible from its seed.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
iterations - 150 code: legacy/reference/sumo/mapgen.py L242-249: netgenerate --rand --rand.iterations N --rand.min-distance 80 --rand.max-distance 250 --no-turnarounds --default.lanenumber 2, keys such as rand_150
min_distance_m m 80.0 code: legacy/reference/sumo/mapgen.py L242-249: netgenerate --rand --rand.iterations N --rand.min-distance 80 --rand.max-distance 250 --no-turnarounds --default.lanenumber 2, keys such as rand_150
max_distance_m m 250.0 code: legacy/reference/sumo/mapgen.py L242-249: netgenerate --rand --rand.iterations N --rand.min-distance 80 --rand.max-distance 250 --no-turnarounds --default.lanenumber 2, keys such as rand_150
lanes_per_direction - 2 code: legacy/reference/sumo/mapgen.py L242-249: netgenerate --rand --rand.iterations N --rand.min-distance 80 --rand.max-distance 250 --no-turnarounds --default.lanenumber 2, keys such as rand_150
lane_width_m m 3.5 code: legacy engine run.py L412-424 lane_width_m, preset legacy
seed - 1 code: mapgen.py L244: the _sN suffix of a procedural map key, default seed 1
min_angle_deg deg 45.0 todo-calibrate: procedural-random min_angle_deg (calibration pending)
tries_per_iteration - 50 todo-calibrate: procedural-random tries_per_iteration (calibration pending)
max_radius_m m null todo-calibrate: procedural-random max_radius_m (calibration pending)
speed_limit_mps m/s 13.89 todo-calibrate: procedural-random speed_limit_mps (calibration pending)
cycle_s s 60.0 todo-calibrate: procedural-random cycle_s (calibration pending)
amber_s s 3.0 todo-calibrate: procedural-random amber_s (calibration pending)
signal_head_height_m m 5.0 todo-calibrate: procedural-random signal_head_height_m (calibration pending)
junction_radius_factor - 1.5 todo-calibrate: procedural-random junction_radius_factor (calibration pending)

Equations.

  • candidate position: p = p_base + d·(cos θ, sin θ), θ ~ U[0, 2π), d ~ U[min_distance, max_distance]
  • loop closing: a candidate within min_distance of an existing junction becomes an edge to the nearest such junction instead of a new one
  • angle rule: |normalise(θ_new − θ_existing)| >= min_angle at both ends

Assumptions.

  • Right-hand traffic: lane 0 is the rightmost in the direction of travel.
  • Every street is two-way with the same number of lanes each way, straight between its two junctions, and the ground is flat at z = 0.
  • A junction is wherever two streets meet; the growth never creates a crossing without a junction, because a crossing candidate is rejected outright.
  • Turning movements are right from the kerb lane, straight on from every lane, left from the median lane; no turnarounds are generated, which is netgenerate's --no-turnarounds.

Limitations.

  • This is netgenerate --rand's idea, not its algorithm: SUMO's own growth rule, its option defaults and therefore its networks are not reproduced, and a world from this generator is not comparable with one from netgenerate.
  • The growth is unbounded unless max_radius_m is set, so the extent of a 150-iteration network is a draw rather than a parameter.
  • Loop closing is the only source of cycles, so a low min_distance_m gives a network that is nearly a tree and routes badly. The connectivity is reported, not controlled.
  • No buildings, no crossings, no sidewalks and no RSU sites.

Ignores.

  • Terrain: every z is 0 until a DEM is attached (see crate::dem).

Sources.

  • code: legacy/reference/sumo/mapgen.py L242-249: netgenerate --rand --rand.iterations N --rand.min-distance 80 --rand.max-distance 250 --no-turnarounds --default.lanenumber 2, keys such as rand_150

world/source/sumo-net¶

Reads a SUMO net.xml into the same lane-level world the OSM importer produces: edges, lanes, junctions, connections, internal connectors, right-of-way matrices, traffic-light programmes and crossings, with a counted anomaly wherever the two formats disagree.

version 1.0.0; tiers abstract, medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
pedestrian_layer - true code: : SUMO net.xml is the interchange format, and the table of what a SUMO import preserves and loses
roads_licence - UNSTATED code: : "the format has no separate license". The network's data licence depends on where it came from, so the importer claims none unless the caller states one.
default_lane_width_m m 3.2 todo-calibrate: sumo-net default_lane_width_m (calibration pending)
default_speed_mps m/s 13.89 todo-calibrate: sumo-net default_speed_mps (calibration pending)
signal_head_height_m m 5.0 todo-calibrate: sumo-net signal_head_height_m (calibration pending)

Equations.

  • request bit order: bit(s, link) = s[len(s) − 1 − link] SUMO writes a <request>'s response and foes strings with the highest link index first, so link 0 is the last character. Reading them left to right imports a junction's right of way exactly backwards.
  • internal chain: one connector lane per movement: the via lane's shape, concatenated with every internal lane the chain continues through
  • junction area: convex hull of the file's own shape, the junction's position and every lane end that meets it

Assumptions.

  • A net.xml's coordinates are Cartesian metres in the network's own frame, so the importer translates and never projects.
  • Lane index 0 is the rightmost in the direction of travel, which is SUMO's own convention and this project's (vwp-v1 §4.3).
  • A banned turn is the absence of a <connection>: netconvert has already applied every turn restriction by the time it writes the network.
  • A <request>'s response names the links this one gives way to, and every response is also a foe. A response without a foe is reported.
  • allow beats disallow when a lane states both, and a lane that states neither admits every class, which is SUMO's own default.

Limitations.

  • Buildings, land use and material classes are not in the format, so a SUMO world has none; the propagation environment class is the caller's default for the whole world rather than defaulted from lane density, which §1.1 records as TODO: calibrate.
  • An internal junction — the waiting position inside a large intersection — has no equivalent in this model, so a movement's chain of internal lanes is concatenated into one connector and the count is reported. A vehicle therefore cannot wait inside the junction.
  • A &lt;tlLogic&gt; that controls links at several junctions becomes one plan per junction with the same cycle and phases. The programme's identity is lost, so a coordinated corridor's junctions are no longer visibly one controller.
  • A per-connection class restriction cannot be expressed: a Connection carries no class mask, so the movement stays permitted unless it admits no class at all, and the loss is counted.
  • Geometry is not cut at a requested boundary — elements are kept or dropped whole — so a bounded import's extent can exceed the request by one edge.
  • &lt;location projParameter&gt; is not evaluated, so the metre frame stays in SUMO's projection while the geodetic anchor comes from origBoundary. The disagreement between that projection and this crate's local tangent plane is UNVERIFIED.
  • A walking area is an area in SUMO and a polyline here, so its geometry is the area's outline traversed as a path.

Ignores.

  • &lt;roundabout edges&gt;: only its nodes are read, to set the junction control.
  • Lane acceleration, changeLeft, changeRight, endOffset, customShape and the &lt;neigh&gt; element.
  • Actuated, delay-based and NEMA traffic-light programmes are carried into World::signal_programmes with their phases' minDur/maxDur (and NEMA's name, vehext, yellow, red) and &lt;param&gt; A delay-based programme runs as actuated (delay-based-as-actuated`), and SUMO's induction-loop detectors are not imported: the engine's own detectors call the phases.

Sources.

  • code: SUMO Road Networks documentation: the elements <edge>, <lane>, <junction>, <connection>, <tlLogic>, <request>
  • code: : SUMO net.xml is the interchange format, and the table of what a SUMO import preserves and loses

world/terrain/copernicus-glo-30¶

Reads a raster digital elevation model and resamples it bilinearly onto the world's own metre grid, so that the world's ground height comes from measured terrain instead of the z = 0 plane.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
cell_m m 30.0 code: (both DEMs are 30 m posts, so 30 m is the resolution the data carries)
interpolation - bilinear code: ("resampled onto the world grid with bilinear interpolation")
margin_m m 30.0 todo-calibrate: world/terrain/copernicus-glo-30 margin_m (calibration pending)
void_search_cells - 8 todo-calibrate: world/terrain/copernicus-glo-30 void_search_cells (calibration pending)
void_default_m m 0.0 todo-calibrate: world/terrain/copernicus-glo-30 void_default_m (calibration pending)
min_plausible_elevation_m m -500.0 todo-calibrate: world/terrain/copernicus-glo-30 min_plausible_elevation_m (calibration pending)
max_plausible_elevation_m m 9000.0 todo-calibrate: world/terrain/copernicus-glo-30 max_plausible_elevation_m (calibration pending)

Equations.

  • bilinear resampling: h(x, y) = Σ_k h_k · w_k over the four surrounding source samples, w = (1−tx)(1−ty), tx(1−ty), (1−tx)ty, tx·ty
  • void repair: h = Σ_present h_k·w_k / Σ_present w_k, falling back to the nearest non-void sample within void_search_cells
  • srtm tile georeferencing: sample (ix, iy) at (lon0 + ix/(n−1), lat0 + iy/(n−1)) degrees, (lat0, lon0) from the file name, n from the byte count

Assumptions.

  • The raster holds Copernicus COP-DEM-GLO-30 exported as an ASCII grid.
  • Heights are metres above the DEM's own datum, and that datum is taken to be the world's z = 0. Neither DEM's geoid is converted to an ellipsoidal height, and the offset between them (tens of metres in places) is not modelled.
  • A void is missing data, not sea level: it is interpolated or filled from a neighbour, never read as 0 m, unless the search finds nothing at all.

Limitations.

  • The GeoTIFF and DT2 distributions are not parsed; a GeoTIFF DEM must be converted to an ASCII grid first (gdal_translate -of AAIGrid).
  • The vertical datum is not converted, so an absolute height is only as good as the source's datum matching the world's.
  • A geodetic raster is sampled through the world's local tangent plane, so the resampling error grows with distance from the world origin exactly as the projection's does.

Ignores.

  • Slope-aware or bicubic resampling: specifies bilinear and the grid records that it was used.
  • Buildings and vegetation: both DEMs are surface-ish models whose treatment of canopy and roofs is not separated here.

Sources.

  • dataset: Copernicus DEM COP-DEM-GLO-30-F. Free of charge with reproduction, distribution, adaptation and modification rights, worldwide and unlimited in time. Resampling is a modification, so the attribution recorded is the modified-data wording: produced using Copernicus WorldDEM-30 © DLR e.V. 2010-2014 and © Airbus Defence and Space GmbH 2014-2018 provided under COPERNICUS by the European Union and ESA; all rights reserved. A redistributor must also add: The organisations in charge of the Copernicus programme by law or by delegation do not incur any liability for any use of the Copernicus WorldDEM-30. WorldDEM-10 is excluded. The distribution is a GeoTIFF this crate does not parse; the supported path is an ASCII-grid export.

world/terrain/los-profile¶

The terrain elevation profile along a link, and the knife edges it puts in the way: the world-side query that the P.526 terrain diffraction model consumes.

version 1.0.0; tiers high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
sample_spacing_m m 30.0 dataset: SRTMGL1 (1 arc-second) and Copernicus COP-DEM-GLO-30 post spacing. Sampling at the source's own post spacing is the only interval that neither invents detail nor discards it.
max_samples - 1024 todo-calibrate: los-profile max_samples (calibration pending)
obstruction_threshold_m m 0.0 code: local-maximum test of the profile, as ITU-R P.526-14 Eq. 26/31/33 requires it
min_profile_samples - 3 code: local-maximum test of the profile, as ITU-R P.526-14 Eq. 26/31/33 requires it

Equations.

  • line height: line(f) = a_z + (b_z − a_z)·f, f = s/total in the horizontal plane
  • clearance: h(s) = ground(s) − line(s); h > 0 obstructs
  • knife edge: an interior sample i with h_i > threshold, h_i > h_{i−1} and h_i >= h_{i+1}, contributing (d1, d2, h) = (s_i, total − s_i, h_i) These are the arguments of ν = h·sqrt(2(d1 + d2)/(λ·d1·d2)); the square root and J(ν) belong to the propagation model.

Assumptions.

  • The line between the two antennas is straight: no atmospheric refraction and no earth curvature. Over the 1 km at which a 5.9 GHz V2X link is already below sensitivity, the earth's bulge is about 2 cm, which is under the height quantum.
  • The ground between two samples is the linear interpolation the terrain grid gives, so a feature narrower than the sampling interval is invisible — as it is in the source data, whose posts are the same width apart.
  • Endpoint z values are absolute world heights, antenna height included. It is the caller's job to add the antenna height to the ground, which is what Site::antenna_position does.

Limitations.

  • Buildings, vehicles and foliage are not in the profile: the other obstacle models own those, and adding them here would double-count them.
  • The obstructed length is a trapezoidal estimate at the profile's resolution, not an exact intersection of the line with the surface.

Ignores.

  • Diffraction itself: this query returns geometry, and obstacle/terrain/knife-edge-p526 turns it into decibels.

Sources.

  • dataset: SRTMGL1 (1 arc-second) and Copernicus COP-DEM-GLO-30 post spacing. Sampling at the source's own post spacing is the only interval that neither invents detail nor discards it.

world/terrain/srtm-30¶

Reads a raster digital elevation model and resamples it bilinearly onto the world's own metre grid, so that the world's ground height comes from measured terrain instead of the z = 0 plane.

version 1.0.0; tiers medium, high; hosting in-process; draws no random numbers.

Parameter Unit Default Range Source
cell_m m 30.0 code: (both DEMs are 30 m posts, so 30 m is the resolution the data carries)
interpolation - bilinear code: ("resampled onto the world grid with bilinear interpolation")
margin_m m 30.0 todo-calibrate: world/terrain/srtm-30 margin_m (calibration pending)
void_search_cells - 8 todo-calibrate: world/terrain/srtm-30 void_search_cells (calibration pending)
void_default_m m 0.0 todo-calibrate: world/terrain/srtm-30 void_default_m (calibration pending)
min_plausible_elevation_m m -500.0 todo-calibrate: world/terrain/srtm-30 min_plausible_elevation_m (calibration pending)
max_plausible_elevation_m m 9000.0 todo-calibrate: world/terrain/srtm-30 max_plausible_elevation_m (calibration pending)

Equations.

  • bilinear resampling: h(x, y) = Σ_k h_k · w_k over the four surrounding source samples, w = (1−tx)(1−ty), tx(1−ty), (1−tx)ty, tx·ty
  • void repair: h = Σ_present h_k·w_k / Σ_present w_k, falling back to the nearest non-void sample within void_search_cells
  • srtm tile georeferencing: sample (ix, iy) at (lon0 + ix/(n−1), lat0 + iy/(n−1)) degrees, (lat0, lon0) from the file name, n from the byte count

Assumptions.

  • The raster holds NASA SRTMGL1 .hgt tiles.
  • Heights are metres above the DEM's own datum, and that datum is taken to be the world's z = 0. Neither DEM's geoid is converted to an ellipsoidal height, and the offset between them (tens of metres in places) is not modelled.
  • A void is missing data, not sea level: it is interpolated or filled from a neighbour, never read as 0 m, unless the search finds nothing at all.

Limitations.

  • The GeoTIFF and DT2 distributions are not parsed; a GeoTIFF DEM must be converted to an ASCII grid first (gdal_translate -of AAIGrid).
  • The vertical datum is not converted, so an absolute height is only as good as the source's datum matching the world's.
  • A geodetic raster is sampled through the world's local tangent plane, so the resampling error grows with distance from the world origin exactly as the projection's does.

Ignores.

  • Slope-aware or bicubic resampling: specifies bilinear and the grid records that it was used.
  • Buildings and vegetation: both DEMs are surface-ish models whose treatment of canopy and roofs is not separated here.

Sources.

  • dataset: NASA Earthdata SRTMGL1 (1 arc-second). Openly shared without restriction under EOSDIS data-use guidance; citation requested, not legally required. The vertical accuracy is UNVERIFIED: the Earthdata page defers to Rodriguez et al. 2006, which was not retrieved.
